      1. **CRITICAL — `open_position_atomic`'s RPC call site in `index.ts` was never updated to pass `p_strategy_profile`**, a REQUIRED (no SQL default) parameter added to this RPC's signature by the PRIOR session's `20261001180000_v4_exits_and_funding.sql` migration. Fixed: `p_funding_cost: trade.fundingCost` added to all three call sites.

Fixed: the flatMap now also emits for `intradayLsOpportunityContext`/`intradayLsCandidate`, carrying `armId`/`direction`/`bias`. `buildEntryQuestionsForAsset` (entry-question.ts) now takes the whole opportunity object instead of a bare asset symbol, conditionally naming direction/arm/bias in the instructions text — **conditionally**, so an Aggressive-shaped opportunity (no armId/direction/bias) produces byte-identical text to before; a dedicated regression test pins this exactly, matching every previous phase's discipline for shared-code widening. Advisory for `intraday_ls` (`entry_gate_mode='advisory'` — SKIP is recorded, never suppresses the trade); unchanged BLOCKING for Aggressive's own existing use of the identical question pair (`entry_gate_mode='blocking'`).
    - **5.1c (adversarial critique) — entirely new.** A genuinely different question, not a third vote: *"Assume this candidate is going to fail… what is the strongest evidence that the trade is vulnerable?"* New module `model/jev/adversarial-question.ts` — two Choice questions, `failure_risk` (LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH) and `failure_mode` (a fixed six-value taxonomy: `MOMENTUM_EXHAUSTION`/`COUNTER_TREND_PRESSURE`/`WEAK_VOLUME_CONFIRMATION`/`RANGE_COMPRESSION`/`STRUCTURE_BREAK`/`NONE`). Verified before building: Jev's answer schema is a closed `noul | choice | score` union (`schema.ts`) — **no free-text answer type exists**, confirmed against the vendor skill docs too — so a Choice-over-taxonomy was the only workable shape, and it happens to satisfy `CLAUDE.md`'s pre-existing "never model-authored prose" rule for free. `NONE`'s own criteria text states precisely what it means ("no material, specific vulnerability visible — NOT unsure, NOT none-of-the-above-fit") per explicit user instruction, since it's the easiest option to misread. Advisory only, for every profile that asks it today (intraday_ls only).
    - **No composite score** — deliberately did not build a weighted veto/entry/adversarial blend; that would be invented policy ahead of evidence. Raw per-layer outputs persist; only §6's (not-yet-built) Executed population gets to say whether any of them predicts realized R.
    - **Full probability distributions persisted structurally, not just winning values — a user-flagged required change, not optional.** New `jsonb` columns (`entry_quality_distribution`, `expected_move_distribution`, `failure_risk_distribution`, `failure_mode_distribution`) store each Choice/Score answer's complete `probabilities` map verbatim. Rationale: §6 needs to test whether a *specific* option (e.g. `failure_mode = MOMENTUM_EXHAUSTION`) predicts realized R, which the thresholded/winning value alone can't answer, and `output_payload`'s free-form JSON shape is explicitly not a stable contract (confirmed: zero `src/` code reads it today). Required widening `EntryOutcome` (previously **discarded** `entryQuality.probabilities` and all of `expectedMove`'s confidence/legend/probabilities entirely) and adding the equivalent `AdversarialOutcome` type.
    - **Three independent prompt-version columns** (`veto_prompt_version`/`entry_prompt_version`/`adversarial_prompt_version`), not one composite string — found that `promptVersionForRow` is a single local assigned exactly once per row, so one batched call answering all three question families could previously only ever record ONE version string. A null column means "this layer did not run for this row," never "ran under an earlier version."
    - **Computed SL/TP prices now persist unconditionally, closing a real gap in the labeler's foundation.** `gateResult.computedStopLossPrice`/`computedTakeProfitPrice` are `null` on every gate-rejection path (`gate.ts`) — so a vetoed or risk-rejected V4 candidate previously persisted no levels at all, and §6's (future) labeler cannot simulate a trade it has no levels for. Fixed: Pass 1 now also computes `intradayLsComputedPrices` via `computeStopLossTakeProfitPrices` (the EXISTING shared helper, reused not reimplemented) at candidate-build time, and the insert falls back to it whenever the gate itself produced nothing.
    - **`decision_type` discriminator added** (`'candidate' | 'management'`, nullable) — derived purely from position state (`openPosition ? 'management' : 'candidate'`), for every profile, zero behavior change. **Deliberately does NOT yet support two rows for one (run_id, asset) this pass** — see below.
    - **Closed-bar look-ahead concern, investigated and found NOT a live bug — but the evidence backing the "not a bug" claim was itself weaker than assumed, and is now actually verified.** User raised: does V4's bias layer risk reading an in-progress (unclosed) bar? Traced definitively: `evaluateBias` applies zero filtering and structurally cannot (receives bare `number[]`, no timestamps); `candles` (4h) and `ohlc30m` (30m) are both deliberately UNFILTERED at the provider boundary (`coingecko.test.ts` actively asserts this), resting entirely on an assumption that `/ohlc`'s timestamps are close-time and the in-progress candle is withheld server-side. The repo's own recorded evidence for that assumption (180 candles, uniform 4.0h gaps) does NOT actually distinguish a close-stamped completed bar from an open-stamped in-progress one — both produce uniform gaps. **Settled with a live reconciliation, not just re-asserted**: compared the 30m bar stamped 14:30 UTC (2026-10-02) against this project's own stored 5m spot in `market_bars` — its `open`=86551 matches stored spot at 14:00, its `close`=86112 matches stored spot at 14:30, proving the bar covers 14:00→14:30 and that timestamps genuinely are close times. **No look-ahead exists today.** Added a permanent, cheap runtime guard anyway (`strategy/closed-bars.ts`'s new `reconcileClosedBar`): reconciles the newest 4h/30m candle's close against the 5m spot point at the identical timestamp, logging (never filtering, never fail-closed — filtering would now be the WRONG fix) if they diverge beyond a provisional 0.5% tolerance. Test fixtures use the real verified numbers, including an adversarial "what if timestamps meant open-time instead" case that reproduces the actual ~0.506% real-world gap and correctly trips the check.
    - **Data-staleness semantics, investigated and found the stated concern NOT real, but worth the explicit writeup requested.** User asked whether `maxDataStalenessMinutes: 10` could fail V4 closed on perfectly valid 30-minute-old bars. Traced: it gates exactly one thing — the spot quote's `dataAsOf` (`build-context.ts`) — and `checkStrategyDataSufficiency` has no clock at all, every predicate is a bar-count check. Bar recency is never age-checked anywhere in the codebase; `WINDOW_SCAN_BARS=4` already deliberately tolerates an edge up to ~2h old. The real residual risk is the inverse of what was asked: a 10-minute tolerance on a *provider-reported* field means a transient CoinGecko `last_updated` hiccup hard-skips the whole cycle — worth watching in Stage C, not changed in this pass.
    - **Review item deliberately NOT done this pass, and explicitly flagged as such rather than silently dropped: "candidate vs management" dual-row emission (`asset_occupied` shadows).** User correctly identified that one `agent_decisions` row currently has to mean two different things at once for an occupied asset ("manage the open position" AND "an opportunity was detected and not taken"), corrupting §6's future `decision_outcomes` keying. Fully designed (two `CandidateSource` entries per occupied asset feeding the SAME batched call via the existing, untouched `collectModelCandidates`; the existing gate's own `position already open; CLOSE first` rejection needs no new gate logic; `agent_decisions_run_asset_unique` needs widening to `(run_id, asset, coalesce(decision_type,'candidate'))`; Pass 1's detection gate needs to drop its `openPosition === null` condition) but NOT implemented — judged the single highest-risk, most structurally invasive piece of 5-IMPL (restructuring Pass 2's one-row-per-asset loop into one-or-two), and deliberately sequenced as its own separately-scoped, separately-tested follow-up unit rather than risked alongside everything else in one pass, matching this project's own "one implementation unit at a time, verify between" discipline. Un-gating Pass 1 detection from FLAT-only is bundled into the SAME follow-up, since it's the other half of the same change.

Validated offline with `pglast` (19 statements parsed clean) before `supabase db push` — applied live and confirmed via direct column query (18/18 present).

Full gate: **728/728 tests**, typecheck/lint/build clean.

Retrofitting that harness would be a materially different, much larger undertaking than this pass's own scope.

  - **Occupied-asset shadow candidates, DONE, 2026-10-03 — the "Candidate vs management" item deferred at line 55 above.** With live trades now open on both assets, this item stopped being hypothetical: every subsequent cycle was silently losing the `asset_occupied` counterfactual §6 needs, permanently, the moment either position's own asset also had a six-arm opportunity detected on it. Implemented exactly as designed at deferral time, with one addition found necessary during implementation:
    - Pass 1's `if (settings.strategyProfile === 'intraday_ls' && openPosition === null)` guard dropped to `if (settings.strategyProfile === 'intraday_ls')` — detection (bias, six arms, cost gate, the consumed-opportunity lifecycle) now runs identically regardless of position state, per plan §3.4 ("detection runs regardless of position state — it is free, same data").
    - **Found and fixed while un-gating Pass 1, not anticipated at deferral time**: un-gating detection alone would have let an occupied asset's real `intradayLsCandidate` leak into THREE places that were never designed to see it occupied — the `collectModelCandidates` call site's candidate substitution (would have silently dropped the open position out of `managementCandidates` entirely, since that branch requires `candidate.action === 'HOLD'` — meaning the real position would go completely unmanaged, no HOLD/ADD/REDUCE/CLOSE/MODIFY_PROTECTION, whenever a shadow opportunity happened to be detected on it, a much worse regression than anything this pass set out to fix), the `entryOpportunities` flatMap (would have spent a real Jev entry/adversarial call on a candidate already known to be gate-rejected), and the main row's own `armIdForRow`/`biasForRow`/`opportunityBarTsForRow` (would have duplicated the opportunity data onto BOTH the management row and the new shadow row, reintroducing exactly the one-row-two-meanings ambiguity this whole change exists to remove). All three now explicitly `!openPosition`-gated.
    - The occupied asset's existing management row is completely unchanged. A SEPARATE `'candidate'` row is now also inserted when `openPosition && r.intradayLsCandidate` — evaluated through the EXISTING `evaluateRiskGate` (reusing the SAME `gateContext` already built for the asset this cycle) with no new gate logic, since `evaluateOpen`'s own first check (`currentState !== 'FLAT'`) already rejects with `'position already open; CLOSE first'`. Deliberately asks Jev nothing (occupation is a structural fact no news/entry/adversarial question could change) — `model_version='not-called'`, all three §5.1 layers null. Non-fatal on insert failure (`console.error`, never throws) — observational telemetry, same "side write, not a trading action" precedent as the `market_bars` upsert; must never block the real management decision's own dispatch.
    - **Migration** `20261003150000_occupied_asset_shadow_candidates.sql` — dropped `agent_decisions_run_asset_unique` (`unique(run_id, asset)`, live since the table's original creation), replaced with a unique INDEX over `(run_id, asset, coalesce(decision_type, 'candidate'))` — an expression index, since a plain table UNIQUE constraint can't reference an expression. Every pre-2026-10-03 row has `decision_type` NULL, so `coalesce(...)` resolves to `'candidate'` for all of them, preserving the OLD at-most-one-row guarantee byte-for-byte across all historical data; going forward this permits exactly one `'candidate'` + one `'management'` row per asset per run, nothing looser. Validated offline with `pglast`.

Verification is the existing 728/728 suite passing unchanged (confirming zero regression to any of the pure modules this touches — `gate.ts`, `collect-candidates.ts`, `lifecycle.ts` — none of which were modified) plus typecheck/lint/build, plus a live cycle once deployed (both BTC and ETH are currently open under `intraday_ls`, so the very next hourly cycle that detects anything on either asset exercises this path for real).

  - **§6B — Jev continuous-evaluation architecture, DESIGN PROPOSAL approved, 2026-10-03.** User asked for a deep architecture review of how §6 should evolve into a broader LLMOps-style evaluation/learning loop for the Jev layer, explicitly requesting critique over agreement. Measured against the live DB rather than estimated: V4 has run 17 cycles → 2 candidates → 1 distinct arm ever fired; project lifetime is 4 positions total; BTC/ETH daily-return correlation is 0.882 (a paired observation ≈ 1.06 independent samples). That arithmetic drives the whole design: detecting a 0.1R prompt-expectancy improvement needs ~1,570 observations/arm (~8+ years at the observed rate), and selecting the best of 5 challenger prompts on ~100 cases inflates the winner by ~0.116R of pure noise alone — already exceeding the target effect. A second independent review pass caught two load-bearing code-level errors in the first draft: (1) the plan's own §6.1 shadow-simulation design specified 5m-close replay for stop/target breach detection, but `market_bars`' 5m series is close-only (live-verified: 0 of 1600 rows carry high/low) while the 30m series is true OHLC (266/266 do) — so the original design couldn't actually detect a stop breach at all; (2) §6.2's "veto value: news_veto vs executed" row compares two different measurement processes (10-minute live poll vs bar replay) and attributes the difference to the veto, which is unsound. Both are corrected in §6B and the obsolete §6 paragraphs are marked superseded inline (not left as a second, contradicting spec). Key design decisions: judge roles mostly rejected (Jev-judging-Jev on its own prediction is circular — same-model label leakage, not independent validation; replaced with deterministic signatures and invariance probes); the veto can be empirically tightened but never loosened on simulated data (censoring — blocked trades have no outcome, and simulation is optimistic exactly on the tail events a veto exists for); advisory layers should be scored against a model-free price-path statistic computed identically for every candidate (not simulated trade P&L), which turns an 8-year P&L comparison into a ~7-week paired comparison; a regression case requires a correct answer derivable from inputs alone — a losing trade is never one. User's own second-pass review (after reading the full proposal) caught two further defects before approval: an earlier draft folded model+prompt hashes INTO the case identity, which would have given champion and challenger different case ids for identical input and broken the whole paired-comparison design (fixed: `case_id = hash(input)`, `evaluation_id = hash(case_id, model, prompt_hashes, inference_config)` — one case, many evaluations); and the predicted Jev failure-mode taxonomy and a proposed "realized failure signature" were treated as one categorical variable for a confusion matrix, which is a category error since `WEAK_VOLUME_CONFIRMATION` is a setup property with no realized-path counterpart (fixed: analyzed as `P(realized class | predicted vulnerability)`, two separate taxonomies, not a single accuracy metric). Written to the plan file as §6B (replaces nothing — §6 is rewritten in place so there is exactly one current spec, not two overlapping ones). **Design only — nothing built yet.** Sequencing agreed: #1 prompt-artifact hashing → #2 stable per-row Jev-request record → #3 case_id/evaluation_id → Tier 2 signal diagnostics → §6 outcome evaluation → conditional challenger machinery. `trading-strategy-v1.md` §22's binding scope-change prerequisite (`CLAUDE.md`/`ai-workflow-rules.md:17`/`project-overview.md:146` must be updated before replay/backtest work) applies starting at Tier 1, not to this Tier-0 work.
  - **§6B P0 item 1 — prompt-artifact content hashing, DONE, 2026-10-03.** The first, narrowest unit from the sequencing above: bind each hand-maintained `*_QUESTION_VERSION` string to a SHA-256 hash of the actual rendered prompt text it describes, so a wording change without a version bump is a test failure rather than a silent drift caught later by chance (as `MANAGEMENT_QUESTION_VERSION`'s own missed bump, and `strategy_version`'s drift to a never-shipped `v3.1`, both already were).
    - New `model/jev/prompt-hash.ts` — `hashPromptContent(parts: string[])`, SHA-256 over canonical JSON, hex-encoded. Deliberately real cryptographic hashing rather than a toy checksum, since the same primitive is designed to be reused for `evaluation_id = hash(case_id, model_id, prompt_hashes, inference_config)` in the later case-capture work (§6B), where collision resistance is the actual property being relied on.
    - One `*PromptFingerprint()` function added to each of the four prompt modules (`question.ts`, `management-question.ts`, `entry-question.ts`, `adversarial-question.ts`), each rendering its builder function(s) against a small set of fixed, representative inputs chosen to cover every branch whose WORDING differs (e.g. long vs short for the veto; legal-tighten vs not, disableAdd true/false, aggressive-context present/absent for management — 4 variants, not a full combinatorial cross, chosen so every distinct text string appears in at least one) and hashing the canonical JSON of the rendered output.
    - New `prompt-provenance.test.ts` pins each fingerprint against its CURRENT version string in a `KNOWN_GOOD: Record<version, hash>` map — keyed by version, not recomputed, which is what makes both drift directions fail: a wording change with no version bump leaves the lookup key unchanged but the fresh hash stale (fails); a wording change WITH a version bump produces a lookup key with no pinned entry at all (also fails, forcing a human to deliberately record the new hash rather than having it silently accepted).
    - **Live-demonstrated, not just argued**: made a one-word wording change to the veto's real `EXCLUSION_CRITERIA` text (no version bump), ran the suite — the provenance test failed with a clear diff showing old vs new hash, while the other three modules' tests stayed green (confirming no cross-module contamination). Reverted via `git diff` confirming a byte-exact match to the original, re-ran — green again. This is the literal red→green proof requested, not a simulated one.
    - 10 new tests (5 `prompt-hash.test.ts` — determinism, one-character sensitivity, order-sensitivity, digest format, empty-input well-definedness; 5 `prompt-provenance.test.ts` — one per module's pin plus a key-collision guard). Full gate: **738/738 tests**, typecheck/lint/build clean.
    - Scope discipline: this unit does NOT touch `output_payload`, does not build a case/evaluation record, and does not change the live decision path at all — purely additive, zero behavior change to any trading decision. #2 (stable per-row Jev-request record) and #3 (case_id/evaluation_id) are next, in that order, per the agreed sequencing.

One canonical scope statement added as a new "Backtest/Replay Scope" subsection in `project-overview.md`, with `CLAUDE.md` and `ai-workflow-rules.md` each striking their bare "backtesting" exclusion and pointing back to it rather than each carrying independent wording — deliberately, to avoid recreating the exact hand-maintained-copy-drift problem §6B P0 item 1 just fixed, one scope statement over. **Narrowly scoped**: in-scope is a deterministic replay/backtest harness over `market_bars`, replaying only the deterministic strategy layers (regime, six-arm detection, gate, sizing, exits) via the SAME pure functions live code already uses, bound by `trading-strategy-v1.md` §22–23's existing look-ahead controls and anti-overfitting protocol. Explicitly still out and NOT reauthorized: Jev/LLM historical replay as performance evidence (training-data contamination can't be ruled out — legitimate only for software/robustness diagnostics per §6B §4), any live-parameter auto-tuning from backtest results, and everything else still on the Out of Scope list (real money, exchange execution, multi-agent, RAG, streaming feeds, etc.). `trading-strategy-v1.md` §22's own `[OPEN]` marker updated to `[RESOLVED 2026-10-03]`. Pure documentation change — no code touched, nothing to test/build.
  - **§6B P0 item 2 — stable per-row Jev-request record, DONE, 2026-10-03.** Closes the per-asset/stability gap named in §6B §5: the full Jev request (model + state + verbatim question text) was already being persisted in `output_payload.request`, but that column is explicitly declared NOT a stable contract (the P0-item-1-adjacent migration's own header: "nothing in src/ reads it... should not be relied on"), is `null` for an entire class of rows (no-call, call-failed, every occupied-asset shadow row), and — the defect that actually mattered — is **batch-wide, not per-asset**: every row from a cycle carried every asset's news/position/opportunity and every question id the whole cycle asked, not just that row's own slice.

Asset-filtering uses the explicit list of all ten already-exported question-id-builder functions (`vetoQuestionId`, `managementActionQuestionId`, etc.) — never a string-prefix/suffix pattern match, which would be fragile against the three different id conventions these modules use (`veto_btc` vs `btc_action` vs `btc_entry_quality`).
    - **A deliberate, flagged precedent departure**: the projection's envelope (schemaVersion/asset/model fields/portfolio shape) is validated by a Zod schema at construction time — the FIRST Zod-validated persisted jsonb shape originating in this backend (confirmed via audit: `output_payload`/`input_payload` are both explicitly `z.unknown()` in `src/shared/decisions/types.ts`, by prior deliberate design — "over-specifying this now would presume methodology details... explicitly deferred"). Justified explicitly: the whole point of this column is to BE the stable contract `output_payload` explicitly isn't, so validating it is the correct departure, not an oversight. The deep Jev snapshot fields (`news`/`position`/`opportunity`/`questions`) are deliberately NOT re-declared as a second Zod schema — each already has one precise TypeScript source of truth, and duplicating them would risk exactly the two-copies-diverge drift P0 item 1 exists to eliminate, one column over; Zod validates only the envelope a future reader actually needs a runtime guarantee about.

Persisted as `null` on the occupied-asset shadow row unconditionally (that row never calls Jev at all, by design).

  - **§6B P0 item 3 — case_id / evaluation_id, DONE, 2026-10-03.** The last of the three P0 units (prompt-artifact hashing → stable request projection → this). Implements the plan's own review correction verbatim: *"A case is the input. An evaluation is one model's answer to it."* An earlier draft of the plan folded model+prompt into the case's own identity, which would have given a champion and challenger evaluating the IDENTICAL market situation two DIFFERENT case ids — destroying the pairing §6B §9's whole comparison design depends on.
    - New `model/jev/case-identity.ts`: `caseInputFromRequestProjection(projection)` strips `JevRequestProjection` down to ONLY the market-situation fields (asset/evaluatedAt/news/position/opportunity/portfolio) — explicitly excluding `modelRequested`/`modelResolved`/`questions`, which are evaluation-specific. `computeCaseId(caseInput) = hash(JSON.stringify(caseInput))`. `computeEvaluationId(caseId, projection, inferenceConfig?)` additionally folds in the resolved model id and, for each question family the row actually asked (veto/management/entry/adversarial — determined by id membership in `projection.questions`, not by threading a new per-branch flag through `index.ts`, since management has no independent prompt-version column at all today), that family's CURRENT module-level prompt fingerprint — **directly reusing P0 item 1's four `*PromptFingerprint()` functions**, exactly as that item's own comment anticipated ("designed to be reused... where collision resistance is the actual property being relied on"). `inferenceConfig` defaults to `{}`, documented as a deliberate placeholder — this system has no caller-supplied sampling/determinism parameter today.
    - Wired into `index.ts`: computed immediately after `jevRequestProjectionForRow` succeeds, inside the SAME try/catch (case/evaluation cannot exist without a valid projection, so one failure correctly nulls all three together). Same non-fatal discipline as #2 and the occupied-asset shadow row.
    - **Migration** `20261003190000_jev_case_evaluation_identity.sql` — two nullable `text` columns, `jev_case_id`/`jev_evaluation_id`, plus a partial (non-null-only) index on `jev_case_id` — the exact lookup pattern a future case-capture query needs ("every evaluation of this case"), since MULTIPLE rows legitimately sharing one case_id (champion + challenger) is the whole point, not an edge case to forbid. `jev_evaluation_id` deliberately carries no uniqueness constraint — a live trading cycle must never fail an insert over a telemetry coincidence. Validated offline with `pglast`.
    - **12 new tests** (`case-identity.test.ts`), centered on the one property that actually matters: **identical market situation → identical case_id regardless of which model/questions were asked** (the decisive test — constructs a "champion" and "challenger" projection differing in model AND questions entirely, asserts equal case_id), plus the champion/challenger worked example itself (same case_id, different evaluation_id when `inferenceConfig` differs), different-asset/different-news/different-timestamp all producing different case_ids, different resolved-model-id and different-question-family-asked both producing different evaluation_ids, insertion-order-independence of the prompt-hash component (object key order must not matter, only family presence), and the fallback to `modelRequested` when `modelResolved` is null. Full gate: **760/760 tests**, typecheck/lint/build clean.
    - **This closes all three §6B P0 units.** Every `agent_decisions` row with a genuine Jev call now carries: a stable per-asset request projection, a case_id identifying the market situation independent of model/prompt, and an evaluation_id identifying this specific model+prompt+config's answer to it — the complete substrate plan §6B §16 names as Tier 0/1 ("capture"). Next per the agreed sequencing: Tier 2 signal diagnostics (the zero-outcome-needed repeatability/degeneracy/discrimination/feature-sensitivity suite) — gated on nothing further; §6 outcome evaluation and conditional challenger machinery remain downstream of that.
    - **DEPLOYED, 2026-10-03.** Migration applied live and confirmed (`jev_case_id`/`jev_evaluation_id` both present, `text` type). `agent-cycle` redeployed.
  - **§6B Tier 2 — signal diagnostics, RUN LIVE, 2026-10-03.** The gate between "capture" (P0 items 1-3, closed above) and any further investment in a Jev layer: does any layer carry exploitable information at all, tested with zero trade outcomes. New manual harness `model/jev/jev-tier2-diagnostics.live-check.ts` (same `*.live-check.ts` convention as the existing veto-wording smoke test — not part of `deno test`, real API calls, ~35 per run), running the plan's own pre-registered battery in its own specified order (repeatability first, since every other threshold is "beyond the noise floor repeatability measures," never an arbitrary number): repeatability (20 identical calls on one frozen fixture) → degeneracy + discrimination + feature-sensitivity (an 8-fixture direction×quality×volume matrix, analyzed three ways from one set of calls) → the four invariance probes (cross-asset isolation, direction flip, news ablation, exclusion-list injection). Every pass/fail criterion was written into the script BEFORE it was run and never adjusted afterward.
    - **Result: 8 of 9 checks PASS**, full raw data in `context/diagnostics/jev-tier2-signal-test-2026-10-03.json`.
    - **Repeatability — reassuringly tight.** Noise floor on the frozen fixture: `noul` sd=0.002, `entry_quality` ENTER-probability sd=0.020, `expected_move` score sd=0.022, `failure_risk` HIGH-probability sd=0.012. Far tighter than the Aggressive-era 34/34-SKIP-at-confidence-0.42–0.47 anecdote implied — this particular (textbook-strong) fixture consistently resolved ENTER at ~0.70, not a coin flip.
    - **Degeneracy: PASS.** The 8-fixture matrix produced a 0.33 spread in ENTER-probability — 16x the noise floor. `entry_quality` is clearly responsive to fixture content, not a near-constant predictor.
    - **Discrimination: PASS, both directions.** `P(ENTER|strong) > P(ENTER|weak)` by 0.08 (long) and 0.13 (short), both well past the 3x-noise-floor significance bar.
    - **Feature sensitivity (volume): PASS for long, FAIL for short — a genuine, narrow, actionable finding, not a blanket failure.** Confirmed-vs-weak volume moved `entry_quality` by 0.17 for long candidates (clearly reading the feature) but only 0.04 for short — below the significance threshold. Notably, the SEPARATE `failure_mode` layer correctly flagged `WEAK_VOLUME_CONFIRMATION` on every weak-volume fixture in BOTH directions — so the volume signal is present somewhere in the model's reasoning for shorts, it just isn't moving `entry_quality`'s own probability as strongly as it does for longs. Worth a closer look if short volume-sensitivity specifically becomes decision-relevant later; not severe enough to redesign or remove the layer.
    - **Cross-asset isolation: PASS — the single highest-stakes untested assumption in the system, confirmed.** `question.ts` puts both assets' news in one shared `state` object and relies entirely on the provider's documented "questions evaluated in parallel and in isolation" property — never tested until now. ETH's `noul` moved only 0.01 (0.03→0.04) when BTC carried catastrophic hack news in the SAME request; well inside the noise floor. No leakage detected.
    - **Direction flip: PASS, by a wide margin.** Identical negative news scored `noul=0.68` as a long candidate (correctly a high threat) vs `noul=0.14` as a short candidate (correctly a much lower one, since negative news favors a short) — confirms the §5.1a direction-aware veto fix is doing its job, not just compiling.
    - **News ablation: PASS.** Material news 0.68 vs empty news 0.04 — the veto responds to actual evidence, not to the asset's name alone.
    - **Exclusion-list injection: PASS.** Pure price commentary scored 0.03, indistinguishable from the empty-news baseline (0.04) — the documented exclusion criteria are being honored, not just stated.
    - **Important caveat, stated plainly rather than overclaiming the result:** these are SYNTHETIC fixtures, deliberately constructed to be clearly strong or clearly weak. Per plan §6B's own "a synthetic pass is necessary but not sufficient" framing — this proves the entry_quality/failure_risk layers CAN discriminate when given a clean, well-separated scenario; it does NOT yet prove they discriminate USEFULLY on the actual marginal candidates V4's real detector produces, which (per the only two live observations so far) were both near-50/50. The gap between "clean synthetic signal" and "ambiguous real-world signal" is exactly what §6 (outcome evaluation, not yet built) exists to measure, now that this layer has cleared the much cheaper bar of "not obviously degenerate."
    - Full gate unaffected: **760/760 tests** still pass unchanged (the new script matches the existing `*.live-check.ts` convention and is correctly excluded from `deno test`'s glob), lint clean.

The flat DB column is still updated in migrations purely for UI-display accuracy (Settings/Home screens read it directly) — it no longer drives any backend logic.

The consumed-opportunity lifecycle (`opportunity_bar_ts`) is bar-timestamp-based, not cycle-count-based, so it already handles more cycles re-observing the same closed 30-minute bar correctly — a real signal still emits exactly once per genuine bar close, regardless of cadence.

Wired into `index.ts`'s Pass-1 loop (the one that builds `passOneResults`, which determines Pass 2's execution order — the OTHER `settings.assets` loop, populating `openPositionsByAsset`, is order-independent and was left alone). A no-op at the 1.2% stop floor (all 4 fit); matters once wider ATR-driven stops or a cash constraint make a cap actually bind. 6 new tests (`idempotency.test.ts`) — same-bucket stability, one-per-bucket advancement, full-cycle wraparound, rotation-not-reorder, the live 2-asset case, single/empty-universe no-ops.

User-supplied tokens (`#070A09` bg, `#2E1B45`@5% glass, `#292F36` border, `#CAD3D9` text, `#7A838C` muted, `#E0C709` accent, Manrope) used verbatim; `--w-pos`/`--w-neg` added (desaturated, the one token pair not in the user's list) under the extension's own still-applicable "color is semantic, never decorative" rule.
  - **The highest-risk technical item: Tailwind v4 has no config file, auto-detects source files project-wide — unguarded, the web app's class usage would leak into the extension's OWN CSS bundle.** Fixed with reciprocal `@source` scoping: the extension's `theme.css` gets one new line, `@source not "../web"`; the web stylesheet uses `@import 'tailwindcss' source(none)` + an explicit `@source`. **Found and fixed live, not just reasoned about**: the first version of the web stylesheet's `@source './**/*.{ts,tsx,html}'` path was relative to the CSS file's OWN directory (`src/web/styles/`), which has no `.tsx` files in it at all — so it silently generated an EMPTY `@layer utilities` (no error, just a dashboard with zero Tailwind classes applied, confirmed live in the browser as a completely unstyled page before the fix). Corrected to `@source '../**/*.{ts,tsx,html}'` (one level up, to `src/web/`) and reconfirmed working.
  - **Extension build independence — the literal instruction, verified three ways, not just asserted**: (1) `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/the existing 781-test suite all pass unchanged — nothing web-related is in their scope. (2) An initial hash-diff against a pre-session baseline showed a spurious CSS difference; traced to a confound (the baseline was captured BEFORE `npm install @fontsource-variable/manrope` changed `node_modules` state, unrelated to any source edit — confirmed by hash-diffing two clean builds from the identical, fully-reverted git-stashed tree, which still differed from the stale baseline but matched each other exactly). (3) The real, confound-free test: built the extension with `src/web/` present, then with it physically removed, SAME `node_modules` both times — **byte-for-byte identical `dist/` output** (file-by-file `shasum`, zero diff). Visually confirmed too: the extension popup screenshot after this change shows its original, unmodified violet theme and layout.
  - **A second real bug found and fixed live, not anticipated in the plan**: Overview's NAV query used `order('captured_at', {ascending:true}).limit(3000)` — but the Supabase-hosted PostgREST endpoint silently caps rows per request below what's asked for (returning fewer rows is not an error), so an ASCENDING query silently returned only the OLDEST rows once the real row count passed the cap, making "latest" (the last array element) stale by thousands of rows — confirmed live: Portfolio Value showed `$10,002` (a pre-capital-injection figure) while Available Cash correctly showed the post-injection `$13,996` on the same screen. Fixed by querying DESCENDING instead and reversing in JS: `data[0]` is always the true latest regardless of any cap, and if rows are capped the chart simply loses some early history rather than ever reporting a wrong "latest" value — a safer degradation than the ascending approach has.
  - **A third real bug found and fixed live**: the Decisions/Overview "Type" column labelled EVERY `decision_type='candidate'` row "Shadow candidate" — but that decision_type value is the ordinary path for any FLAT asset's routine per-cycle evaluation (including a plain HOLD with no detected opportunity), not specifically the occupied-asset shadow-candidate concept. Confirmed live: AVAX/SUI (both flat, no open position) were rendering as "Shadow candidate" for ordinary HOLD rows. Fixed with a new `isShadowCandidate(decisionType, riskStatus)` helper (`data/display.ts`) requiring BOTH `decision_type='candidate'` AND `risk_status='rejected'` — matching the actual mechanism (shadows are deterministically gate-rejected with `'position already open; CLOSE first'`) and matching the ALREADY-correct check the decision-detail page had used from the start. Reconfirmed live: a genuine shadow row (`ETH OPEN_LONG`, `pullback_long`, occupied) now correctly shows "Shadow candidate"/`REJECTED`, while flat-asset HOLDs show plain "Candidate".
  - **Five pages, built against real data constraints found while building, not assumed in advance**: Overview (status strip, 4 stats incl. **Total P&L from `realized_pnl_cum + unrealized_pnl`, not raw NAV** — NAV includes the 2026-10-03 capital injection and would chart a fake +100% spike; a NAV/P&L toggle exists, with contribution markers detected GENERICALLY from any ≥$500 single-tick cash jump, never a hardcoded date, so a future contribution marks itself for free; open positions; last-8 decisions). Decisions (filterable, `Arm/Bias`/`Type`/`Model`/`Execution` columns — **dropped the mockup's "Confidence" column entirely**, since every live decision carries a constant `confidence:1`, architecture.md's own documented invariant, and the column would show a fake unvarying 100%; replaced with Arm/Bias, which is real and was invisible everywhere before this). Decision detail (deterministic/blocking-model/advisory-model split, mirroring the extension's own stop-loss-vs-invalidation separation at a larger scale; full persisted probability distributions rendered per Jev layer, never just the winning label; live-verified against the exact documented BTC case — SKIP/MEDIUM/COUNTER_TREND_PRESSURE, executed anyway). Positions & Trades (full open/closed/ledger tables; `price_r`/`position_pnl_r` rendered as two separate fields, never one "R"; `high_water_tracked_from IS NULL` renders "not tracked — permanently ineligible", never a fabricated 0). AI Judgment (n-disclosure banners on every section — n=2 for the full advisory set at build time — no composite score computed anywhere, matching the project's own explicit refusal to invent that policy). Strategy & Settings (three-column risk table — profile/ceiling/effective-min — specifically because this project had a real multi-week bug where those three silently diverged; **live-confirmed the ASSET-4 sizing fix**: table reads 15.00% / 35.00% / 15.00%, not the pre-fix 30%/35%/30%).

- **CFG-1 — configurable strategy, observability, and the path to automatic improvement, IN PROGRESS, started 2026-10-06.** Triggered by a live root-cause investigation into "it never opens short, and we're down $84." Full plan: `/Users/nishchal/.claude/plans/pricing-and-model-selection-ethereal-fox.md`'s "CFG-1" section. **Six measured findings, not assumptions**: bias has been LONG on 100% of 770 BTC/ETH decisions since 2026-09-21 (daily regime never once read DOWN); the regime filter has **no measured directional edge and an inverted point estimate** (forward-1d: UP +0.349% vs DOWN +0.641%, p≈0.45, n=150); NEUTRAL bias occupies 31–50% of 4h points yet `fade_long`'s joint trigger has fired **zero times in 1,784 observations**; RSI's live distribution is asymmetric (≤30 occurs 0.4–0.5% of the time vs symmetric 30/70 thresholds assuming otherwise); 35 of 36 real entries are `pullback_long` — the system is structurally a leveraged dip-buyer, and the loss is that thesis failing a few times, not a malfunction. Four pre-existing code defects found in the same pass: `isOpportunityStillValid` (the signal-drift rule) is dead code with tests but no caller; the management-path ATR for `intraday_ls` silently reads the 4-hourly figure (the entry-path wiring doesn't cover management); the giveback ratchet samples MFE/MAE under V4 but can never exit (`plan.ts:194` gates the exit on `'aggressive'` specifically) — a direct contradiction of plan §4.2's own exit table; three sufficiency floors are duplicated literals instead of references to their own named constants.
  - **A fifth defect surfaced mid-implementation, during the Stage 0 volume investigation, not anticipated in the original plan**: `strategy/aggressive/features.ts`'s `volumeTrend()` — the breakout arm's `volumeTrendRatio ≥ 1.2` confirmation — is not measuring volume at all. Verified live against the raw CoinGecko API: `/market_chart`'s `total_volumes` (the only volume field this system has ever had access to; `/ohlc` never includes volume at any tier below paid Pro/Enterprise) is **rolling 24-hour trailing volume** resampled every 5 minutes, not incremental per-interval volume — confirmed directly (BTC reads ~$25–34 **billion** per 5-minute point, moving only single-digit percent between consecutive samples). A ratio of two overlapping windows of a slow-moving 24h aggregate is structurally pinned near 1.0 (live-measured across all four assets: 0.97–1.11), which is exactly consistent with the breakout arm almost never clearing its own confirmation gate. **Decision (explicit user call, not Claude's)**: this becomes Stage 1B correction #9 — the volume term is **removed**, not replaced. A bare `ret60mPct`-sign-only breakout ships deliberately undesigned; whatever eventually confirms it is a separate, Stage-3-validated research question, so a silent substitution never gets to hide behind a "this was broken, now it's fixed" label it didn't earn. True incremental volume is unavailable from this provider at this tier — documented as a permanent ceiling, not worked around.
  - **The reward loop explicitly demoted, as a rule, not just an ordering**: *"Replay discovers candidates and configurations. Live reward adaptation only ever adjusts risk among already-validated strategies."* `arm_weights` moved from the original plan's Stage 4 to **Stage 5**, after live shadow trials — at ~2.8 candidates/day across four correlated (ρ=0.622 measured) assets with one arm carrying 35 of 36 entries, building a live reward loop before offline validation exists would add machinery that cannot learn while looking like it does. `reward_loop_enabled` and `shortEnabled` both ship `false` and stay `false` through Stage 4.
  - **Plan went through two user review rounds before implementation began, both substantive**: round 1 caught a genuine self-contradiction ("behavior-neutral" claimed for a unit that also revives dead arms) — resolved by splitting into **Stage 1A** (pure refactor + provenance, must prove byte-identical to current V4 via two permanent named configs, `v4-compat` the frozen control and `v4.1-corrected` the deliberate divergence) and **Stage 1B** (the enumerated corrections, each requiring its own differential test asserting the divergence set has no silent extras). Round 2 added a **Stage 0 capture-first requirement** (ingested_at/batch provenance and arm decomposition are irreversible if delayed — everything else can be built later against data that already exists), replaced a flat `n≥30` learning threshold with a tiered evidence ladder (<50 descriptive-only / 50–100 conservative haircut / 100+ / 200+, each requiring effective-n, holdout confirmation, minimum effect size, and cross-regime stability — not just raw count), named a `label_kind` taxonomy so shadow-disabled-direction candidates never pool with executed trades, and corrected an overstated claim that Jev's advisory layers constitute López de Prado-style meta-labeling (they don't — no fitted secondary model exists; the accurate term is **advisory signal evaluation**).
  - **Stage 0 — capture, DEPLOYED and LIVE-VERIFIED.** `market_bars` gains `close_time` (explicitly-named alias of `open_time`, which has always silently held the bar's close instant), `ingested_at`, `batch_id`, `data_version`, threaded through from the caller's own `nowIso`/`run_id` rather than a bare clock read (migration `20261006120000`) — `ingested_at`/`batch_id`/`data_version` deliberately **not backfilled** on the 7,766 pre-existing rows (unknowable, not fabricated; same discipline as `decision_type`'s own precedent). `agent_decisions` gains `arm_family` (`breakout`/`pullback`/`fade`, derived from `arm_id`) and `direction`, **backfilled** on all 42 historical arm-bearing rows (a safe deterministic derivation of data already present, unlike the market_bars fields). New pure `armFamilyOf()` in `strategy/intraday-ls/detectors.ts`; both `agent_decisions` insert sites updated; `market-bars.ts`'s three `barsFrom*` functions now take a `BarProvenance` parameter. Full gate: **784 tests**, typecheck/lint clean. Deployed (`agent-cycle`); live-verified on a fresh manual cycle — every new `market_bars` field populated correctly under one shared `batch_id`, and `arm_family`/`direction` correctly null on the (arm-less, HOLD-only) decision rows from the same cycle.
  - **Stage 1A — config schema, presets, observability layer: DEPLOYED and LIVE-VERIFIED, 2026-10-06.** Deliberately **scoped down from the original Stage 1A description** mid-implementation, and said so rather than silently delivering less than promised: the full per-primitive config threading (editing `bias.ts`/`detectors.ts`/`protection.ts`'s own signatures to read every value from config) was judged too large and too risky to rush in one sitting, given it would require editing the exact ~15 test files this plan's own Stage 1A discipline says must pass **unedited**. What shipped instead is real, complete, and independently valuable — it is what actually answers the plan's own motivating question live, not a placeholder:
    - **Config schema + two permanent presets** (`src/shared/strategy/config-schema.ts`, `config-presets.ts`, `config-validate.ts`) — every field transcribed directly from its live source (cited inline, file+line), never re-derived. `v4-compat` golden-tested field-by-field against `STRATEGY_PROFILES.intraday_ls` (`profiles.ts`) and every detector/protection constant; a differential test asserts `v4.1-corrected` diverges from it on **exactly** `shortEnabled`, `breakoutMinVolumeTrendRatio` (→ null, a removal), `signalDriftRuleEnforced`, `givebackEnabledForIntradayLs` — no silent extras. Cross-field validation (`config-validate.ts`) catches the exact ASSET-4 saturation class of bug (`maxSingleTradePct × assetCount > maxTotalNotionalPct`) at write time, plus EMA ordering, fade RSI ordering, and RSI/ATR-period-vs-floor consistency.
    - **`strategy_configs` table + content hash**, reusing the P0-item-1 `hashPromptContent` primitive deliberately (not a second hashing scheme) — `agent-cycle/db/strategy-config.ts`'s `loadActiveIntradayLsConfig` re-validates AND re-hashes on every read, so a hand-edited row fails loudly. `ensureConfigSeeded` is the idempotent first-run seeder; computing hashes at insert time (not migration time) means there is no risk of the migration's own hash ever disagreeing with the runtime verifier.
    - **The actual observability payoff — `regime_state`/`eligible_arms`/`no_candidate_reason`/`strategy_config_hash` persisted on EVERY intraday_ls decision row**, not only when an arm fires (unlike the pre-existing `arm_id`/`bias`). Wired as pure *observation* around the existing, unmodified inlined detection block in `index.ts` — the detection LOGIC was not touched, only instrumented at each of its five existing branch points (data-insufficient / regime-null / no-arm-triggered / cost-gate / opportunity-consumed). **Live-verified immediately**: `select regime_state, count(*) from agent_decisions group by regime_state` now directly reproduces this plan's own F1 finding (bias has read LONG on literally every row) as a one-line query against the live table, not `market_bars` archaeology — the explicit acceptance criterion Stage 1A's own plan text named.
    - Full gate: **797 tests** (13 new: the golden + differential suite, relocated to `supabase/functions/agent-cycle/domain/strategy-config-presets.test.ts` after discovering `src/shared/**` test files break `npm run typecheck`'s root `tsc -b` project — no other `.test.ts` file had ever been colocated under `src/shared/` before this, for exactly that reason), typecheck/lint/build clean. Migrations (`20261006140000`) validated offline with `pglast`, pushed; `agent-cycle` redeployed; confirmed live — both presets seeded with correct hashes, `v4-compat` active, a real manual cycle producing `regime_state='LONG'`/`eligible_arms=['breakout_long','pullback_long']`/a populated `strategy_config_hash` on all four assets, with `no_candidate_reason` correctly distinguishing `opportunity_consumed` (BTC/ETH/AVAX — an edge WAS detected, already recorded from a prior tick) from `no_arm_triggered` (SUI — genuinely nothing fired).

  - **Stage 1B — the five corrections carried forward from 1A, CODE-COMPLETE, NOT YET DEPLOYED OR COMMITTED, 2026-10-06.** (Volume-confirmation removal, directionPolicy/arm-enable wiring, and short-reachable-but-disabled gating were already wired and deployed as part of Stage 1A's actual shipped code — see `activeIntradayLsConfig`/`policyConfig` in `index.ts`'s Pass 1 — leaving these five as the genuinely remaining items.)
    - **Management-path ATR fix.** `index.ts`'s management branch (previously `if (settings.strategyProfile === 'aggressive')`, guarding which ATR feeds `buildTargetProposal`'s MOVE_CLOSER/MOVE_OUT step size) widened to `'aggressive' || 'intraday_ls'`, both now calling `managementAtrPctFor(settings.strategyProfile, ...)` instead of a hardcoded literal. `managementAtrPctFor` itself already had a correct `intraday_ls` case (added in an earlier pass) — this call site simply never reached it. Entry-path sizing was already correct; only the management/steering path had the gap.
    - **Giveback-gate fix, the most architecturally interesting of the five.** `position-monitor/plan.ts`'s giveback EXIT (sampling always ran; the exit itself was hardcoded `input.strategyProfile === 'aggressive'`) now also fires for `intraday_ls` when the active config's `givebackEnabledForIntradayLs` is true. Since `position-monitor` is a separate Edge Function from `agent-cycle` with no pre-existing path to `strategy_configs`, this required answering the architectural question Stage 1A's own tracker entry left open — resolved by reusing the established cross-function-import precedent already in this codebase (`position-monitor/plan.ts` already imports `closePosition` straight from `agent-cycle/broker/accounting.ts`): `position-monitor/index.ts` now also imports `loadActiveIntradayLsConfig` directly from `agent-cycle/db/strategy-config.ts` — the SAME config row `agent-cycle` itself reads, so the two functions can never silently disagree on whether the ratchet is live. Deliberately does NOT call `ensureConfigSeeded` from this function (agent-cycle is the one writer); a not-yet-seeded config reads as `false` — today's exact pre-existing behavior — rather than seeding from a second place. A genuine hash-mismatch (tampered row) still throws and fails the whole monitor tick closed, same discipline as every other critical-input failure in this function.

Same values, same error-message text (now interpolated instead of hardcoded) — a same-value refactor, not a behavior change, and deliberately NOT the larger "wire config-schema.ts's `minOhlc30mBars`/`minSpot5mPoints` fields into this check" task, which is a genuine behavior-change candidate for a future pass, not this cleanup.

Wired into `index.ts`'s Pass 1 as a new branch between the opportunity-consumed check and candidate promotion: gated on the active config's `signalDriftRuleEnforced` (false in `v4-compat` — unreachable there, matching "no production caller today"; true in `v4.1-corrected`), a failing drift check sets a new `no_candidate_reason = 'signal_stale'` and — deliberately — does NOT set `intradayLsOpportunityContext`, so `opportunity_bar_ts` is never written and the same trigger bar can still qualify a later cycle if price reverts, rather than being permanently burned. Full gate: **810 tests**, typecheck/lint/build clean. **Deployed, live-verified (both `agent-cycle` and `position-monitor`), committed and pushed (`e27a9cf`)** — all three steps done after this entry was first written.
  - **Stage 2 — deterministic replay engine, CODE-COMPLETE, DEPLOYED (the extraction only), NOT YET COMMITTED, 2026-10-06.** Full design (including two real corrections found by actually running it, not just reading the code) lives in the plan's own "CFG-1 Stage 2" section. Four internal gates, same "work on one unit, verify before continuing" discipline as every prior stage:
    - **2B.0 (live-input inventory, a BLOCKING gate completed before any reader code was written) found TWO real gaps**, both resolved by exclusion rather than a silent proxy: (1) the live spot price (`marketData.price`, from `/coins/markets`) is not a bar series and has no `market_bars` row at all — resolved by sourcing it from the per-decision audit trail (`market_snapshots.price`, a flat column written unconditionally every cycle) instead; (2) the fade arm's own hourly `closeSeries`/`volumeSeries` (a THIRD, separate `/market_chart` fetch, distinct from both `candles` and `dailyCloseSeries`) have no `market_bars` timeframe at any granularity — resolved by always assembling them empty (inert for LONG/SHORT bias; `detectFadeOpportunity`'s own length guard degrades cleanly) and excluding any real `regime_state='NEUTRAL'` row from the golden-replay comparison set, reported separately rather than silently passed. Both are permanent, documented limits, not defects — and both are zero-impact today since bias has never once recorded NEUTRAL live.
    - **2A — `detectCandidate` extracted.** The `intraday_ls` Pass-1 block (`index.ts:854-974`) moved verbatim into a new pure `strategy/intraday-ls/detect-candidate.ts` (zero DB access — the one read it needs, `readLastConsumedOpportunityBarTs`, stays the caller's job exactly as `shouldEmitOpportunity` already receives it today, mirroring the zero-exception "pure core, impure shell" convention already established by `position-monitor/plan.ts`/`cycle/build-context.ts`/`cycle/plan-decision.ts`). One deliberate, minor, documented cost change: the DB read now runs unconditionally per asset/cycle instead of only after earlier gates passed (a few extra cheap indexed point-selects, never a correctness change — the value depends only on prior cycles' own writes). 10 new composition/wiring tests (one per branch). **Full existing suite passed GREEN, UNEDITED** — the gate's own proof, exactly as Stage 1A's discipline requires. Deployed; live-verified against two real post-deploy cycles reproducing the exact pre-extraction row shape across all four assets.
    - **2B — `market-bars-reader.ts`'s `assembleAsOfCycle` built, then CORRECTED once after actually running it live rather than trusting the design on paper.** First version excluded any bar whose own `ingested_at` wasn't populated-and-in-range — live-checked and found this would make **daily replay require ~50 real days and 4h replay ~8 days** to become possible at all (`filterNewBars`' dedup means an already-stored bar is never re-stamped, so a pre-Stage-0 daily bar's `ingested_at` stays null forever even though every cycle since has continued to re-fetch and re-confirm the identical value — CoinGecko is always re-requested in full, never incrementally). Corrected to reason per-*series*: one confirmed bar in an (asset,timeframe) series proves that fetch re-verified the whole window it covered, so every bar with `close_time` at-or-before that confirming write is visible, not just the one bar that happened to get freshly persisted. Rests on one named, explicit assumption (an already-closed bar's value doesn't change between fetches) rather than a silent premise. 14 fixture tests, including two pinning the correction itself.
    - **2C — the golden-replay driver (`run-golden-replay.ts` + two DB-read modules) is written and type-checks cleanly.** A manual dry run against the one real `pullback_long` candidate pair (BTC+ETH, `2026-10-06T14:15:00.961Z`) surfaced a SECOND, purely temporal blocker distinct from 2B's fix: **zero daily bars had been confirmed for ANY asset yet** (a daily bar lands once per 24h; Stage 0 had shipped only ~2-4 hours before this attempt) — not a bug, a plain fact. Added an explicit pre-flight check to the driver itself that refuses to run and states this plainly, rather than producing a wall of confusing mismatches that look like a harness defect.
    - **CLOSED, 2026-10-07 ~11:10 UTC — the exhaustive window-wide replay is done and explained end-to-end, not just spot-checked.** No live `SUPABASE_URL`/`SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` available locally to run `run-golden-replay.ts` as a literal process (and deliberately not fetched via `supabase projects api-keys`, per this project's established secret-handling discipline) — so its exact logic was run against the FULL window instead, data pre-fetched via the same safe `db query --linked` path already used throughout: **278 candidate-eligible rows, 94 decision cycles, all 4 assets.**
      - **Found and fixed a real defect in `fetchRealDecisionsInWindow` itself** (not in `assembleAsOfCycle`/`detectCandidate`): an occupied asset writes TWO rows per `(run_id, asset)` — a `'management'` row (no `arm_id`/`bias` by design) and a separate `'candidate'` shadow row (which carries them) — see CLAUDE.md's own "occupied-asset shadow candidates" entry. The fetch wasn't filtering `decision_type`, so it compared `detectCandidate`'s output (which only ever recomputes detection, independent of occupancy) against BOTH rows, producing a false mismatch against the management row that had nothing to do with correctness. Fixed: scoped to `decision_type IN (NULL, 'candidate')`, mirroring the DB's own `coalesce(decision_type, 'candidate')` unique-index semantics exactly.
      - **After that fix, every remaining mismatch belongs to exactly one explained class, zero exceptions**: a row decided before its own series' first confirming write. The daily series' real confirming write landed at `2026-10-07T00:15:01.047Z` — 15 minutes after the calendar UTC midnight close (CoinGecko's `/market_chart` hadn't yet surfaced the new point at the very first post-midnight cycle) — correcting an earlier, approximate "~00:00 UTC" framing in this session's own narration; the driver's pre-flight check was already correct throughout, since it gates on "has any bar been confirmed," never on calendar time. **Checked explicitly: zero mismatches at or after that exact timestamp, across all 4 assets.**
      - **Net result: 278 replayed, 6 excluded (NEUTRAL bias, as pre-registered), 131 pre-confirmation mismatches fully explained, zero unexplained mismatches anywhere in the window.** This is the closed, exhaustive proof Stage 2's own gate required.
    - Same-session live health check (2026-10-07 ~06:45 UTC, 14+ hours after this stage's own deploy): 7/8 `decision` runs and 12/12 `monitor` runs completed cleanly in the trailing 2 hours; the one failure was the already-documented, accepted `coingecko rate limit exceeded` (the known 15-min-cadence quota tradeoff), self-healed next cycle. 6 positions opened and closed since this stage's own deploy, all via legitimate `time_stop`/`agent_close` reasons, zero stuck/anomalous positions — direct evidence the Stage 1B + Stage 2 changes are operating correctly against real trades, not just passing tests. Portfolio NAV $19,801.97, realized P&L cum -$103.27 — ordinary paper-trading variance, not a system fault.
    - The golden window and its required branch-coverage rule were pre-registered **before** implementation (not picked after seeing what replays cleanly): the entire provenance-complete range, never a hand-picked subset, with the driver refusing to report a verdict unless all 4 assets, >=10 cycles, >=1 `no_arm_triggered`, >=1 `opportunity_consumed`, and >=1 populated `arm_id` are present — live-checked and already comfortably satisfied (6-22 `no_arm_triggered` and 3-15 `opportunity_consumed` per asset, 2 real candidate rows).
    - **The governing invariant, stated prominently per explicit user review feedback**: Stage 2 is a REPRODUCTION engine, not a counterfactual strategy simulator — every row replays under the EXACT `strategy_config_hash` it actually ran under (never "the currently active config") and against REAL historical account state (a plain DB read of what already happened, never a simulated portfolio). Testing a *different* config against history is explicitly not what this stage does or will ever do; Stage 3 sidesteps that harder problem entirely via model-free price-path statistics instead of portfolio simulation.
    - Typecheck/lint/build clean throughout; full suite **834 tests** (810 + 10 `detectCandidate` + 14 `market-bars-reader`). **Committed and pushed (`fa04d71`)**, on top of Stage 1B (`e27a9cf`).
- **EXP-1 — multi-account experiment platform, Stage E1 DONE, DEPLOYED, LIVE-VERIFIED, 2026-10-07.** Explicit user instruction, interrupting the CFG-1 Stage 2→3 sequence deliberately (Stage 3 resumes after): run ~20-30 simultaneous paper accounts at mixed cadences (mostly 60min, some 30, a handful 15), config × cadence × starting-capital as the real variation axes, for a week. Full design in the plan's own "EXP-1" section, reviewed and corrected once by the user before approval (synchronization must be proven on `market_tick_id`, never `decided_at`; variant/portfolio split with capital on the portfolio, not the variant; variant immutability via freeze-on-start + a resolved-config snapshot on every run; a dispatcher-level `market_ticks.logical_tick_at unique` to close a tick-claiming race the per-account fixes alone don't cover).

(2) `agent_runs_one_running_decision_idx` was a GLOBAL mutex on `(kind) where status='running' and kind='decision'` — at most one decision cycle could run system-wide; widened to `(portfolio_id, kind)`. New pure resolution: `globalSettings = readSettings(...)` → `variant = loadVariantForPortfolio(supabase, portfolio.experiment_variant_id)` → `settings = resolveAccountSettings(globalSettings, variant)` → `effectiveDecisionIntervalMinutes = variant?.decisionIntervalMinutes ?? strategy.decisionIntervalMinutes` (the dual-source trap caught before shipping — see below). Aggregate `MonitorRunSummary.status` priority: `completed` (if anything genuinely completed) > `skipped` > `failed` > `duplicate_tick` — documented as a deliberate choice for the eventual multi-portfolio case; with exactly one portfolio today every branch is mutually exclusive so this reduces to the old derivation exactly.

- ~~**V0 execution mode: manual-only (2026-09-19, explicit user product decision)**~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-23/24 — `agent-cycle` is now ALSO automatic, every 15 minutes, per explicit user instruction.** `pg_cron` job `agent-cycle-15min` (`*/15 * * * *`, migration `20260923190000_agent_cycle_15min_cron.sql`) — created inactive, activated by a one-shot DB-native job at its first natural firing at-or-after 2026-09-24 12:30 IST (07:00 UTC), which then unschedules itself. **The manual "Run agent" button is completely unaffected** — separate infrastructure (`supabase.functions.invoke` with `{trigger:'manual'}`) from the cron's `net.http_post`; `agent-cycle`'s handler already had a fail-safe-default `parseTrigger` (unrecognized/empty body → `'scheduled'`, never accidentally `'manual'`) built in anticipation of exactly this, so no application code changed, only infrastructure. `market-refresh-5min` was DISABLED (`cron.alter_job(..., active => false)`, not dropped/unscheduled — trivially revertible) in the same change, since `agent-cycle` already upserts `market_quotes` on every run; `position-monitor-10min` is untouched, still fully automatic. ~~`decision_interval_minutes` (180) is now genuinely dead~~ **WRONG — this claim caused a real bug, see below.**
  - **Real, quantified cost the user was shown before confirming**: one `agent-cycle` invocation is 7 CoinGecko requests (Balanced) or 11 (Aggressive, per `providers/coingecko.ts`'s own "1+3N"/"1+5N" comments — NOT the 1-request-per-tick the user initially assumed, which was corrected with an exact endpoint-by-endpoint breakdown before proceeding). At 15-minute cadence that's ~2,976 ticks/month × 7-11 = **~20,800-32,700 requests/month**, still above the documented 10,000/month CoinGecko Demo-tier cap even with `market-refresh` disabled (which alone only removes 8,640/mo). **User explicitly confirmed proceeding at 15 minutes anyway, to revisit the interval later** — this is a known, accepted tradeoff, not an oversight; watch live for CoinGecko rate-limit/quota errors, which could degrade `position-monitor`'s own independent polling too (same shared API key).
  - Live state confirmed post-deploy: `cron.job` shows all 4 jobs correctly (`position-monitor-10min` active, `market-refresh-5min` inactive, `agent-cycle-15min` inactive-pending-activation, `agent-cycle-15min-activate-once` active and one-shot). Migration validated offline (`pglast`) and registered in `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` (this pass also went back and registered the two EARLIER unregistered migrations, `20260923150000`/`20260923180000`, closing a bookkeeping gap from when they were applied via `db query --file` instead of `db push`).
  - Prior entry, now superseded, kept for history: "The decision cycle (`agent-cycle`) now runs exclusively from the Home screen's 'Run agent' button — never on a schedule... `agent_settings.is_paused` is now permanently `false` (migration `20260919131706_manual_execution_mode.sql`) so a manual click actually executes the pipeline rather than being skipped."
  - **Bug found and fixed same day, 2026-09-24: scheduled ticks were silently no-oping for 5 of every 12 cycles.** `agent_settings.decision_interval_minutes` was left at its pre-cron value (180) when the cron was set up — turned out NOT dead: `cycle/idempotency.ts`'s `buildDecisionIdempotencyKey` floors a *scheduled* trigger's key to `decision-<nowIso floored to decisionIntervalMinutes>`, so at 180 minutes every tick within the same 3-hour window (06:00-09:00 UTC, etc.) produced the IDENTICAL key. `cron.job_run_details` showed all 6 ticks from 07:15-08:30 UTC "succeeded" (the HTTP POST always returns 200), but `agent_runs` showed only ONE genuine new decision row in that span (`decision-2026-09-24T06:00:00.000Z`, from the 07:15 tick) — every later tick collided on the unique idempotency key and silently became a `duplicate_tick` no-op. Net effect: the system was still only deciding once per 3 hours, just now doing so automatically instead of by click, not actually running every 15 minutes as intended. **Quick fix applied**: `UPDATE agent_settings SET decision_interval_minutes = 15` — buckets now floor to 15-minute windows matching the cron, confirmed by direct query.
  - **Permanent fix deliberately deferred, parked for the next feature/enhancement pass** (explicit user instruction — "park the permanent fix for the next enhancement"): `src/shared/strategy/profiles.ts`'s `STRATEGY_PROFILES` already declares the semantically-correct value per profile (`aggressive: decisionIntervalMinutes: 15`, `balanced: decisionIntervalMinutes: 180`), and `index.ts:358` already computes `const strategy = strategyFor(settings.strategyProfile)` — but `strategy.decisionIntervalMinutes` is never actually read anywhere; both `buildDecisionIdempotencyKey` (index.ts:385) and the news-lookback window (`lookbackMinutes = decisionIntervalMinutes + newsLookbackOverlapMinutes`, index.ts:431) still read the flat `settings.decisionIntervalMinutes` (the raw `agent_settings` column) regardless of active profile. The quick fix above works for right now (Aggressive is the active profile and 15 happens to be its correct value), but if the profile is ever switched back to Balanced, `decision_interval_minutes` will silently stay at 15 unless someone remembers to flip it back to 180 by hand — this is exactly the class of bug the per-profile field was designed to prevent, it just was never wired in. **Next enhancement should wire `strategy.decisionIntervalMinutes` into both use sites, replacing `settings.decisionIntervalMinutes` entirely**, so the bucket width and news-lookback window self-adjust with whichever profile is active. **This unwired gap is now doubly stale** — `profiles.ts` still says `aggressive: 15`, but the live cadence (see next entry) is 60; the parked fix, whenever it lands, must update the profile's own declared value too, not just wire the read sites to it.
- **Cadence changed 15 → 60 minutes (2026-09-27), explicit user instruction: "Change the agent/ trade execution from 15 minutes to 60 minutes."** `pg_cron` job `agent-cycle-15min` was unscheduled (not merely disabled) and replaced with `agent-cycle-60min` (`0 * * * *`, top of every hour), migration `20260927120000_agent_cycle_60min.sql`. `agent_settings.decision_interval_minutes` updated `15 → 60` in the same migration, in lockstep with the schedule — learned from the 2026-09-24 bug above that this column silently drives both idempotency-key flooring and the news-lookback window, so leaving it stale after a cadence change would have quietly narrowed news lookback to 15 of the 60 minutes since the prior decision. Live-verified post-push: `cron.job` shows `agent-cycle-60min` active on `0 * * * *`, `agent_settings.decision_interval_minutes = 60`. Manual invocation untouched, as with every prior cadence change. **Side effect, not the motivation: this also resolves the CoinGecko quota overage accepted on 2026-09-24** — at 7-11 requests/invocation, hourly cadence is at most 744 ticks/month × 11 ≈ 8,184 requests/month, now under the 10,000/month Demo-tier cap. `context/current-configuration.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and `context/architecture.md` updated to match; `context/specs/trading-strategy-aggressive-v3.md`'s H6 pre-registration also updated (cadence is an infra parameter, not a strategy parameter, and H6 has still not started — no Aggressive-originated entry exists yet — so this is completing pre-registration, not tuning to an outcome).

The two positions from V1's first live trade (BTC + ETH longs, opened 2026-09-21 08:41 UTC) had no automated SL/TP monitoring for roughly 22 hours (secret created 2026-09-21 08:32 UTC with the placeholder, fixed 2026-09-22 ~07:50 UTC) — checked at both discovery and resolution, neither position ever crossed its stop or target during the gap, so no realized loss resulted, but the exposure window was real while it lasted. `cron.job_run_details` reporting "succeeded" throughout this entire window was never meaningful evidence either way (`net.http_post` is fire-and-forget — see Architecture Decisions) — the lesson generalizes past this one incident.

The replacement is a much slower daily-trend regime filter, framed explicitly as a hypothesis to falsify (H1-H5 in the doc), not a claimed edge.

Fixed by migration (`20260922065804_agent_decisions_output_payload_nullable.sql`). (2) `agent-cycle`'s idempotency key floored to the 3-hour `decision_interval_minutes` bucket regardless of trigger source, so one manual click claimed the whole window and every further deliberate click that session returned `duplicate_tick` — see the dedicated Completed entry below for the fix (manual/scheduled key split + a real concurrency mutex, not just a wider key).
- ~~**Known, intermittent, external — "coingecko rate limit exceeded."**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-22 — and the "outside this project's control" framing above was wrong, not just outdated.** This project had never used a CoinGecko API key at all — every request went to the fully anonymous public endpoint, rate-limited to **5-15 calls/min shared across all of CoinGecko's worldwide anonymous traffic**, not the 10,000/month the user (correctly) recalled from CoinGecko's own docs — that figure belongs to the free **Demo** tier (100/min + 10,000/month), which requires registering and sending a key, something this project had simply never done. One `agent-cycle` run alone bursts 7 requests near-simultaneously (`Promise.all` across `/coins/markets` + 3 endpoints × 2 assets); against a 5-15/min shared bucket that's enough on its own, well before any monthly cap is remotely in play. User registered a free Demo key and set it as both a Supabase secret and a local `.env` value; see Completed for the full wiring. **Live-verified fixed**, not just plausible: 3 back-to-back manual `agent-cycle` invocations (21 CoinGecko requests within a few seconds) all completed cleanly — a load that had reliably failed earlier the same session.
