Gemini is removed entirely from the code path — no fallback, shadow, or comparison provider — but its secrets are still in place, deliberately: no live cycle has yet actually called Jev (both open positions are LONG, so no `OPEN_LONG` candidate has reached the veto step), so the precondition for unsetting `GEMINI_API_KEY_*` hasn't been met yet. See the dedicated Completed entry immediately below for the full record, and "Next Up" for what's still open (the live ALLOW-pass-through check, a live-called Jev cycle, then Phase 4).
- **Phase 2 — "Jev as a portfolio-management decision layer" (2026-09-22/23): DEPLOYED and live-verified.** Widens Jev from pure entry-veto to managing existing positions (`HOLD`/`ADD`/`REDUCE`/`CLOSE`/`MODIFY_PROTECTION`), deterministic code remaining the sole authority over final size/price throughout. See the dedicated Completed entry for the full record — repository audit first, a plan draft, four independent user corrections applied during review (two genuine correctness defects: a realized-P&L gap in `close_position_atomic`, and an unsafe blanket confidence-gating rule that would have kept risk on for a low-confidence CLOSE), then a controlled live smoke test (2026-09-23) covering the full chain from Jev's raw response through to the next `position-monitor` tick. **The first real management decision genuinely reached Jev** (`model_version: 'jev-1.13.0'`, non-degenerate probability distributions) **and correctly resolved to HOLD for both assets** — zero trades, positions byte-for-byte unchanged, NAV reconciling exactly.
- **Strategy Profiles + Aggressive V3.1 (2026-09-23): CODE-COMPLETE, NOT YET DEPLOYED OR COMMITTED.** Adds `agent_settings.strategy_profile` (`'balanced' | 'aggressive'`, fails closed) as a first-class selector, routed through a new `strategy/registry.ts` dispatch module. **Balanced** is the exact deployed Phase 2.1 flow, unmodified — proven by three regression suites (`regime.test.ts`/`rules.test.ts`/`build-context.test.ts`) passing unedited and a dedicated test that its effective sizing caps stay exactly 0.20/0.30 after the DB ceiling raise. **Aggressive** (`strategy_version: v3.1-jev-intraday-30m`) is new: two deterministic edge-triggered opportunity detectors originate entries (never Jev), its own ATR30-derived protection regime, its own capital policy, and Jev judges detected opportunities via ENTER/SKIP rather than originating them. Full design: `context/specs/trading-strategy-aggressive-v3.md`.
  - **Built in three explicitly gated passes into `index.ts`** per the user's own instruction (strategy selection only → Aggressive routing → shared downstream integration), each independently re-running the full test suite before proceeding — a discipline that caught two real bugs before they shipped: (1) the shared management branch was feeding Balanced's 4h ATR into Aggressive's MOVE_CLOSER/MOVE_OUT step size instead of its own 30m figure; (2) the pre-existing ADD/REDUCE-too-small-to-fill fallback reverted to the RAW Balanced-regime candidate rather than the Aggressive-neutralized one, which could have silently resurrected a Balanced regime-flip CLOSE the moment a small ADD came back `not_applicable`.
  - **First live manual test (same day) surfaced that the shipped protection mechanism was structurally inert** — investigated with real data (not guessed): the pre-registered +1.5R profit-locking stop computed a price ABOVE entry for a long, which `positions_sl_tp_ordering_valid` makes impossible to persist (zero executions, ever); Jev's management context carried none of the profit-state fields it was documented as receiving (a live call proved it); no high-water tracking existed anywhere; R was sampled only at manual decision cycles, so a real BTC position's +1.80R peak was never seen by anything. This was **not** an Aggressive strategy failure — the entire observed giveback happened while the system was still running Balanced, before the profile switch — but it was a real defect in what had just shipped.
  - **Revised into V3.1** (plan reviewed and corrected through one more round — the reviewer caught that an initial fix conflated market-path R with economic P&L R, a distinct bug from the one being fixed): two separately-persisted R metrics (`priceR`, `positionPnlR` — see the spec's §6), an immutable ruler captured atomically at origination (`initial_entry_price`/`initial_stop_loss_price`/`initial_risk_usd`, plus `partial_realized_pnl_usd` so REDUCE doesn't misreport as giveback), position-monitor-sampled high-water state (`sampled_mfe_r`/`sampled_mae_r`), and a monotone giveback ratchet that closes the position (`close_reason: 'profit_giveback'`) when accumulated profit retraces too far from its peak — sampled under either profile, but only ever EXITED under Aggressive. Retires the old, structurally dead `deterministicTighten` mechanism outright rather than leaving it alongside the new one.
  - Legacy positions (the two live BTC/ETH longs from 2026-09-21) get the ruler backfilled only where structurally provable no protection change ever executed; `high_water_tracked_from` is deliberately left NULL for them — permanently ineligible for the giveback mechanism, since their true historical peak predates any tracking.
  - Also fixed in passing: `MODIFY_PROTECTION` is now omitted from the offered action set whenever no legal stop-tighten exists (previously offered-then-silently-rejected — a shared-code fix, applies to both profiles), and the management action question is reframed around marginal return rather than "are you bullish?" for Aggressive specifically.
  - **Pre-deploy review caught one real gap**: `positionPnlR`'s realized component was GROSS of the fee each REDUCE actually paid (`trades.realized_pnl` is deliberately gross everywhere else in this codebase, and `reducePosition` was simply accumulating that same gross figure into the new `partial_realized_pnl_usd`) — meaning repeated REDUCEs on one position would have silently overstated the profit the giveback ratchet was protecting, by the cumulative fees paid. Fixed by introducing `ReducePositionResult.partialRealizedPnlDelta = realizedPnl − fee`, feeding it (not the gross `realizedPnl`) into `partial_realized_pnl_usd` — `trades.realized_pnl`/`positions.realized_pnl`/NAV reporting are untouched, still gross, matching every existing test. 3 new tests in `accounting.test.ts` pin this exactly.
  - Full gate: **585/585** tests, clean `deno check`/typecheck/lint/build. Migration validated offline (`pglast`). **Not deployed, not committed** — per the original brief's explicit instruction, awaiting the user's go-ahead.
- **Phase 2.1 — "decouple entry eligibility from position management" (2026-09-23): DEPLOYED and live-verified.** User-reported control-flow bug investigated and found NOT to describe the deployed code (it matched pre-Phase-2 behavior, stale by about an hour) — reported back honestly with evidence before any plan was written. Surfaced and fixed one real defect anyway: `news_veto_enabled` was silently also gating the management layer (now split into its own `management_enabled` flag), plus a real test-coverage gap (`cycle/collect-candidates.ts`'s `collectModelCandidates`/`shouldCallModel`, 23 new tests) that had let the original coupling go unnoticed. The news-provider-failure coupling was reviewed and deliberately kept, promoted to a named, documented invariant with its product consequence stated explicitly. Full gate: 438/438 (first test needing a real OS permission — `--allow-read`, scoped narrowly). Deployed and live-verified with a second controlled cycle — both assets again genuinely reached management under the new independent flag. ADD/REDUCE/CLOSE/MODIFY_PROTECTION remain unit-proven but not yet observed executing live across two real cycles now — an honest, unforced gap, not something to force by manipulating data.

## Current Goal

- ~~Build the smallest end-to-end autonomous BTC/ETH paper-trading loop: market data → news → indicators → one Gemini decision → risk gate → paper broker → Supabase → Chrome extension.~~ **Superseded** — this loop is fully built, deployed, and live (Trading Strategy V1). The model call in it is being migrated from Gemini to Jev (see Current Phase); once that deploys, the loop's model step reads "one Jev veto call," not "one Gemini decision."

All of this — coin-id mapping (`BTC`→`bitcoin`), endpoint URLs, the two-tier granularity — is private to this one file; nothing outside it knows CoinGecko exists.

- **Unit 4 — news adapter: complete, via RSS (CryptoPanic abandoned by user decision).**
  - `src/shared/news/types.ts` / `provider.ts` unchanged from the original design (see below) — `NewsProvider` was always provider-agnostic, so the provider swap took zero changes to either file. This is the interface boundary actually paying for itself, not just a stated principle. (`getRecentNews(assets, lookbackMinutes)` — sizing `lookbackMinutes` from `agent_settings.decision_interval_minutes + news_lookback_overlap_minutes` remains the caller's job, i.e. Step 7, agent-cycle wiring, position-model plan numbering.)
  - **User decision (2026-09-18), overriding the original CryptoPanic instruction:** given the unresolved free-tier uncertainty (below), avoid CryptoPanic for V0 entirely. Use free public RSS feeds from a small curated set of reputable outlets instead — no paid API, no account, no token, no web scraping. Explicit requirements: RSS only, BTC/ETH relevance filtering, freshness filtering, deduplication, normalize into the existing schema, fail gracefully per-feed, don't expand scope.
  - `supabase/functions/agent-cycle/providers/rss-news.ts` — `RssNewsProvider`. 8 feeds (Cointelegraph, Decrypt, Bitcoin Magazine, CryptoSlate, The Block, Bitcoin.com News, CoinDesk, The Defiant) — **every URL checked live before being added** (HTTP 200, genuine RSS 2.0, real recent items; two redirect via 301/308 and are kept at their original URL since `fetch()` follows redirects by default, confirmed live rather than assumed). Feed failures are isolated per-feed via `Promise.allSettled`; the call only throws (fails closed) if **every** feed fails — one dead source among a redundant curated set shouldn't blind the cycle, but a total outage still must not look like "no news today."
  - Relevance filtering is a word-boundary regex per asset (`\b(bitcoin|btc)\b`, `\b(ethereum|eth|ether)\b`, case-insensitive) — **verified programmatically against real false-positive traps** (method, weather, together, ethereal, aesthetic — none match) **and true positives** ($ETH rallies, ETH/USD — all match) before use, not assumed safe from eyeballing the pattern.
  - Cross-source dedup by normalized headline text (lowercase, punctuation stripped), on top of exact `externalId` dedup — a wire story two outlets both carry shouldn't produce two decision-feed entries for the same event.
  - New dependency: `fast-xml-parser` — pinned to `^5.11.1`, not the `^4.5.0` first tried, because `npm audit` flagged a moderate CDATA/comment-injection CVE in `XMLBuilder` (the *serialize* side) in versions before 5.7.0. This codebase only ever uses `XMLParser` (the *read* side) and never constructs XML, so the vulnerable path isn't reachable either way — took the patched version anyway since the fix was a trivial version bump. Registered in both `package.json` and the root `deno.json` import map, same dual-runtime mechanism as `zod` (Unit 3).
  - Real API research done before writing any parsing code, not assumed: fast-xml-parser's actual output shapes for the same logical field vary by whether the source XML used CDATA and/or attributes (plain string vs. `{__cdata}` vs. `{'#text', '@_...'}`) — confirmed against a live-captured Cointelegraph item; RSS `pubDate` is RFC 2822, not ISO 8601 — confirmed `new Date(...)` parses it correctly in Deno/V8 before relying on it; fast-xml-parser collapses a single-child group to a bare object instead of a one-element array — guarded explicitly, a known library gotcha rather than a hypothetical one.
  - `rss-news.test.ts` — 14 Deno tests, fixture XML built to mirror the real captured structure (CDATA link, `guid isPermaLink`, RFC 2822 date, CDATA HTML description). Includes three **deliberately separated** failure-path tests after checking, live, that `fast-xml-parser` is lenient (does not throw on mismatched tags, binary garbage, or truncated markup — only on things like an unclosed CDATA section): one test for "well-formed XML, item fails per-field validation," one for "parses fine but isn't RSS-shaped," and one for the one input that actually throws from the parser — an earlier draft asserted "malformed XML degrades gracefully" using input that turned out not to exercise the catch block at all, caught by checking what the library actually does rather than trusting the test's own name.
  - `rss-news.live-check.ts` (manual-only, same pattern as `coingecko.live-check.ts`) — live run against all 8 real feeds returned 36 correctly-tagged BTC/ETH items across 7 of 8 sources, HTML cleanly stripped from summaries, no cross-asset false positives on real headlines (an Iran-sanctions/bitcoin story tagged BTC-only, an Ethereum-upgrade story tagged ETH-only).
  - `deno check`, `deno test` (14/14), `tsc -b`, `vite build`, `oxlint` all pass clean.

RSS was the user's resolution, not a finding that CryptoPanic's status was confirmed one way or the other.
- **Unit 5 — deterministic indicators: implemented and test-verified.**
  - `src/shared/indicators/types.ts` — `MarketIndicators` (rsi14, ema20, ema50, macdHistogram, atrPct, volumeRatio, and two *signed* fields — distanceFromSevenDayHighPct / distanceFromSevenDayLowPct, rather than one unsigned "distance" — so the model reads direction, not just magnitude) and `RecentClose`, both matching `market_snapshots.indicators`/`recent_closes` exactly.
  - `supabase/functions/agent-cycle/indicators/calculate.ts` — the actual math. **Started this in `src/shared/`, moved it here after `tsc -b` failed**: `tsconfig.app.json` includes all of `src/`, so a Deno-only test file (`Deno.test`, `jsr:` imports) placed under `src/shared/` collides with the Vite/tsc build, which has no idea what those are — unlike the Unit 3 provider test, which never hit this because it already lived under `supabase/functions/`. The fix isn't a shim for tsc; it's that the calculation code belongs there anyway — `architecture.md` § System Boundaries already lists "indicator calculation" explicitly under what `agent-cycle` owns. Only the *type* is genuinely dual-runtime (the extension reads already-computed indicators back from Postgres to display); the computation itself is called from nowhere but the agent cycle. Only `types.ts` stays in `src/shared/indicators/`.
  - Source series matter and are deliberately not uniform: RSI/EMA20/EMA50/MACD read `closeSeries` (hourly, per Unit 3); ATR% and the 7-day high/low read `candles` (4-hourly, true OHLC — ATR needs real highs/lows, closeSeries doesn't have them); volume ratio reads `volumeSeries` (hourly). The 7-day window is sliced by actual timestamp against the latest candle, not by a fixed element count — robust to any provider gaps.
  - RSI and ATR both use Wilder's smoothing (the standard definition, not a plain moving-average ratio). MACD is the standard 12/26/9, returning only the histogram (the only variant `MarketIndicators` asks for).
  - Fails closed on short input: `InsufficientDataError` (local to this module — a calculation-boundary failure, not a provider one, so it doesn't belong in `src/shared/providers/errors.ts`) if a series is shorter than an indicator's minimum window. Real cycles are nowhere near these minimums (Unit 3: ~721 hourly points, ~180 4-hourly candles vs. minimums of 15–35) — this guards malformed/truncated upstream data, not normal operation.
  - **Every non-trivial expected value in `calculate.test.ts` was computed independently in Python first**, not hand-derived in the test file — direct lesson from Unit 3's own epoch-timestamp bug (trusted mental arithmetic once already; wasn't going to trust RSI/EMA/MACD/ATR arithmetic by hand either). 18 Deno tests: exact matches to the Python reference (RSI, ATR%, volume ratio, EMA — asserted to 1e-9), trivial closed-form edges (constant series → EMA equals the constant, MACD histogram exactly 0, ATR exactly 0; monotonic series → RSI exactly 100 or 0), the 7-day window filter genuinely tested against an out-of-range candle with extreme values that must NOT leak into the result, every `InsufficientDataError` boundary, and the top-level `calculateIndicators`/`getRecentCloses` orchestration.
  - One bug pre-empted rather than found: the test fixture originally spaced synthetic candles using a fractional day count through `Date.setUTCDate`, whose behavior for non-integer input isn't something to rely on — rewritten as plain epoch-millisecond arithmetic before ever running it, on the same "don't trust unverified date arithmetic" instinct the epoch-ms bug in Unit 3 established.
  - `deno check`, `deno test` (18/18), `tsc -b`, `vite build`, and `oxlint` all pass clean from the corrected file locations.

- **Step 2 (position-model plan) — shared domain types/schemas: complete, tested.**
  - `src/shared/positions/types.ts` — `Direction`, `PositionStatus`, `CloseReason`, and `Position` (mirrors the live `positions` table exactly, including the new Step 1 columns). `PositionState` (`'FLAT'|'LONG'|'SHORT'`) is deliberately a plain TS union, not a Zod schema — it's never parsed from external input, always computed by trusted code from an already-validated `Position`, so Zod's actual job doesn't apply. `derivePositionState(position: Position | null)` is the one place that computation happens, so Steps 3/4/5/7 import it rather than re-deriving "open position exists ⇒ LONG/SHORT" ad hoc in more than one place.
  - `src/shared/decisions/types.ts` — `Action`, `PrimaryDriver`, `ReasonType`, `RiskStatus`, `SizeCapApplied`, `InvalidationCondition`, `Reason`, `ModelDecisionProposal`, `AgentDecision` (mirrors `agent_decisions` exactly, flat, including every Step 1 column).
  - **`ModelDecisionProposal` is a Zod discriminated union on `action`, not one flat object with nullable SL/TP** — `OPEN_LONG`/`OPEN_SHORT` structurally require `stopLossPct`/`takeProfitPct`; `HOLD`/`CLOSE` structurally cannot have them. Gets Step 3 real type narrowing (`if (proposal.action === 'OPEN_LONG') proposal.stopLossPct` is a `number`, no guard needed) instead of a runtime check the type system could have ruled out. Invalidation is `.min(1)` on the open branches only (contract doc: an open always represents a new thesis) — HOLD's invalidation requirement is state-dependent (non-empty only while a position is already open), which depends on context this schema doesn't have, so that specific rule is left to Step 3, not encoded here.
  - **Every proposal branch uses `.strict()`, not Zod's default strip mode** — code-standards.md "treat LLM output as untrusted until schema validation succeeds." Caught this needed doing while planning the test for it: under strip mode, a `HOLD` payload carrying a stray `stopLossPct` would parse "successfully" with that field silently dropped, quietly defeating the whole point of the discriminated union. `.strict()` turns that into a real rejection instead — verified directly (`decisions.test.ts`: `'HOLD carrying a stray stopLossPct is REJECTED, not silently stripped'`).
  - **`Reason` is also a discriminated union** (`NEWS` requires `newsId`; `TECHNICAL` forbids it) — found while writing the *test* for the original flat version: a `newsId: z.string().uuid().nullable()` shape let a `NEWS` reason parse with `newsId: null`, silently permitting exactly the invalid state "citing news without saying which one." Caught before it shipped, fixed at the source, not patched around — same category of catch as the SL/TP-ordering-vs-exhaustion-ceiling bug in Step 0, one layer up.
  - `AgentDecision` stays fully flat (not nesting an `effective` sub-object for the four `effective*Pct` fields) — deliberately matches the table's own flat column layout, consistent with every other normalized type in `src/shared/` ("persistence should be a near-direct map, not a translation layer"). `inputPayload`/`outputPayload` are `z.unknown()`, not further specified — over-specifying their shape now would presume NEWS/TECHNICAL methodology details the user has explicitly deferred to a separate review.
  - Test files live under `supabase/functions/agent-cycle/domain/` (`positions.test.ts`, `decisions.test.ts`), not beside the types in `src/shared/` — same reason as Unit 5's `calculate.ts`: `tsconfig.app.json` includes all of `src/`, so a `Deno.test`/`jsr:`-import test file placed there collides with the Vite/tsc build. The types themselves stay in `src/shared/` since they're genuinely dual-runtime (the extension reads `Position`/`AgentDecision` rows back from Postgres to render them); only the test files needed to move.
  - 27 new Deno tests (8 positions, 19 decisions) — the discriminated-union structural guarantees (missing SL/TP on opens rejected, stray SL/TP on HOLD/CLOSE rejected, empty invalidation on opens rejected, old `BUY`/`SELL` vocabulary rejected), the `Reason` union's NEWS/TECHNICAL split, `derivePositionState`'s three branches, and representative `Position`/`AgentDecision` accept/reject cases including a HOLD with every open-only field null and a rejected proposal still carrying its full `effective_*` config (denormalized on every row regardless of outcome, by design).
  - `deno check`, `deno test` (86/86 across the whole repo, no regressions), `tsc -b`, `vite build`, `oxlint` all pass clean. No migration touched, no other context file touched — scoped to exactly the types Step 2 named, per the plan's binding protocol.

  - `src/shared/risk/appetite-mapping.ts` — `riskAppetiteThresholds(appetite)`, the pure lookup flagged pending three separate times before this unit existed to hold it. Returns `{minConfidence, riskBudgetPct}` per tier, exactly the plan's numbers (conservative 0.75/0.75%, balanced 0.65/1.00%, aggressive 0.55/1.50%) — balanced reproduces the confidence bar the system has described as current since Unit 2's seed, verified by a dedicated test rather than assumed consistent.
  - `src/shared/risk/sl-tp.ts` — `computeStopLossTakeProfitPrices` (percentage → absolute price, both directions), `exhaustionPrice` (now the one real implementation — see below), `validateStopLossTakeProfit` (configurable pct-bounds check + the ordering-and-exhaustion-ceiling combined predicate from Step 0, now as real production code instead of test-local logic).
  - `src/shared/risk/sizing.ts` — `deriveRiskBasedNotional` (risk-at-stop sizing) and `applySizingCaps` (takes the minimum across risk-based/single-trade/asset-exposure/cash, records which one bound). Confidence never enters sizing anywhere — it's a gate in `gate.ts`, never a size multiplier, matching the contract doc exactly.
  - `src/shared/risk/gate.ts` — `evaluateRiskGate`, the orchestrating pure function matching the contract's evaluation order exactly: `HOLD` → `not_applicable` unconditionally; `CLOSE` → `rejected` only if `FLAT`, else `approved` with **no confidence check, no re-entry block, no caps** — verified directly with a CLOSE at confidence 0.01 against a 0.99 threshold, and a CLOSE with an active stop-out block, both still approved; `OPEN_LONG`/`OPEN_SHORT` → state check, confidence, SL/TP validation, stop-out re-entry block (direction-matched only — proven with a same-asset opposite-direction case that is NOT blocked), then sizing. Staleness is deliberately **not** a gate check here — confirmed against architecture.md's own Agent Cycle ordering (freshness validated at step 4, before the model is ever called at step 8), so a `ModelDecisionProposal` never exists for stale data by construction; re-checking it inside the gate would be redundant with Step 7's actual responsibility, not missing coverage.

Now concretely demonstrated rather than argued abstractly: three tests span the 5% crossover exactly — a 3% stop clamped to the single-trade cap ($2,000, matching the plan's own worked example precisely), an 8% stop approved outright at the risk-derived $1,250 with no cap, and the exact 5% boundary where both candidates tie and resolve to "approved, no cap" (the risk-based figure is checked first). Still open for the user to revisit if they'd rather the numbers land differently now that it's visible in real output, not just arithmetic.

A genuine minimum-viable-notional floor *above* zero remains unspecified and was **not** silently invented — flagged here rather than guessed at, since no such number exists anywhere in the plan, the migration, or prior discussion.
  - **Closed a loop from Step 0**, per that step's own stated purpose ("these become the test inputs for Steps 3–5, so the contract is enforced rather than merely documented"): `contract.fixtures.ts`'s local `exhaustionPrice` reimplementation (necessary when Step 0 had zero production code to import) is gone; `contract.test.ts` now imports the real one from `src/shared/risk/sl-tp.ts`, so those 19 tests check actual production code, not a parallel copy that could quietly drift from it. `isValidSlTp` (the ordering-only helper) deliberately stays test-local — it validates already-computed *prices* (matching the DB's own `positions_sl_tp_ordering_valid` constraint shape), while the production `validateStopLossTakeProfit` validates *percentages* and does the price conversion itself — different input shape for a different layer, not the same function twice.

No migration touched, no context file touched — scoped to exactly what Step 3 named.
- **Step 4 (position-model plan) — paper broker/accounting: complete, tested against every Step 0 fixture.**
  - `src/shared/trades/types.ts` — the `Trade` type deferred from Step 2 (it needed the broker's actual field shape to design against, which didn't exist yet). `TriggerReason` reuses `positions/types.ts`'s `CloseReason` directly rather than redefining an identical 4-value enum under a second name — they really are the same set of values on two tables. Modeled as a plain union of three `.strict()` branches (open / agent-close / automatic-close), not a single `z.discriminatedUnion`, because the real discriminant is a combination of `intent` category and provenance, not one literal field the way `ModelDecisionProposal.action` is — same "illegal combinations don't parse" guarantee, just without clean call-site narrowing.
  - `supabase/functions/agent-cycle/broker/accounting.ts` — `applySlippage`, `openPosition`, `closePosition`, and (added while implementing, see below) `computeNav`/`computePositionValue`. Lives under `agent-cycle/`, not `src/shared/`, matching the established indicators/providers precedent — this is server-only execution logic, and architecture.md already named this exact location for it in Step 1's own edit.
  - **The primary correctness proof: one test loops over all 6 of Step 0's `ACCOUNTING_SCENARIOS` and asserts the real `openPosition`/`closePosition` functions reproduce every expected value exactly** (quantity, both fees, both cash-after figures, cost basis, realized P&L) — including the gap-through-exhaustion case, where the broker's own exhaustion clamp (not the test) is what makes the realized loss land at exactly `-2000` rather than `-3000`. This is what actually proves Step 4 satisfies Step 0's contract, not just that freshly-invented numbers happen to work.
  - **A genuinely subtle correctness point, found by re-reading the exact DB constraint rather than assuming the first design instinct was right:** `trades_provenance_valid` (Step 1) requires `trigger_reason = 'agent_close'` *exactly* whenever `decision_id` is set — no exception for exhaustion. So when a short gaps past exhaustion right as an agent-initiated `CLOSE` is executing (rare — the monitor's 10-minute cadence should already have closed it long before the next 3-hour agent cycle — but not impossible, e.g. a missed monitor run), `positions.close_reason` becomes `'collateral_exhausted'` (accounting truth) while `trades.trigger_reason` stays `'agent_close'` (required provenance) — **the two columns are allowed to diverge, and the broker does so deliberately rather than by accident.** Both directions of this are tested explicitly: the fully-automatic case (both fields become `collateral_exhausted`) and the agent-initiated-but-gapped case (they diverge as described). Getting this wrong would have meant a real `INSERT` rejected by the DB constraint at runtime, not just a design nitpick.
  - **A second real catch, found while independently verifying a fresh slippage-inclusive test case in Python:** Step 0's stated accounting identity (`cashAfterClose − startingCash == realizedPnl − totalFees − totalSlippage`) only holds because Step 0's fixtures used zero slippage. Once `realizedPnl` is computed from *fill* prices (which already embed slippage cost, correctly — P&L should reflect what actually happened, not a hypothetical zero-slippage fill), re-subtracting `totalSlippage` separately double-counts it. Verified independently before writing it into a test: the identity that actually holds with real slippage is the simpler `cashAfterClose − startingCash == realizedPnl − totalFees` (no separate slippage term) — both forms coincide only when slippage is 0, which is all Step 0 ever exercised. This would have been a wrong test assertion if written from the contract doc's exact wording without checking it against real nonzero-slippage numbers first.
  - `computeNav`/`computePositionValue` — trading-domain-contract.md §2's NAV formula, added now rather than left for Step 5 or 7 to reinvent, since *both* the position monitor (Step 5) and agent-cycle wiring (Step 7) persist `nav_snapshots` and need it. A short's value is floored at 0 (not just its unrealized P&L) for the same "collateral is the entire downside" reasoning as the close-time exhaustion clamp — protects a NAV read at an arbitrary snapshot moment from going negative on one position.
  - Slippage direction is derived purely from `side` (BUY moves the fill price up, SELL moves it down) — `direction` (long/short) never enters `applySlippage` directly, only via the `side` it's already been mapped to (`OPEN_LONG→BUY`, `CLOSE_LONG→SELL`, `OPEN_SHORT→SELL`, `CLOSE_SHORT→BUY`), matching how `side` was designed to carry exactly this mechanical meaning back in Unit 2's original schema.
  - One TypeScript literal-widening fix needed while implementing: a ternary's result got assigned into a plain object literal (`tradeCore`), and TS silently widened the narrow `'CLOSE_LONG'|'CLOSE_SHORT'` result to `string` in that position — breaking the `Trade` union's ability to match any branch. Fixed with `as const` on each ternary arm; a real, if minor, gotcha worth knowing about the next time a literal type needs to survive being spread into an object.
  - 12 new Deno tests (the full 6-scenario fixture loop, the provenance-divergence pair, longs-never-clamp, slippage direction, an independently-verified slippage-inclusive round trip, trade-shape checks, and 4 NAV/valuation cases including the floor) = 134/134 across the whole repo. `deno check`, `tsc -b`, `vite build`, `oxlint` all clean. No migration, no context file touched — scoped to exactly what Step 4 named plus the NAV addition, which is documented here rather than silently expanded into.
- **Step 5 (position-model plan) — position monitor: complete, deployed, and live-verified end-to-end against real market data.**
  - `supabase/functions/position-monitor/triggers.ts` — pure trigger detection (`findFirstTrigger`, `resolveFillPrice`), no I/O. Walks a replayed price-point window chronologically and returns the *first* breach, which is what "SL wins" (contract doc §5) actually means across a multi-point window — proven mathematically (a single point can't breach both SL and TP given valid ordering) and empirically (a short gapping past its exhaustion price is caught as a stop-loss breach with no separate exhaustion-detection code needed, since a valid short's SL always sits below `2×entry`).
  - `supabase/functions/position-monitor/plan.ts` — `planMonitorActions`, the pure per-tick decision core: given already-fetched price points per open position, buckets each into stale (logged, held) / held (no trigger) / closed (calls Step 4's `closePosition` with `decisionId: null`), threading cash sequentially across multiple closes in one tick. `index.ts` is reduced to reading this input from the database and persisting its output — no accounting math or trigger logic lives in the entrypoint itself, same split as every prior step.
  - `supabase/functions/position-monitor/index.ts` — the Edge Function entrypoint, the first one in this project. Implements architecture.md's 6-step cycle exactly: idempotency via a floored-to-`monitor_interval_minutes` key (a retried invocation of the same tick collides on `agent_runs_idempotency_key_unique`, detected via the insert's own unique-violation code rather than a separate check-then-write); zero open positions skips the price fetch entirely; "since last run" replay window (bootstraps to `now − 2×interval` on the first-ever run); stale assets are held, not closed, and a run is only marked `skipped` (vs. `completed`) if *every* open-position asset was stale; NAV snapshot re-reads cash and open positions fresh after all closes rather than projecting from in-memory state, so a lost race is reflected correctly regardless of this run's own view; unexpected errors mark the run `failed` with `error_detail` rather than guessing at partial state (fail closed).
  - **`close_position_atomic`** (new migration `20260918124421_position_monitor_rpc.sql`) — a Postgres function, the single shared atomic-persistence primitive for closing a position, callable by both this monitor and, later, the agent cycle's own `CLOSE` handling (one owner, never two implementations of the race guard). Does **not** reimplement any accounting math — the caller (this Edge Function, and later the agent cycle) runs Step 4's pure `closePosition()` first and passes the already-computed trade fields in; the function's only two jobs are (1) a conditional `UPDATE positions ... WHERE status = 'open'` inside the same transaction as the fill, so a concurrent closer reliably loses rather than double-executing (Postgres's row-level MVCC makes this atomic with no extra locking needed), and (2) applying `net_cash_delta` to `portfolios.cash` via a fresh `cash = cash + delta` read-modify-write rather than trusting the caller's possibly-stale pre-computed `cashAfter` — this also incidentally protects against two *different* assets' trades racing on shared cash, a case the contract doc never separately specified a guard for. `security invoker` (not `definer`): the only caller is the service-role key, which already bypasses RLS and holds full table grants by default, so there's no privilege gap definer semantics would need to bridge, and invoker avoids that class of privilege-escalation footgun entirely. Granted to `service_role` only.
  - **Live-verified against the real database, both required interleavings of the concurrent-close race** (via `supabase db query --linked`, which authenticates through the CLI's own management-API session — never the service-role key): seeded a real open position, called `close_position_atomic` twice with different trade IDs for the same position. First call: `won_race: true`, correct `cash_after`, position closed with the right fields. Second call (simulating the future agent-cycle's `CLOSE` arriving after the monitor already closed it): `won_race: false`, `cash_after: null`, confirmed via direct query that exactly one trade row exists and portfolio cash was untouched by the losing call. This is the literal scenario from the plan's required acceptance criterion, proven against real Postgres concurrency semantics, not just the Step 0 fixture. Test rows cleaned up afterward; portfolio cash restored.
  - **Deployed and live-verified end-to-end against real CoinGecko data**: seeded a real BTC long position (entry $90,000, stop $85,000) against the live BTC price (~$81,000, already through the stop); invoking the deployed function closed it correctly — `stop_loss` trigger, observed-price fill (not the stated stop level, per the fill-price policy), correct realized P&L, correct NAV snapshot, correct cumulative P&L. Also live-verified: the zero-open-positions fast path (real `agent_runs` row, zero price-fetch calls) and the idempotency guard (a second immediate invocation of the same tick correctly detected as a duplicate rather than redoing work). All test data cleaned up afterward; only the one genuine zero-positions `agent_runs` row was kept as real history.
  - **The function is invoked (both for these live checks and by the eventual cron job) using the public anon/publishable key as the bearer token, never the service-role key** — Edge Functions' default JWT verification accepts any valid project JWT including the anon key; the function's *own* privileged Supabase client is constructed from its auto-injected `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, which never passes through this session. This is also why the concurrent-close race could be proven live without ever handling that key directly (see below).
  - **Real deploy-time discovery, not caught by any local tool**: `supabase functions deploy` does **not** resolve the project-root `deno.json` import map the way local `deno check`/`deno test` do (confirmed against Supabase's own docs, then empirically) — each function needs its **own** `deno.json` for deployment (auto-discovered from the function's directory, no `--import-map` flag needed, confirmed by testing directly). Added `supabase/functions/position-monitor/deno.json` (mirroring the root's `zod` entry, plus the new `@supabase/supabase-js`, plus `compilerOptions.strict` so nearest-config-wins doesn't silently loosen local type-checking). **This is a genuine forward-looking flag for Step 6/7**: `agent-cycle/` has never been deployed either, and will need the identical treatment the first time it is — not something to rediscover from scratch.
  - **Real live-data discovery**: PostgREST serializes `timestamptz` as `"...+00:00"`, not `"...Z"` — confirmed against `agent_settings.created_at` before writing any row-mapping code. Zod's `.string().datetime()` (used by every domain schema, matching CoinGecko's own `Z`-suffixed convention) rejects the `+00:00` form by default. This had been latent since Step 2 (nothing before this step ever parsed a real fetched row into these schemas — Steps 0–4 only ever hand-constructed `Z`-format fixtures) and would have thrown the first time *any* code tried. Fixed at the DB-row-boundary (`index.ts`'s `toIsoZ`, via `new Date(x).toISOString()`) rather than loosening the schemas — keeps every domain type uniformly strict rather than accepting offsets that should never occur from any other source. **Flagged for Step 7**: the same normalization will be needed anywhere it reads `positions`/`agent_decisions`/`trades` rows back from Postgres.
  - `@supabase/supabase-js` added to `package.json` (`^2.58.0`) and both `deno.json` files — the first real Supabase client usage in this codebase (every prior DB interaction was either raw SQL via the CLI or REST reads with the anon key).
  - **`nav_snapshots.realized_pnl_cum` is a genuine running total**, not this run's own P&L under a misleading name: carries forward the most recent prior snapshot's cumulative figure (across either run kind) and adds only this run's delta. Caught and fixed while implementing, before it shipped as a wrong value.
  - `deno test` 20 new (12 triggers, 8 plan) = 158/158 across the whole repo (plus a pre-existing, unrelated `rss-news.test.ts` time-bomb found and fixed in its own separate commit — a hardcoded fixture anchor date had finally drifted far enough past real wall-clock time to fail 11/14 of those tests; nothing to do with this step). `deno check`, `tsc -b`, `vite build`, `oxlint` all clean.
- **Step 6 (position-model plan) — Gemini structured output + `callModel` seam: complete, live-verified against the real API with all three of the user's keys.**
  - `supabase/functions/agent-cycle/model/payload.ts` — `ModelCallPayload`, the exact object `callModel` receives and (later, Step 7) `agent_decisions.input_payload` persists verbatim. Plain TS interfaces, not Zod — this is data the system constructs and sends, not untrusted input to validate. Assembling real portfolio/market/news data into this shape is Step 7's job; this step only fixes its contract.
  - `supabase/functions/agent-cycle/model/prompt.ts` — the system prompt (`PROMPT_VERSION = 'v2-position-model'`), adapted from the superseded `gemini-decision-agent.md` §5 draft for the new action vocabulary, mandatory SL/TP-as-percentage, the synthetic-1x-short explanation, and the explicit invalidation/stop-loss separation. Carries forward unchanged: news-is-data-not-instructions, NEWS/TECHNICAL reason tagging, the overall evidence shape — the actual reasoning methodology is still the user's explicitly-deferred separate review, not something this step touched. **The exact required wording change applied verbatim**: "Trade only when the evidence crosses the decision threshold; otherwise HOLD," replacing the old "most cycles should be HOLD" framing.
  - `supabase/functions/agent-cycle/model/gemini-schema.ts` — `GEMINI_RESPONSE_SCHEMA` (Gemini's UPPERCASE-type responseSchema dialect, confirmed live), a loose `RawGeminiResponse` Zod schema, and `parseModelOutput`/`mapRawDecisionToProposal` mapping the flat raw wire shape into `ModelDecisionProposal[]` (Step 2). The wire shape is deliberately flat with nullable SL/TP (not an attempt at a conditional/oneOf schema) — correctness is enforced by re-parsing into `ModelDecisionProposal`'s own `.strict()` discriminated union, the same "loose wire shape → strict domain parse" split used at every other external boundary in this codebase, not a second, weaker validation layer.
  - **A real content-strictness gap found and fixed while implementing, same class of bug as Step 2's original `Reason`/`ModelDecisionProposal` catches**: the first draft of the HOLD/CLOSE mapping branch silently *omitted* a stray non-null `stopLossPct`/`takeProfitPct` before handing the object to the strict schema — which would have let the model contradict its own action (proposing a stop for a decision that structurally can't carry one) pass validation with the inconsistency quietly dropped, defeating the exact guarantee `.strict()` exists to enforce, just one layer earlier. Fixed to reject instead (`gemini-schema.test.ts`: `'HOLD carrying a stray stopLossPct is REJECTED, not silently stripped'`).
  - `supabase/functions/agent-cycle/model/call-model.ts` — `callModel(payload, apiKeys, fetchImpl?)`, the one seam every model call goes through (CLAUDE.md § AI-specific rules). Tries each key in order; **only a key-specific failure (HTTP error or network throw) rotates to the next key** — a content-level failure (non-`STOP` finish reason, unparseable JSON, a schema-shape mismatch) fails the call immediately without rotating, since the same prompt against the same model would hit the identical problem regardless of which key sent it, and rotating would just burn all three keys' quota for no chance of a different outcome. All-keys-exhausted raises `ModelCallError` naming every key's own failure reason.
  - **No vendor SDK** (`@google/genai` or similar) — raw `fetch` against the classic `generateContent` REST endpoint, matching the exact pattern already established for CoinGecko and RSS (typed request/response, Zod validation, no SDK dependency). Also sidesteps a second round of the exact "does this npm package need its own per-function `deno.json` to deploy" question Step 5 already answered once.
  - **Real live findings, verified directly against the API with the user's actual keys (2026-09-19), not assumed from docs** — a secondary doc-synthesis source claimed the classic `generateContent` endpoint was "legacy," superseded by a new "Interactions API," and that `gemini-2.5-flash` no longer appeared in current examples. Direct probing contradicted the alarming parts of this, same lesson as CoinGecko's own "docs/search said X, live API says otherwise" pattern earlier in this project: `models.list` confirms `gemini-2.5-flash` is still live and fully available on all three keys (alongside newer `gemini-3.5/3.6/3.7/3.8-flash` models — worth knowing about, not switched to, since the user already made this exact model choice explicitly one day earlier and nothing found here suggests a reason to override it); a direct `generateContent` call with `responseMimeType`/`responseSchema` returns HTTP 200 with correctly-shaped structured JSON; `systemInstruction: { parts: [...] }` (no `role` field) is accepted; `nullable: true` on a responseSchema property is honored.
  - **A genuinely important operational finding**: `gemini-2.5-flash` spends real "thinking" tokens (100+ observed on a *trivial* one-line test prompt) that count against the *same* token budget as the visible JSON output (confirmed live: `generationConfig.thinkingConfig.thinkingBudget: 0` measurably zeroes `usageMetadata.thoughtsTokenCount`). The superseded spec's original `maxOutputTokens: 4096` recommendation predates this finding and would risk a silent `MAX_TOKENS` truncation — mid-JSON — on a real, much longer production prompt. Raised to **8192**. Thinking itself was deliberately left at its default (not forced to a budget of 0): that's a reasoning-quality lever adjacent to the NEWS/TECHNICAL methodology the user explicitly deferred to a separate review, not a plumbing decision this step should make unilaterally — flagged here rather than silently chosen either way.
  - **camelCase used throughout, including in the raw JSON Gemini is asked to emit** — deliberately not snake_case (which the superseded spec's draft schema used out of typical REST-convention habit). There's no functional reason a schema-constrained model needs snake_case field names, and using one casing convention everywhere in this codebase (payload → prompt → responseSchema → raw parse → domain type) removes an entire case-conversion translation layer, and the class of bugs it could otherwise introduce, that existed only to satisfy a stylistic default.
  - **Live-verified end-to-end with a realistic two-asset payload** (`call-model.live-check.ts`, manual-only, real API/quota — same pattern as every other provider's live-check script): one FLAT asset with a news item, one LONG position with prior invalidation conditions. The real model response correctly proposed `OPEN_LONG` on the flat asset with valid, in-bounds SL/TP percentages and reasons citing both a named indicator and the specific news item by content; correctly proposed `HOLD` on the open position and **organically reaffirmed its exact prior invalidation text verbatim** — the anti-flip-flop behavior the prompt asks for, genuinely working end to end even though (see Open Questions) it isn't backed by a deterministic gate check.
  - `deno test` 20 new (12 schema/mapping, 8 key-rotation) = 178/178 across the whole repo. `deno check`, `tsc -b`, `vite build`, `oxlint` all clean.
  - **`CLAUDE.md` and `code-standards.md` updated** to match the exact new HOLD wording (both previously said HOLD is "valid and preferred" — the same "biased default" framing the prompt itself moved away from; `project-overview.md` already had the corrected wording from Step 1). `code-standards.md`'s `callModel` bullet now states the key-rotation-vs-content-failure distinction explicitly, closing the docs-sync debt progress-tracker.md had flagged three separate times since Unit 2.
  - **Incidental fix, unrelated to the seam itself**: `.env`'s three `GEMINI_API_KEY_*` lines had stray whitespace around `=` (`KEY = value`) — Deno's `--env-file` tolerates this (confirmed), but plain `bash`/`source` does not, silently misinterpreting the value as a command to execute. Stripped the whitespace directly (a local, gitignored file — no tracked-file or review risk). See Session Notes for a related incident this caused.

- **Corrective fix (2026-09-19, between Steps 6 and 7) — state-dependent invalidation is now deterministically gate-enforced.** Closes the Step 6 Open Question exactly as flagged: `evaluateRiskGate`'s `HOLD` branch (`src/shared/risk/gate.ts`) now rejects a HOLD on a LONG/SHORT position (i.e. `context.currentState !== 'FLAT'`) that carries empty `invalidation`, rather than returning `not_applicable` unconditionally. FLAT + HOLD with empty invalidation is unaffected (still valid — there's no open thesis to invalidate). A narrow, one-branch change, deliberately scoped no wider: no change to the prompt, model, output schema, sizing, SL/TP validation, CLOSE handling, or anything else in the gate. This still cannot (and doesn't try to) verify the reaffirmed text is *honest* — only that the structurally-checkable part (a real, non-empty list) is actually present, same limit as every other content-shape check in this codebase.
  - The old blanket test `'HOLD is always not_applicable, regardless of state'` asserted exactly the gap this fix closes, so it no longer held — replaced with the four cases the user specified: FLAT+HOLD+empty (valid), LONG+HOLD+empty (rejected), SHORT+HOLD+empty (rejected), LONG/SHORT+HOLD+non-empty (valid). Net +3 tests (one removed, four added) = 181/181 across the whole repo. `deno check`, `tsc -b`, `vite build`, `oxlint` all clean.

- **Step 7 (position-model plan) — agent-cycle wiring: complete, deployed, live-verified end-to-end. This is the last step of the position-model plan** — market data → RSS news → indicators → portfolio/position state → Gemini → deterministic risk gate → paper broker → Supabase persistence, all wired together for the first time. No UI work, per explicit instruction — the Chrome extension UI is a separate, later phase.
  - `cycle/build-context.ts` (pure) — `checkMarketDataFreshness`, `deriveBlockedDirections`, `buildAssetInput`, `buildOpenPositionInput`, `buildPortfolioConstraints`, `buildRiskGateContext`: everything needed to shape raw fetched/read data into `ModelCallPayload` and `RiskGateContext`. `cycle/plan-decision.ts` (pure) — `planDecisionExecution`: given a proposal + its gate result, decides whether to execute and computes the full accounting via Step 4's `openPosition`/`closePosition` — never touches the database itself. `cycle/decision-record.ts` (pure) — `derivePrimaryDriver`/`citedNewsIds`, the two fields deliberately never asked of the model directly (Step 2's own reasoning). `db/row-mappers.ts` — `rowToPosition`/`toIsoZ` **extracted from Step 5's position-monitor** (same behavior, shared location — the agent cycle needed the exact same DB-row mapping and PostgREST timestamp normalization a second time, so it moved rather than being duplicated, per CLAUDE.md's "do not duplicate business logic").
  - New migration `20260919055617_agent_cycle_open_rpc.sql` — `open_position_atomic`, symmetric to Step 5's `close_position_atomic` but deliberately simpler: **no conditional race-guard**, since trading-domain-contract.md §6 already establishes opens have no equivalent race (the monitor never opens; overlapping agent cycles are prevented by the `agent_runs` idempotency key). `positions_one_open_per_asset_idx` remains a DB-level backstop, not the primary mechanism. Same two real jobs as `close_position_atomic`: persist exactly what `openPosition()` already computed, and apply `net_cash_delta` via a fresh read-modify-write.
  - `index.ts` — the third Edge Function entrypoint. Implements architecture.md's 14-step Agent Cycle exactly (step 14, "surface to the extension," needs no code — the extension already reads persisted state via its existing anon-key REST access). Checks `agent_settings.is_paused` before acquiring idempotency or fetching anything (a paused cycle doesn't even get an `agent_runs` row — an endless stream of "skipped, paused" records every cadence would just be noise). Maintains a live in-memory map of open positions across the whole cycle, refreshed as each asset's own action executes in turn, so a later asset in the same run correctly sees the NAV impact of an earlier one's close/open rather than a stale snapshot taken once at the top.
  - **A real, load-bearing bug found and fixed by live-testing, not by any local tool**: the first draft set `agent_decisions.position_id` to the new position's id *before* that position existed in the database (`open_position_atomic` hadn't run yet) — a foreign-key violation, since `positions.opened_by_decision_id` *also* has an FK back to the decision, meaning neither row can be inserted first with a direct reference to the other. Resolved the same way real-world circular-FK schemas always do: insert the decision first with `position_id: null`, call `open_position_atomic` (which can now legally reference the already-existing decision), then a small follow-up `UPDATE` links the decision to its new position. Live-verified directly (via `open_position_atomic` called through `supabase db query --linked`, reproducing index.ts's exact sequence) after the fix, not just re-deployed and hoped for.
  - `deno check`/`tsc -b`/`vite build`/`oxlint` all pass on every new file; `deno test` unaffected by the new orchestration code itself (27 new fixture tests across `cycle/*.test.ts` cover the pure logic — `build-context`, `plan-decision`, `decision-record` — the same "pure core, thin untested I/O shell verified live" split as Step 5) = 208/208 across the whole repo.
  - **Mid-step, user-directed model/key change (2026-09-19), found by live-testing this exact step**: `gemini-2.5-flash` — the user's own explicit choice from Step 6, confirmed working via direct API calls at the time — started actively failing during Step 7's live verification: 2 of 3 free-tier keys returned HTTP 503, and the third an explicit HTTP 404 stating "gemini-2.5-flash is no longer available to new users," naming `gemini-3.6-flash` as the replacement — even though `models.list` still listed `gemini-2.5-flash` as available on all three keys at that exact moment (a listing-vs-invocation gap, not a fluke — confirmed by re-checking `models.list` immediately after). Surfaced to the user rather than silently deciding; **the user's resolution: retire the 3-key free-tier rotation entirely in favor of a single paid-tier key (`GEMINI_API_KEY_1`) and switch to `gemini-3.6-flash`.** This is the exact "launch moves to a single paid-tier key — a config change behind the same seam, not a code change" transition already anticipated in this file's own Architecture Decisions, just happening now rather than at a later "launch" milestone: `callModel`'s rotation loop needed zero changes (a one-element `apiKeys` array degenerates correctly), only `agent-cycle/index.ts`'s `Deno.serve` handler (now reads only `GEMINI_API_KEY_1`) and `model/call-model.ts`'s `MODEL_ID` constant changed. Verified `gemini-3.6-flash` live before switching (same `responseSchema`/`thinkingConfig` mechanism, correct structured output) — it also spends *more* thinking tokens by default (275-298 vs. 100-162 for 2.5-flash on the same trivial prompt), reinforcing that the `maxOutputTokens: 8192` headroom from Step 6 remains the right call, not something to shrink back down.
  - **Full pipeline live-verified end-to-end against the real deployed function, real market data, real news, and the real (paid) model** — not just fixtures: `agent_settings.is_paused` temporarily flipped to `false` for exactly one test invocation, then restored to `true` immediately afterward regardless of outcome (paper-trading-agent risk discipline: never leave the agent capable of autonomous action as a side effect of testing). Result: a real completed run, BTC and ETH both correctly `HOLD`/`not_applicable` with real Gemini reasoning (`primary_driver: BOTH`, i.e. it cited both a real news headline and real indicator values), correct `market_snapshots`, correct zero-change `nav_snapshots` (cash/nav both exactly $10,000, matching a HOLD-only cycle). Since a HOLD-only real result doesn't exercise the open/close execution paths, **`open_position_atomic` and the agent-initiated branch of `close_position_atomic` (`decision_id` + `trigger_reason='agent_close'` together — the one provenance combination Step 5's monitor-only testing never exercised) were each separately, directly live-verified** against the real database with realistic seeded decisions/positions, reproducing `index.ts`'s exact sequence (including the circular-FK follow-up update) rather than trusting the unit tests alone for the one part of the system that unit tests structurally cannot reach. All synthetic test rows cleaned up afterward; the two genuine HOLD decisions from the real run were kept as real history, matching the established "keep honest history, remove only synthetic test artifacts" discipline.
  - **Deliberately not done in this step, flagged rather than silently assumed**: no `pg_cron` schedule was created for the decision cycle. The user's Step 7 instructions named the data/decision/execution pipeline but not scheduling, and — combined with `is_paused` already being a dedicated safety gate specifically meant to prevent "the agent begins trading the instant the schema lands" — automatic recurring invocation felt like a materially bigger, separate decision than building and manually verifying the function. The function is fully deployed and working; it currently only runs when explicitly invoked (manually, or by a cron job that doesn't exist yet). Unpausing and/or scheduling are both still open, explicit actions for the user to take when ready — same posture as Step 5's Vault-secrets gap, just for a different reason (there, the mechanism didn't exist yet; here, it's a deliberate scope boundary).
  - Secrets: `GEMINI_API_KEY_1` (now the paid key) pushed to the deployed function's real secret store via `supabase secrets set --env-file=.env` — this is a separate store from the local `.env` used for `--env-file` smoke tests, and needed re-pushing after the key value changed.
- **UI Step 1 (Chrome extension UI plan) — design system + popup shell: complete.** The first of a separately-numbered UI sequence (Steps 1-6) the user scoped after the backend was finished, reconciling this file's existing `ui-context.md` design tokens with a set of Stitch-generated reference mockups the user supplied for structure/density/typography (not colour — see below).
  - **Reconciliation, not a port**: the reference designs are a borderless Material-3 tonal system (six stacked surface tones, zero borders); `ui-context.md` — which the user chose to keep as-is — specifies the opposite ("Borders and surface contrast instead of heavy shadows"). Resolution: structure, density, and typography come from the reference designs; colour and depth strategy stay exactly as `ui-context.md` already had them. The mapping needed no palette rewrite, because the existing `--state-*-subtle` tokens already **are** the tinted-badge backgrounds the reference designs build with alpha overlays (`bg-tertiary/15` → `bg-state-success-subtle`, etc.) — two were simply missing (`--state-info-subtle`, `--state-neutral-subtle`) and were added alongside the existing four.
  - **Typography**: three vendored variable fonts (`@fontsource-variable/{geist,space-grotesk,jetbrains-mono}`), one per role — Space Grotesk (headlines), Geist (body — replacing the never-actually-loaded Inter token value), JetBrains Mono (numbers *and* the uppercase micro-labels, which is what gives the reference designs their dense, technical character). Fonts are imported from `theme.css`, which puts them in Vite's module graph — confirmed live via a real `npm run build` that the woff2 files land in `dist/assets/` automatically, with **no change needed to `scripts/copy-extension-assets.mjs`** (which only ever handled `manifest.json`, precisely because manifest isn't part of that module graph). An 11-step type scale (`.type-headline-{lg,md,sm}` / `.type-body-{lg,md,sm}` / `.type-data-{lg,md,sm}` / `.type-label-{md,xs}`) bundles family+size+weight+line-height+tracking into one class per role — deliberately not letting size and family be chosen independently, since the source designs pair them by hand everywhere. `label-*` bakes in `uppercase` (every use in the reference designs is uppercase, no counterexample); `data-*`/`label-*` get `font-feature-settings: 'tnum' 1, 'zero' 1` for non-shifting digits on live-updating prices — something the reference designs' own spec calls for but never actually implements.
  - **Radius**: tightened from Tailwind's stock 6/8/12px toward the reference designs' 2/4/8px density without going all the way (4/6/8px) — this system keeps borders, which read better with slightly more room than a borderless design needs.
  - New `src/components/ui/` (`card.tsx`, `badge.tsx`, `button.tsx`, `status-dot.tsx`, `progress-bar.tsx`, `stat.tsx`, `section-header.tsx`), `src/components/states/` (`loading-state.tsx`, `empty-state.tsx`, `error-state.tsx` — deliberately distinct visual treatments per ui-context.md's "never hide system failures behind an empty UI"), `src/components/shell/` (`app-header.tsx`, `bottom-nav.tsx`, `app-shell.tsx`). All generic/domain-agnostic primitives — e.g. `Badge`'s `long`/`short`/`hold`/`flat` variants are named for readability at trading-state call sites but resolve to the same colours as the generic `success`/`error`/`neutral` variants; no fetching, no feature logic.
  - **No shadcn init** — nothing in Step 1 needs Radix, and shadcn's generated components assume `--background`/`--foreground`/`--radius` variable names this token set doesn't use; `cva` + the existing `cn()` helper give equivalent ergonomics without an aliasing shim. Revisit if a later UI step genuinely needs a Radix primitive (e.g. a real Switch/Slider in Settings).
  - Popup resized from the Unit-1 scaffold's 400×560 to **420×600** (matching the reference designs' frame width; height chosen to fit a 56px header + 56px nav with real room for content between them, not 64px nav which — unlike the designs' own phone-framed context — would be an outsized fraction of a 600px popup).
  - `App.tsx` rewritten from the Unit-1 "scaffold, no agent connected" placeholder to the real shell wired to 4 tabs via local `useState` (no router — unjustified at this size; structured so a later detail view can push over a tab). Home tab additionally previews the primitives against representative values (so this step's own verification exercises real rendered content, not empty boxes — explicitly not feature work); Positions/Activity/Settings tabs each demonstrate one of the three shared states (empty/loading/error) as their placeholder, rather than all showing the same "coming soon" text.
  - Explicitly not ported from the reference designs (all confirmed fictional for this project): leverage/`1.1x LEV`/PERPs, `BTC/USDT` pairs (V0 is `BTC/USD` spot), SOL, trailing stops, multi-model consensus, SHA-256/Merkle audit hashes, `NON-CUSTODIAL EXECUTION`, websocket/`STREAM LIVE` chrome (CoinGecko is REST-polled, not streamed), fabricated latency figures, invented ID taxonomies, and external image URLs that would 404 or leak requests from the extension.
  - **Visual verification performed, not just claimed**: a Vite dev server (`.claude/launch.json`) serving `extension/popup.html`, screenshotted at 420×600 in the Browser pane. Confirmed live: all three vendored fonts rendering distinctly (not system fallbacks), the full badge/button/stat/progress-bar set with correct colours, tab switching across all four tabs (verified via direct DOM `.click()` dispatch after the pane's synthetic mouse-input path proved unreliable while backgrounded — a tooling limitation of this session, not a defect in the component code, confirmed by cross-checking the same navigation via `get_page_text` independent of any screenshot), and the active-tab accent-color + bold-label treatment on the bottom nav.
  - `npm run typecheck` / `build` / `lint` all clean; `deno test supabase/` 208/208 unaffected (confirms no backend behavior touched). `dist/manifest.json` and all three fonts' woff2 subsets confirmed present after a real build.
- **UI Step 2 — Home: complete.** The extension's first real Supabase-backed screen — no mock data anywhere; every figure on the page (NAV, cash, positions, prices, the latest decision and its reasoning) is a live read against the real database, live-verified against real rows from Step 7's own test runs.
  - **First-ever frontend Supabase client** (`src/supabase.ts`), and a real architectural question resolved along the way: how does the extension get a URL + key at all, given `.env.example`'s "never `VITE_`-prefixed anywhere" note? Resolved by re-reading what that note actually protects — CLAUDE.md's Security rules enumerate the privileged secrets that must never enter client code (Gemini keys, service-role key), and the anon key is deliberately not on that list, because RLS (not secrecy of the key) is the real boundary; "the extension ships the public anon key" was already decided back in Unit 2. Hardcoded directly in `src/supabase.ts` rather than any `VITE_` env var, so the question can't resurface ambiguously later — not a new policy, just resolving an implementation detail per an already-standing decision.
  - **`src/supabase.ts` deliberately does not live under `src/lib/`** — that path is already claimed by the `@/lib/*` alias, which resolves to the project-root `lib/` (shadcn's `cn()` helper, Step 1's own vite.config.ts alias-order comment). A file at `src/lib/supabase.ts` would have been unreachable at `@/lib/supabase`.
  - `src/features/home/{queries,use-home-data,format,home-screen}.tsx` — read-only queries (agent_settings, portfolios, nav_snapshots, positions, market_snapshots, agent_decisions, news_items) composed into one view model by a plain polling hook (30s interval; no data-fetching library added for a need this small), rendered by `home-screen.tsx` using only Step 1's existing primitives. `src/features/decisions/display.ts` holds the Action/RiskStatus -> badge-variant/label mappings outside `home/` on purpose: Positions, Activity, and the Decision-detail screen will all need the identical mapping, not a per-screen copy.
  - **A real race fixed before it shipped**: the initial polling hook created a fresh `cancelled` closure per call and only ever invoked the *first* one's cleanup (on unmount) — a later 30s tick's response could still overwrite a newer tick's if they resolved out of order. Fixed with a monotonic request-id ref; only the most recently *started* request's result is ever applied, regardless of resolution order.
  - **Per-position unrealized P&L is computed on the frontend, not read from a stored value** — `nav_snapshots.unrealized_pnl` is a portfolio-level aggregate; no per-position figure is ever persisted, so there is nothing else to display instead. `format.ts`'s `unrealizedPnl()` mirrors `accounting.ts`'s own formula exactly and is explicitly commented as display-only, not authoritative for anything execution-related — the one place this UI step knowingly computes something rather than only reading it, and only because no alternative exists.
  - **Two self-caught mistakes, fixed before verification**: (1) the "Unrealized P&L" sublabel's percentage first divided by `nav - pnl` — a hand-wavy stand-in for "NAV without this gain" that doesn't actually mean anything precise — corrected to divide by `portfolios.starting_capital`, a real, stable reference point already being fetched anyway; (2) a leftover fragment from an unfinished thought hardcoded the string `"gemini-3.6-flash"` directly into a card — caught before running the app at all (the exact class of doc/code staleness the Gemini model-name sync earlier this session exists to prevent), removed outright rather than wired up, since model version wasn't actually part of Step 2's brief.
  - **Live-verified with real data**, not fixtures: real PAUSED status from `agent_settings.is_paused`; real NAV $10,000.00 / +$0.00 unrealized (0.00%) matching the one nav_snapshot Step 7's test run produced; real BTC $80,957.00 (+4.38%) and ETH $2,623.80 (+5.60%) from `market_snapshots`; the real HOLD decision from Step 7's test, its TECHNICAL reason rendered verbatim and its NEWS reason's `newsId` resolved through `news_items` into an actual headline prefixed onto the raw reason text (the raw text itself embeds the bare UUID — a genuine, observed model-output quirk from Step 7, rendered as-is rather than silently rewritten, per ui-context.md "render only persisted decision reasons"). No SL/TP block and no risk-rejection block rendered, correctly, since the live decision was a HOLD with `risk_status: not_applicable`. Confirmed via `get_page_text` (not just a screenshot) and zero console errors; tab-switching across all four tabs re-confirmed unaffected.
  - Run Now / Pause are genuinely non-functional in this step, matching the user's own "presentation" framing and Step 1's identical precedent — no `control` Edge Function exists yet to wire them to safely, and inventing one wasn't in scope for a UI step. Buttons are fully styled and correctly reflect the real paused/running state (label and which is primary swap accordingly), each carrying a `title` tooltip saying so.
  - `npm run typecheck` / `build` / `lint` all clean; `deno test supabase/` 208/208 unaffected.

- **UI Step 3 — Decision + Position detail: complete.** The extension's first navigable screen — Home's "Latest decision" card now pushes into a full detail view, no bottom nav (matching the reference designs' own detail screen), with a back arrow returning to whichever tab was active.
  - **No router added.** `App.tsx` tracks `selectedDecisionId: string | null` alongside its existing `tab` state; when set, `DecisionDetailScreen` fully replaces `AppShell` rather than nesting inside it — consistent with Step 1's own "structured so a later detail view can push over a tab" design note, and with the reference designs' detail screen genuinely having no bottom nav of its own.
  - **A real second-consumer signal, acted on immediately**: `fetchLatestMarketPrices` and `fetchNewsHeadlines` (originally `home/queries.ts`) moved to a new `src/features/market-data/queries.ts` the moment Decision-detail needed the identical two queries — the exact "move it when a second real consumer appears, don't move it speculatively" call made explicitly *not* to make for `fetchAgentSettings` back in Step 2. New `src/features/positions/queries.ts` (`fetchPositionById`, open or closed — distinct from Home's `fetchOpenPositions`, which only ever needs open rows) anticipates Step 4's Positions screen wanting the same lookup, not just Decision-detail's own "position linkage" section.
  - **Technical and news evidence are read from `agent_decisions.input_payload`**, not re-derived from `reasons[]` text — the actual indicator values and the full news list the model saw, not just what its prose happened to mention. `input_payload` is untyped jsonb crossing a real DB -> TypeScript boundary (this system's own internal record, but no compile-time guarantee travels with it), so `decision-detail/queries.ts` parses it with a loose, fully-optional Zod schema (not `.strict()` — a missing/renamed field degrades that one stat to absent, not a crashed screen) rather than a blind cast.
  - **Risk gate detail is unconditional** here, unlike Home's card (which only surfaces risk when rejected/clamped) — `not_applicable` is the only status that hides the section, so an `approved`/`clamped` open's actual effective caps and sizing are always inspectable, not just its failures.
  - **Two real lint findings fixed, not suppressed**: (1) the polling hook's `refresh` synchronously called `setState('loading')` as its first action while also being invoked from a mount effect — the exact "cascading render" shape react/oxlint flags, and rightly, since the initial state is already `'loading'` so that reset was a wasted identical re-render on mount. Split into a plain `refresh` (used by the effect) and a `retry` wrapper that adds the loading-reset back specifically for the one case that wants visible feedback — a manual click after an error — called from a click handler, not an effect. (2) `Date.now()` was called directly inside a render body (impure — React re-invoking a render function without an actual clock tick would desync "time ago" labels within one logical render); replaced with the same controlled, periodically-updated `now` state `home-screen.tsx` already established, rather than two different patterns for the identical problem.
  - Position linkage renders an open position's live unrealized P&L (reusing `format.ts`'s `unrealizedPnl()`, the same necessary display-only computation Home already does) or a closed position's `close_reason`/`realized_pnl` — but the live decision has neither (a FLAT HOLD carries no `position_id`), so this section, the SL/TP section, and the risk section are all correctly and verifiably absent rather than rendered empty.
  - **Live-verified against the real HOLD decision from Step 7's test run**: real extracted indicators (RSI 66.6, EMA20 $80,453.90, EMA50 $79,019.80, MACD hist -143.03, ATR% 1.10%, vol ratio 1.04×, 7-day high/low distances -0.88%/+7.89% — matching the raw `input_payload` values exactly), the real news article's full summary text, and real metadata (`model_version: gemini-3.6-flash`, `prompt_version: v2-position-model`, the real `run_id`) — confirming the model-name fix from Step 2 actually reads live data rather than needing to be hand-verified separately. Forward and back navigation both confirmed via `get_page_text`, zero *new* console errors (one pre-existing, harmless 404 for the bare `/` root path — this project has no root `index.html` by design; the real entry is `/extension/popup.html` — accumulates in the console's history from `preview_start`'s own initial probe, not from anything this step's code requests).
  - `npm run typecheck` / `build` / `lint` all clean (zero warnings, not just zero errors); `deno test supabase/` 208/208 unaffected.

- **UI Step 4 — Positions + Activity: complete.** Both dedicated tabs are now real, and both can push into Decision-detail (Step 3) — the same navigation mechanism, now with three entry points instead of one.
  - **Positions** (`src/features/positions/`) shows one card per asset in the universe: the currently open position if any (entry/mark/stop/target, live unrealized P&L, quantity, cost basis, a link to its opening decision), or — new relative to Home's condensed FLAT row — the *most recently closed* position for that asset when flat, with its close reason, realized P&L, and a link to whichever decision is more informative: the one that closed it (agent-initiated) or, falling back, the one that opened it (an automatic stop-loss/take-profit/collateral-exhaustion exit has no closing decision at all — trading-domain-contract.md §4 — so linking to the opening decision is the next most useful thing rather than nothing). `fetchPositionSummaries` (`positions/queries.ts`) fetches a capped, recent window once per portfolio and buckets by asset in application code rather than one query per asset per status.
  - **Activity** (`src/features/activity/`) merges three independent tables into one reverse-chronological feed — `agent_decisions`, `trades`, and `agent_runs` (skipped/failed only) — capped at 50 events total, fetched and correlated in application code rather than a new backend view invented just for this screen. A decision that executed a trade (approved/clamped OPEN or CLOSE) is enriched with that trade's fill price and realized P&L rather than rendered as two separate timeline entries for the same moment; a trade with no `decision_id` (automatic stop-loss/take-profit/collateral-exhaustion) gets its own standalone event, since there is no decision to attach it to.
  - `src/features/decisions/display.ts` gained `CLOSE_REASON_LABEL`/`CLOSE_REASON_BADGE_VARIANT` (the four-value close-reason vocabulary Positions and Activity both render identically) alongside the Action/RiskStatus mappings from Step 3 — same "one shared mapping, not one per screen" reasoning.
  - **ui-context.md's Positions spec also asks for an inline invalidation-state indicator; this step deliberately doesn't add one.** Invalidation lives on the *decision*, not the position, so showing it inline would mean a second query per card for something already one tap away via the opening/closing-decision link this step already added. Noted here rather than silently diverging from a written spec — a reasonable scope call, not an oversight, but flagged as such.
  - **Thoroughly live-verified, including every event type real data doesn't produce yet**: with only 2 real rows in the whole system (both HOLD decisions, zero trades, zero positions), confirmed real data renders correctly first, then temporarily seeded synthetic rows — a rejected OPEN_LONG, an agent-initiated CLOSE with its trade, and three automatic closes (stop-loss, take-profit, collateral-exhausted) each with their own position+trade, plus a skipped decision-cycle run and a failed monitor run — reproducing the *same* circular-FK resolution real `agent-cycle/index.ts` uses (a decision must exist before a position can reference it as `closed_by_decision_id`, but the decision needs the position's id too — insert the decision with `position_id: null`, insert the position, then update the decision). All eight required event types (HOLD, risk rejection, agent CLOSE, stop loss, take profit, collateral exhaustion, skipped, failed) confirmed rendering distinctly and correctly via `get_page_text`, in correct chronological order, zero console errors — then every seeded row deleted and the pristine 2-decision/0-position/0-trade real state confirmed restored afterward.
  - `npm run typecheck` / `build` / `lint` all clean (zero warnings); `deno test supabase/` 208/208 unaffected.

- **UI Step 5 — Settings + controls: complete.** All four tabs are now real. Every figure on this screen is live `agent_settings`/`agent_runs` data or the real risk-appetite mapping — nothing fabricated (no invented "READY"/"SYNCED" health-check strings the reference designs used with no real mechanism behind them).
  - **A scope question resolved before writing any code, not silently decided mid-build**: the user's Step 5 brief lists "pause/resume" and "run now" as controls, but the underlying fact that made these presentation-only in Step 2 — no `control` Edge Function exists, and the anon key is read-only by RLS — is still exactly true; nothing about Step 5 being "the controls step" changes that. Building a real control endpoint is backend work a UI-only step has no business doing on its own initiative (and raises its own unresolved question: this is a single-user, no-login V0 that ships its anon key publicly — a *write* endpoint reachable by anyone who has that key is a materially different exposure than the already-accepted *read* one). Kept consistent with Step 2's established pattern: real current state, displayed correctly, interaction has no effect — flagged clearly rather than either silently building new backend surface or silently leaving the screen wrong.
  - **The risk-appetite selector goes one step more conservative than pause/resume/run-now**: it's not click-interactive at all, rather than "clickable but inert." Pause/resume/run-now are momentary actions where "looks clickable, does nothing" reads honestly as a preview.
