A three-way selector represents *persistent configuration* — letting a click visually highlight a different option with no way to persist it would revert on the next 30s poll and look like a bug, not a deliberate limitation. Shows the real configured value only.
  - `riskAppetiteThresholds()` (`src/shared/`, Step 3 of the backend plan) is reused directly rather than re-tabulated on the frontend — the same appetite → min-confidence/risk-budget mapping the real risk gate uses, not a second copy that could quietly drift from it.
  - **Another second-consumer refactor**: `fetchLatestDecisionRun` (Home-only, hardcoded to `kind='decision'`) generalized into `fetchLatestRun(portfolioId, kind)` in a new shared `src/features/system-status/queries.ts`, since Settings needed the identical query for *both* run kinds (System status shows the last decision cycle and the last position-monitor cycle separately). Same judgment call as `market-data/` (Step 3) and `positions/` (Step 4) before it.
  - **One precise terminology choice**: asset rows show "1× PAPER, LONG/SHORT," not "SPOT" (the reference designs' label). Spot trading conventionally implies long-only; this system supports both directions per asset (trading-domain-contract.md), so "SPOT" would misdescribe it in the same category of error `ai-workflow-rules.md` already warns about for shorts.
  - **Live-verified against real config**: simulated fee/slippage read exactly as `fee_bps: 10` / `slippage_bps: 5` → 0.10% / 0.05% per side; all four risk hard limits (max single trade 20%, max asset exposure 35%, SL range 0.5–15%, TP range 0.5–50%) match the live `agent_settings` row exactly; risk appetite shows BALANCED correctly highlighted with 65% min confidence / 1.00% risk budget, matching `riskAppetiteThresholds('balanced')` precisely; decision cadence "Every 3h" and position-monitor cadence "Every 10m" correctly derived from `decision_interval_minutes: 180` / `monitor_interval_minutes: 10`; System status shows both real completed runs with correct elapsed time. Confirmed via `get_page_text`, zero new console errors (the one present is the same pre-existing, harmless root-path 404 noted in Steps 3-4).
  - `npm run typecheck` / `build` / `lint` all clean (zero warnings); `deno test supabase/` 208/208 unaffected.

- **UI Step 6 — final polish and state testing: complete. This is the last step of the UI plan — all six UI steps, and the position-model plan before them, are now done.**
  - **Audited what had actually been visually verified across Steps 2-5 versus just exercised via API/backend calls, and found four genuine gaps** — not assumed covered, checked directly: (1) **RUNNING** had never been screenshotted — every prior verification used `is_paused: true`; (2) **no currently-*open* position** (long or short) had ever been seeded, so Home's open-position row, Positions' open-position card, and Decision-detail's "linked position, still open" branch had never actually rendered with real shape; (3) **Home's dedicated stale/skipped/failed banner** (`RunStatusBanner`) had never fired for real — Step 4's seeded skip/fail runs were chronologically *older* than the real completed run, so `fetchLatestRun` never picked them up as "latest"; (4) a genuine **ErrorState from a real failure** had only ever been Step 1's static placeholder, never an actual broken query.
  - **All four closed with real, live verification, not just code review**: seeded an open LONG (BTC) and open SHORT (ETH) with real opening decisions, and a fresh stale-skip run more recent than any real one; temporarily flipped `is_paused` to `false`. Confirmed live: the header's pulsing-green RUNNING state and the correct button-set swap (Run now + Pause icon, vs. Resume when paused); Home's warning-bordered stale-cycle banner rendering for the first time; both open positions rendering correctly in Home, Positions (with correct SL-below-entry-for-long / SL-above-entry-for-short coloring), *and* Decision-detail's position-linkage section (clicked through from both); Decision-detail's SL/TP proposal and risk-gate sections rendering real non-null values for the first time (previously only ever seen for a HOLD, where both are correctly absent); and — genuinely useful — Decision-detail's technical/news-evidence *empty-state* branches (`parseEvidence` returning null/[] for the placeholder `{}` payload used in this seed) confirmed working too, not just their happy path.
  - **The real ErrorState test**: temporarily replaced the anon key in `src/supabase.ts` with a deliberately invalid string, confirmed all four tabs independently show their own correct "Could not load X" message with the *real* underlying error text and a working retry — not a shared/generic failure screen — then reverted and confirmed recovery (via Vite HMR re-invoking the same `refresh()` path a manual "Try again" click would).
  - **All 13 states from the brief now confirmed, either in this pass or a prior one**: RUNNING, PAUSED, HOLD, OPEN LONG, OPEN SHORT, CLOSE, SL, TP, RISK REJECTED (Step 4), STALE/SKIPPED (this step, Home's banner; Step 4, Activity's feed), ERROR (this step, real failure), NO POSITIONS (the system's actual current real state throughout this whole phase).
  - **Two real code-polish findings, not just visual ones** — the "final polish" half of this step: (1) the exact same four-line "controlled clock" pattern (`useState(() => Date.now())` + a 30s `setInterval`) had been copied identically into all five screens across Steps 2-5; extracted to `src/hooks/use-now.ts` (a new location for cross-cutting React hooks, distinct from each feature's own `use-*-data.ts`). (2) `src/features/home/format.ts` had accumulated dependents in all five screens despite living under (and being named for) `home/` specifically — moved to a plain top-level `src/format.ts`, matching `src/supabase.ts`'s own precedent for "one shared file every feature needs," rather than inventing a `utils/`/`lib/` directory convention for it (`src/lib/` remaining unusable for this, still owned by the `@/lib/*` alias per Step 1/2).
  - **A real false alarm caught and correctly diagnosed, not just dismissed**: after the refactor, a stale browser tab's *cumulative* console log showed a startling batch of 401s and a `useState is not defined` crash. Correctly attributed to the tab's own history — the 401s were remnants of the deliberate broken-key test above, and the crash was a transient Vite HMR hiccup from editing a mounted component's own hook imports live, not the final code — and *verified* that diagnosis rather than assumed it: closed the tab, opened a completely fresh one, clicked through all four tabs, confirmed zero console errors. The lesson generalizes beyond this step: a live browser tab's console history is cumulative across everything done in that session, not a clean signal for "does the current code work" — a fresh tab is the only reliable way to check.
  - Every seeded row deleted afterward (positions, decisions, trades, runs) and `is_paused` restored to `true`; the real 2-decision/0-position/0-trade state reconfirmed via direct query, matching Steps 4-5's own cleanup discipline exactly.
  - `npm run typecheck` / `build` / `lint` all clean (zero warnings); `deno test supabase/` 208/208 unaffected — this step touched only frontend code.

- **V0 execution mode: manual-only Run Agent (2026-09-19) — complete, deployed, live-verified. A separately-scoped task after the UI plan, not itself a numbered UI step.** Closes the Step 7 open action item exactly as flagged there ("unpausing and/or scheduling are both still open, explicit actions for the user to take when ready") — the user's resolution: schedule nothing, unpause for manual-only use. The decision cycle now runs exclusively from an explicit click; the position monitor is completely untouched and stays fully automatic.
  - **New migration** `20260919131706_manual_execution_mode.sql` — `update agent_settings set is_paused = false`, not an ad-hoc `db query --linked` UPDATE, per `ai-workflow-rules.md`'s Protected Files rule ("`supabase/migrations/*` once applied... except through a deliberate migration" — the mechanism for a durable config change is a new migration, not a one-off SQL command that leaves a fresh deploy's seed out of sync with live reality). `decision_interval_minutes` (180) deliberately untouched. Live-verified: `is_paused: false`, `decision_interval_minutes: 180` unchanged.
  - **CORS added to `agent-cycle/index.ts`'s `Deno.serve` wrapper only** — an OPTIONS preflight handler plus `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`/`Access-Control-Allow-Headers` on the response. Zero changes to `runAgentCycle` itself (the actual pipeline). Necessary because this function had only ever been invoked server-to-server before now (cron, or this session's own direct live-check scripts) — none of which are subject to CORS, a browser-only enforcement mechanism. `extension/manifest.json` has no `host_permissions` entry, so a real loaded extension gets no automatic CORS exemption either — this genuinely had to be handled in the function, not assumed away by "it's an extension." Confirmed live: a real browser-triggered call succeeded end-to-end (see below) — this was tested against the actual mechanism, not inferred from reading the code.
  - **`src/features/home/run-agent.ts` (new)** — `invokeAgentCycle()`, calling `supabase.functions.invoke('agent-cycle')` directly with the anon key. Per the user's explicit instruction, no separate `control` wrapper Edge Function was built (architecture.md's System Boundaries section still describes one as a future possibility — not built, not needed for this). Returns a hand-kept-in-sync mirror of the backend's `CycleSummary` shape, since `agent-cycle/index.ts` is Deno-only and can't be imported from Vite-built frontend code.
  - **Home screen redesigned around this** (`home-screen.tsx`): `PortfolioCard` loses its presentation-only Resume/Run-now/Pause cluster and its "Next cycle" stat (both replaced — no automatic cadence exists to count down to, and showing one would misrepresent manual-only mode, which is exactly what the user's brief calls out explicitly). New `AgentCard`: a "Manual" mode stat, "Last agent run" (real `agent_runs` data, already fetched), a real primary "Run agent" button, and a separate Position Monitor row (`StatusDot` pulse + real last-monitor-run time via `fetchLatestRun(portfolioId, 'monitor')` — reusing Step 5's already-generalized function, zero new backend/query code) — communicating both required facts from the brief (the agent only decides on request; the monitor still runs automatically) in one glance, not just in copy.
  - **A real, load-bearing discovery from testing, not assumed from reading the code**: `runAgentCycle`'s idempotency key floors to the **3-hour** `decision_interval_minutes` bucket regardless of trigger source — a second manual click inside the same window hits the existing `23505` unique-violation path and returns `{status: 'duplicate_tick'}`, a genuine no-op. Fixing the idempotency key itself would mean editing agent-cycle's core pipeline logic, out of this task's explicit "small, isolated configuration/scheduling change" scope — instead, the UI maps every one of the four real `CycleSummary` statuses (`completed`/`duplicate_tick`/`skipped`/`failed`) to an honest, correctly-toned inline message (`duplicate_tick` reads as calm information, not an error), and this file records the behavior so it's a known, deliberate consequence rather than a rediscovered surprise later.
  - **Two scope-boundary decisions made explicitly with the user (AskUserQuestion) before implementing**, not assumed either way: (1) the app header's live RUNNING/PAUSED pulse (`app-header.tsx`, derived from `is_paused`) would otherwise permanently read "RUNNING" the moment `is_paused` went `false` — the user chose to fix it too, now a static "MANUAL" badge with no live fetch (`App.tsx`'s separate `fetchAgentSettings()`/`isPaused` plumbing, whose only consumer this was, removed entirely as dead code). (2) Settings' own PAUSED/RUNNING badge, same underlying field — the user chose to update just the badge to match ("MANUAL"), explicitly leaving its Pause/Resume/Run-now buttons exactly as they already were (still presentation-only, unrelated fix).
  - **Full live end-to-end verification, not just code review**: clicked the real "Run agent" button in the Browser pane against the real deployed function — a genuine new `agent_runs` row (`kind: decision, status: completed`) and two new `agent_decisions` rows (BTC HOLD, ETH HOLD, both 70% confidence) appeared, the UI updated immediately via the post-run `refresh()` call ("LAST AGENT RUN: 1m ago", "Cycle complete — 2 decisions made." in success-green) without waiting for the next 30s poll — proving the CORS fix, the anon-key invocation, and the unpause all work together against the real system, not mocked. Clicked immediately a second time: confirmed the `duplicate_tick` path renders as designed ("Already ran for the current window — try again once it rolls over.", neutral tone). Re-confirmed via `cron.job` that still only `position-monitor-10min` exists — no `agent-cycle` schedule was created. Confirmed `supabase/functions/position-monitor/` has zero diff — untouched, as designed.
  - `npm run typecheck` / `build` / `lint` all clean (zero warnings); `deno test supabase/` 208/208 unaffected (only the `Deno.serve` transport wrapper changed, not `runAgentCycle`).

- **Trading Strategy V1 — evidence-led review and rewrite (2026-09-20): complete. Status PROPOSED, zero code changed.** The user asked for a critical redesign, not a documentation edit, of a new untracked `context/specs/trading-strategy-v1.md` whose actual strategy section (§4) was nine headers with no rules. Closes the NEWS/TECHNICAL methodology review `trading-domain-contract.md:7` has parked since Step 0 and this file has flagged three separate times.
  - **Repository audit found six factual conflicts with the live system** (a "1-hour decision horizon" when execution is manual-only and the data is a deliberate two-tier hourly/4-hourly mix; a described onboarding flow when none exists and the extension is structurally forbidden from writing; a backtest mandate when backtesting is excluded in three files; a model-output list naming a nonexistent "Thesis" field; shorts entirely unmentioned) **and two live implementation defects**: the 7-day-range indicator computes its window over the *current, in-progress* candle (pinning the metric at ≈0, unable to ever express a breakout), and no code anywhere drops the unclosed bar — live indicators are computed on partial data, which a bar-replay backtest could not reproduce, and `distanceFromSevenDayHighPct` additionally mixes live spot against historical candles. Neither defect was fixed (out of scope — this is a specification review, not an implementation task) but both are now documented as preconditions for implementing anything in the new spec.
  - **Three research agents ran genuine external literature review** (WebSearch/WebFetch against peer-reviewed finance journals, NBER/SSRN working papers, and arXiv — not general knowledge) across crypto momentum evidence, volatility/stop-loss/risk-management evidence, and transaction-cost/news/LLM/backtest-validity evidence. This is the first time this project has done external academic research as part of a spec, rather than only reading its own code and docs.
  - **The headline finding reversed the reviewer's own first design.** An initial proposal (a 7-day-breakout entry checked every 3 hours) was drafted, then **withdrawn** after the momentum-research agent returned three independent findings against it: De Nicola (*Ledger* 2021) measures BTC's own first-order return autocorrelation as significantly *negative* at 1h/2h/4h and insignificant at daily; a 2026 *JRFM* study that ran an exhaustive momentum-vs-reversal search on exactly BTC+ETH hourly data 2016-2025 found reversal won (and even that failed bootstrap significance); and Hudson & Urquhart (*Annals of Operations Research* 2021) tested ~15,000 technical rules with multiple-testing correction and found no out-of-sample predictability for Bitcoin specifically. Self-correcting a design already presented to the user, on evidence found after presenting it, rather than defending it or quietly keeping the withdrawal to itself.
  - **A second, independently-discovered finding: the strategy's two mandatory exit rules were optimal under opposite market conditions.** Kaminski & Lo's stop-loss theory (*Journal of Financial Markets* 2014) shows a stop only adds expected value when the return series' autocorrelation exceeds its own Sharpe ratio at the same sampling frequency — i.e. under momentum; under a random walk the "stopping premium" is proven strictly negative. Dao et al.'s trend-convexity paper shows a trend-following strategy's payoff is quadratic in the cumulative move, and that positive skew is a right tail a fixed take-profit removes. Since BTC measures mean-reverting (ρ<0) at 1-4h, the stop-loss and the take-profit wanted opposite regimes — a real, previously-undocumented internal inconsistency, not a matter of finding the right numbers for either one.
  - **The redesign resolves this by moving the entire signal horizon**, not by re-tuning either exit rule: a single 50-day daily-close-vs-moving-average regime state (the empirically-significant band per Detzel, Liu, Strauss, Zhou & Zhu, *Financial Management* 2021 — 5-day and 10-day lookbacks were tested and were *not* significant) replaces the withdrawn 7-day breakout, RSI/MACD/volume-ratio/hourly-EMAs/7-day-range are dropped from the decision entirely (feature importance ≈0 in the ML literature cited, and mechanically near-collinear with price÷MA), the stop is explicitly reframed as a priced catastrophic-risk constraint rather than an alpha source, and the take-profit widens to a ~6R provisional backstop (with a mandatory diagnostic — the fraction of winning trades that touch it before the real exit, the daily regime flip, fires) so the trend's right tail isn't truncated by the very rule meant to protect against it.
  - **The Gemini confidence gate is removed, not just demoted.** Verbalized LLM confidence in the literature reviewed shows AUROC 0.55-0.61 (near-zero ability to rank correct above incorrect predictions) and expected calibration error of 0.24-0.47 that provably cannot be fixed by calibration (monotone recalibration cannot reorder predictions, so it cannot fix a *discrimination* problem) — and in the one study of exactly this use case, 0 of 18 gate configurations survived multiple-testing correction. Gemini's role narrows to a binary veto on a named exogenous-confound question plus writing the audit-trail thesis text; confidence is still requested and logged, but only to eventually build the reliability curve that would justify trusting it, which does not exist yet.
  - **News is reframed from confirm-or-veto to veto-only**, and market commentary explicitly carries zero weight (distinct from genuinely exogenous categories like a hack or a regulatory ruling) — a 12-year, ~64,000-headline analysis found no Granger-causal relationship between crypto news volume and BTC returns at any lag and found price moving *before* the correlated news coverage, not after, which the review treats as evidence that most RSS content restates a chart move already visible to the technical layer rather than supplying independent information.
  - **A genuine risk-appetite finding, found by arithmetic the reviewer ran directly, not sourced from the literature**: since position size is `min(riskBudget/stopDistance, 20%-of-NAV cap)`, and the 20% cap binds below `budget/0.20` for every appetite tier, all three shipped risk-appetite tiers (0.75%/1.00%/1.50%) produce an *identical* position size at every stop distance realistic for this asset class — the risk-appetite dial currently does almost nothing to position size, only to the (now-removed) confidence threshold. Recalibrated budgets (0.25%/0.50%/0.75%) are proposed, restoring genuine separation, verified by direct computation across a stop-distance table in both configurations.
  - **A user-facing critique session, incorporated as eight explicit changes, not deflected or minimized**: presented with a first evidence-led draft, the user identified — correctly, on inspection — that "the only defensible version" overstated the case (the underlying null applies to price-vs-MA rules too, not just what was withdrawn), that a "20-100 day band" is not itself an executable parameter, that a proposed 1% ATR volatility floor had a cost-based justification that silently assumed an hours-long hold and did not survive the redesign to weeks-long holds, that ATR-scaling the stop conflated a sizing property with thesis invalidation, that the 6R take-profit needed a stated fraction-of-trades-that-touch-it diagnostic rather than being asserted as correct, that the news/LLM layer should be genuinely willing to conclude it adds nothing, that "beat buy-and-hold" is the wrong success gate for a strategy that might reasonably trade return for lower drawdown, and that a correlation-based portfolio cap should be a risk-at-stop ceiling instead (correlation-free, since correlation is time-varying and risk-at-stop is already known exactly per position). Verified the removed volatility floor's cost math directly before removing it — at a 3-week hold even a low-volatility asset's expected move dwarfs the round-trip cost, confirming the floor's rationale really had evaporated, not just accepting the critique's word for it.
  - **A five-hypothesis falsification hierarchy (H1-H5)** replaces "backtest, then tune until it looks good": each hypothesis (the regime signal has information; volatility-scaled sizing helps; the wide-backstop exit beats a finite one; the news veto adds value; Gemini's veto adds value beyond the deterministic news rule) is tested in order and gates the next, with an explicit, pre-registered retirement condition for each — including the outright removal of the LLM from the trading path as one_of_five possible honest outcomes, not a foregone "the AI must stay" conclusion.
  - **Explicitly documented as unresolved rather than silently assumed**: whether BTC's volatility has a conventional or *inverse* leverage effect (directly determines whether volatility-scaled sizing helps at all, or whether it de-risks into rallies) — the literature reviewed found credible papers on both sides; whether the crypto return distribution's tail exponent is below 3 (in which case variance, and therefore every Sharpe-ratio-based comparison anywhere in this document, may not be a well-defined statistic); and that this whole exercise cannot produce a validated edge on any realistic timeline (~216 trades needed to distinguish a plausible edge from chance at conventional significance, against an expected 6-20 trades per asset per year under the proposed design) — success is redefined as behavioral correctness and hypothesis-by-hypothesis evidence, not a Sharpe ratio.
  - No code, schema, prompt, or `agent_settings` value changed. `npm run typecheck` / `build` / `lint` all clean; `deno test supabase/` 208/208 unaffected — confirmed explicitly as proof this stayed a specification exercise.
- **Trading Strategy V1 implementation (2026-09-21): Phases 0-7 of the approved plan complete, tested, and live-verified. Phase 8 (deploy) is the one remaining step — privileged infrastructure, the user's action, not mine (see Next Up).** Followed the plan's own binding gap-analysis-first, minimum-delta, verify-at-every-phase discipline; three clarifying questions were resolved via AskUserQuestion before implementation started (first activation is state-based, not crossover-based; post-stop-out re-entry is allowed once the block expires; risk appetite/balance stay migration-configured, no new control endpoint).
  - **Phase 0 live probes, before any code**: confirmed CoinGecko `/market_chart` (any `days=`, at 5-min/hourly/daily granularity) always appends exactly one off-grid "live spot" point after its regular series — 2-3 minutes old, gap always shorter than the series' own interval — while `/ohlc` has no such defect (candles are genuinely closed by construction; confirmed 180 candles at `days=30`, every single gap exactly 4.0h). This refined the plan's own sketched fix: a much simpler "compare the last two gaps" rule (`strategy/closed-bars.ts`'s `closedPoints`) applies only to `closeSeries`/`volumeSeries`/`dailyCloseSeries`, never to `candles` — a real, evidence-based deviation from the approved plan's literal Phase-1 description, surfaced to the user at the time rather than silently implemented differently.
  - **D1 (the partial-bar defect `trading-strategy-v1.md` §0 flagged as a precondition) is fixed, and incidentally fixed for the pre-existing hourly indicators too** — `closedPoints` is applied at the CoinGecko adapter boundary (`providers/coingecko.ts`) to every `market_chart`-sourced series, not just the new daily one, so RSI/EMA/MACD/volume-ratio (which already read `closeSeries`/`volumeSeries`) now also exclude the in-progress bar. This was a real, live bug predating V1 entirely, fixed as a side effect of building the closed-bar module correctly rather than narrowly.
  - **New pure strategy modules** (`supabase/functions/agent-cycle/strategy/`): `regime.ts` (`evaluateTrendRegime` — 50-day SMA, frozen `TREND_MA_LOOKBACK_DAYS` constant, strict `daily_close > SMA50` — equal counts as DOWN, deliberately), `rules.ts` (`stopLossPctFor` = max(2×ATR%, 2.5%) with the ATR%-is-a-percentage-not-a-fraction unit conversion guarded by a dedicated test; `takeProfitPctFor` = 6×stop; `buildCandidateProposal` — the full FLAT/LONG/SHORT × UP/DOWN state machine, exhaustively tested to never return OPEN_SHORT; `vetoedHoldProposal` — added during the Phase 5 rewrite, converts a vetoed or fail-closed OPEN_LONG candidate to HOLD). `src/shared/strategy/types.ts` carries the dual-runtime `TrendRegime`/`RegimeResult` types.
  - **Risk retuning**: `appetite-mapping.ts`'s three tiers now `{0.25%, 0.50%, 0.75%}` risk budget with `minConfidence: 0` across all three (confidence no longer gates — `effectiveMinConfidence: 0` is stamped honestly onto every decision rather than the column being silently repurposed). Two migrations (`20260921060026_trading_strategy_v1.sql`, `20260921061530_agent_decisions_portfolio_risk_effective.sql` — a second, small follow-up rather than editing the first once a gap was found immediately after pushing, per `ai-workflow-rules.md` Protected Files): `max_take_profit_pct` raised 0.50→0.90 (blocker B4 — a 6R take-profit silently rejected every open above ~4.17% ATR under the old ceiling); new `portfolio_risk_ceiling_multiplier` (1.50), `max_total_notional_pct` (0.30), `drawdown_breaker_floor_pct` (0.90), `news_veto_enabled` (true); new `agent_decisions.strategy_version`/`model_vetoed` columns (backfilled `'v0-gemini-originated'`/`null` for the 2 pre-V1 rows) and `effective_portfolio_risk_ceiling_pct`/`effective_max_total_notional_pct`. Both applied via `supabase db push --linked` and live-verified over the REST API, same standard as every prior migration.
  - **Portfolio risk is a genuinely new cross-asset mechanism, not a repair of the pre-existing (and correctly inert) per-asset caps.** Confirmed by re-reading `sizing.ts`'s own doc comment mid-implementation that `currentAssetExposureUsd`/`maxAssetExposurePct` are permanently, correctly 0/inert in V0/V1 — one net position per asset means "per-asset" and "per-trade" are the same number by construction — and told the user this corrected the approved plan's own gap-analysis table, which had mischaracterized it as something to repair. Built instead: `sizing.ts`'s `applySizingCaps` gained two new min-of-N candidates (`portfolio_risk`, `total_notional`); `build-context.ts`'s new `aggregateOtherOpenPositionsRisk` computes both cross-asset aggregates fresh per gate call from live open-position state (risk-at-stop = `quantity × |entryPrice − stopLossPrice|`, the exact dollar figure, not a re-derived percentage; same-direction notional filtered to `direction === 'long'` explicitly, since V1 is long-only but a legacy short must never silently count toward the long-side cap); `gate.ts` gained a drawdown breaker (`nav < peakNav × drawdownBreakerFloorPct`, OPEN-only, `peakNav > 0` guards a fresh portfolio) checked immediately before sizing.
  - **Gemini demoted to binary veto-only** (`model/{prompt,gemini-schema,call-model}.ts`, `model/payload.ts`'s new `VetoCallPayload`/`VetoCandidateInput` — all fully replaced, not left dormant alongside the old decision-originating versions, per this codebase's established "replace, don't leave dead" precedent). New `VETO_PROMPT_VERSION = 'v3-regime-veto'`; the prompt asks exactly one question per candidate ("is there a known exogenous confound?"), with an explicit "do NOT veto because..." list guarding against the model re-litigating the trend or treating price-commentary news as new information, and a "default to NOT vetoing" instruction. `agent_decisions.input_payload` deliberately keeps the full, rich `ModelCallPayload` shape (not the narrower veto payload actually sent) — it's the complete audit-trail record the regime rule itself used, and the existing Decision-detail UI already reads it, so this needed zero UI changes.
  - **`index.ts` rewired into the two-pass structure the plan specified** — the biggest single edit. Pass 1 (per asset, no I/O beyond reads, no model call): build `AssetInput` including `regime`, evaluate the candidate via `buildCandidateProposal`. Collect every OPEN_LONG candidate across both assets; zero candidates makes zero model calls, one or more makes exactly one batched `callModel` call. Pass 2 (per asset, in order — later assets still see earlier ones' executed cash/position effects, same as the single-pass loop this replaced): apply the veto verdict (if any), then proceed through the **entirely unchanged** `buildRiskGateContext` → `evaluateRiskGate` → `planDecisionExecution` → atomic RPCs → persistence path.
  - **Fail-closed semantics for the veto step, decided during implementation (the approved plan named the requirement — "Gemini failure ⇒ fail closed to HOLD" — but not the exact persisted representation)**: a veto-call failure, or a news-provider failure that the veto step depended on (only when `news_veto_enabled` is true — a feed outage does not block trading when veto is deliberately switched off), converts every OPEN_LONG candidate that cycle to a defensive HOLD via `vetoedHoldProposal`, with the failure reason embedded in the persisted `reasons` text. `agent_decisions.model_vetoed` is recorded `null` for this case (not `true`) — reasoned that a null-meaning-"no completed model verdict this cycle" reading is more honest than `true`, and keeps an infrastructure failure from inflating the H4/H5 veto-rate metrics `strategy_version`/`model_vetoed` exist to make queryable. `model_version`/`output_payload` use explicit `'not-called'`/`'call-failed'`/`null` sentinels rather than fabricated values; `prompt_version` is always the real `VETO_PROMPT_VERSION` (a compile-time constant, true regardless of whether a call happened). Documented here since it's a real interpretation of an underspecified corner, not just an implementation detail.
  - **UI (Phase 7, P0 only — P1 regime/veto/portfolio-risk surfacing explicitly deferred, per the plan)**: Settings' "MIN CONFIDENCE" stat (`src/features/settings/settings-screen.tsx`) relabeled to "CONFIDENCE GATE" / "Not used in V1" — the old label would have shown a stray `0.00%` that reads as a live threshold when confidence no longer gates anything. `decision-detail-screen.tsx`'s own `MIN CONFIDENCE` stat was deliberately left untouched — it's a historical per-decision record of what `effectiveMinConfidence` actually was for that row, still factually accurate. **Live-verified in the browser against the real deployed Supabase backend** (not a mock): the Settings screen correctly renders `0.50%` risk budget and `0.50% – 90.00%` take-profit range for the BALANCED tier, confirming the migrated V1 values are genuinely live, alongside the corrected confidence-gate label.
  - **Every context file describing the old Gemini-originates flow was updated to match what's actually built**, per `CLAUDE.md`'s own "update the relevant context file before implementing the dependent change" rule — not left stale the way a smaller change might get away with: `CLAUDE.md` (AI-specific rules rewritten for veto-only; the `PROPOSED`→implemented status line; the flow diagram; the "LLM proposes" architecture-rules bullet marked superseded in place, not silently reworded, so the historical claim stays visible); `architecture.md` (Agent Cycle's 14 steps rewritten to the two-pass structure; Model Boundary rewritten; Risk Gate's confidence bullet marked superseded and the two new portfolio caps + drawdown breaker added to its list; Scheduling's "full Gemini decision loop" phrase fixed); `project-overview.md` (Autonomous Agent feature bullets rewritten). `trading-domain-contract.md` needed no change — the position/action vocabulary and execution semantics it owns didn't change in V1, only decision origination did.
  - **Full verification, every phase**: `deno check` clean across every modified/new file including both manual-only `.live-check.ts` scripts (never part of `deno test`, checked separately since they're the one thing that could silently rot); `deno test supabase/` **263/263 passing**, zero regressions; `npm run typecheck` (`tsc -b`), `npm run lint` (`oxlint`), and `npm run build` all clean; the Settings-screen fix additionally verified live in-browser (screenshot + console/network check, no errors attributable to the change).
  - **Minimum-delta held, verified by name**: `broker/accounting.ts`, the entire `position-monitor/` function, `cycle/plan-decision.ts`, `cycle/decision-record.ts`, `providers/rss-news.ts`, `src/shared/risk/sl-tp.ts`, both atomic RPCs, and every non-Settings UI screen are untouched by this implementation — everything downstream of the risk gate's output never needed to know a proposal was synthesized deterministically rather than model-generated.
  - Self-caught during implementation, no user involvement needed: a `sizing.test.ts` arithmetic error (a first draft assumed the single-trade cap was $20,000; it's `nav × 0.20 = $2,000` — caught by the test failing, independently recomputed before rewriting).
- **Independent market-quote refresh (2026-09-22): complete, deployed, live-verified.** After V1 went live, the user reported BTC/ETH prices in the extension were frozen between manual agent runs — confirmed live before touching anything: the newest `market_snapshots` row was 30 minutes old, the pair before it nearly two days old. Root cause: `agent-cycle` only fetches prices on a manual click (by design) and `position-monitor` only fetches prices while a position is open (by design) — no process existed whose job was keeping a *display* quote fresh. A user-specified constraint shaped the whole design: fix this without enabling the 3-hour agent schedule, without letting refresh trigger trading, and without touching the V1 strategy.
  - **New `market_quotes` table**, not a reuse of `market_snapshots` — that table's `run_id` is `not null references agent_runs`, and `indicators`/`recent_closes` are both `jsonb not null`, so a quote-only write would either fabricate an `agent_runs` row every 5 minutes (polluting the Activity screen) or insert fake indicator payloads (exactly the stale/partial-indicator corruption to avoid). `market_snapshots` keeps its one existing job — the immutable per-decision audit trail the strategy and Decision-detail UI read — and gains no new writer or reader. `market_quotes` is a 2-row table (one per asset), upserted, with `last_refresh_error`/`last_refresh_error_at` cleared on success and set (only) on failure — a failed refresh never touches the price columns, so a CoinGecko outage degrades to "the quote stops advancing," never a fabricated or blanked price. No freshness/staleness UI was added (explicit user decision) — `data_as_of` is fetched but stays unrendered, same as it already was before this fix.
  - **New Edge Function `market-refresh`** — deliberately the most restricted of the three: it imports only the CoinGecko provider and the row mappers, never the strategy, risk gate, broker, or either atomic RPC, and touches no table but `market_quotes`. Confirmed structurally at deploy time (the bundled-asset list Supabase printed pulled in only `coingecko.ts`, `quote-rows.ts`, and their own transitive dependencies — nothing from `model/`, `strategy/`, `risk/`, or `broker/`) and confirmed behaviorally live (`agent_decisions`/`trades`/`agent_runs` row counts were checked identical across three separate manual invocations). No `agent_runs` row and no idempotency key, unlike the other two functions — a duplicate/retried refresh just upserts the same 2 rows again, harmlessly; invariant 10 protects against duplicate *trades*, and there is nothing here to duplicate.
  - **Provider reuse, no duplicated HTTP**: `coingecko.ts`'s low-level helpers (`fetchJson`, `parseOrThrow`, `COIN_ID`, `BASE_URL`) were already module-private by design (`COIN_ID`'s own comment: the coin-id scheme "must never leak past this adapter"), so the new quote path had to live inside that same file — a third standalone exported function alongside `getMarketData`/`fetchRecentPricePoints`, following the same `(assets, fetchImpl?, baseUrl?)` shape as the latter. One small extraction was needed to avoid duplicating the `/coins/markets` URL-building: pulled into a shared private `fetchMarketsSummary`, guarded by the 8 pre-existing `getMarketData` tests (all still passed unchanged, proving the refactor was behavior-preserving before any new code was added on top). Cost: **one** CoinGecko request per refresh, versus `getMarketData`'s `1 + 3N` — confirmed by an explicit call-count assertion in the new tests, not just claimed.
  - **`MarketQuote` (src/shared/market-data/types.ts) is built via Zod's `.pick()` off `NormalizedMarketData`**, not a separately hand-typed schema — makes "MarketQuote is a structural subset" a property Zod enforces rather than a convention two schemas happen to follow, so `agent-cycle`'s own already-fetched `NormalizedMarketData` can be passed straight into the same `toQuoteRow` mapper `market-refresh` uses, with no conversion step (verified directly: a test passes a full `NormalizedMarketData` fixture into `toQuoteRow` and confirms only the 8 relevant fields are read).
  - **`agent-cycle` also upserts `market_quotes` on every manual run**, immediately after its own `getMarketData` call and *before* the freshness gate — zero extra CoinGecko requests (it already has the data), and a cycle the gate is about to skip for staleness still updates the display quote, since fail-closed governs whether that cycle *trades*, not whether the UI shows the price CoinGecko actually just returned.
  - **Cadence: 5 minutes, not the ~1 minute first proposed** — explicit user choice, trading marginally fresher quotes (the extension's own 30s poll already surfaces whatever lands quickly) for meaningfully lower sustained CoinGecko volume (288 requests/day instead of 1,440).
  - **UI change is exactly one file**: `src/features/market-data/queries.ts`'s `fetchLatestMarketPrices` — the single chokepoint all three consumers (Home, Positions, Decision-detail) and all five price-rendering call sites already went through — now selects from `market_quotes` with a real `.in('asset', assets)` filter instead of an unfiltered `order + limit(assets.length * 5) + first-per-asset-wins` workaround (a latent bug fixed as a side effect: that old query could silently drop an asset entirely if the other one had accumulated more than 5 newer rows). Zero component, hook, or polling-loop changes — all four independent `REFRESH_INTERVAL_MS = 30_000` loops are untouched.
  - **Live-verified end-to-end in the browser, without a manual Run Agent**: invoked `market-refresh` directly (see the Vault-secret finding below for why manual `curl` was used instead of the cron path), confirmed `market_quotes.fetched_at` advanced, then reloaded the extension and watched Positions' MARK values change to exactly the new prices ($85,271.00 BTC / $2,722.42 ETH) purely from the UI's existing 30s poll.
  - **Found and reported, not fixed silently — a serious, unrelated live incident surfaced by this work**: manually invoking `market-refresh` failed with `401 UNAUTHORIZED_INVALID_JWT_FORMAT`, traced to the Vault's `service_role_key` secret holding a 23-character placeholder instead of the real key (see Current Phase's CRITICAL entry for full detail and impact — it has been silently failing `position-monitor-10min` since the secret was created, leaving V1's two live open positions unmonitored for SL/TP). Verified my own function's correctness independently of this by invoking it directly with the public anon key over HTTPS (a valid, non-privileged JWT the gateway accepts) rather than waiting on the fix.
  - Offline-validated before touching the live database, same discipline as every prior migration: the new migration's SQL was parsed with real Postgres grammar (`pglast`) before `db push` — 9 statements, clean.
  - `deno test supabase/` **277/277 passing** (21 coingecko + 7 quote-rows are new, both suites otherwise unchanged); `deno check` clean including the new function; `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean. Migration applied and function deployed via `supabase db push --linked` / `supabase functions deploy market-refresh` (user's explicit choice this session: privileged infra run directly, not handed off).
- **Bug fix: `agent_decisions.output_payload` NOT NULL blocked every HOLD/CLOSE manual run (2026-09-22).** User-reported via `/debug`: `could not insert agent_decisions for BTC: null value in column "output_payload" ... violates not-null constraint`. Root cause: the column was `NOT NULL` since V0's very first migration, correct at the time (every decision genuinely called Gemini, always had real output) — Trading Strategy V1's Phase 5 rewrite introduced decisions that legitimately never call the model at all (HOLD/CLOSE never reach the veto step) and persists that honestly as `output_payload: null`, the exact same "no completed model interaction" meaning `model_vetoed`'s nullability already carries — but the column itself was never relaxed to match. Since HOLD/CLOSE is the common outcome most cycles, this broke nearly every manual run since V1 deployed. Fixed with `20260922065804_agent_decisions_output_payload_nullable.sql` (`alter column output_payload drop not null`). Confirmed root cause via the live `agent_runs.error_detail` on the actual failed rows (character-for-character match, seen twice: 2026-09-21 16:00 and 2026-09-22 06:56); confirmed the fix is safe by grepping all of `src/` for any UI code reading `output_payload` — none exists. `input_payload` deliberately stays `NOT NULL` — `agent-cycle` always constructs a real payload per asset regardless of whether a model call happened, so it is genuinely never null.
- **Manual/scheduled idempotency split (2026-09-22): complete, deployed, live-verified.** User-reported: a single manual "Run agent" click claimed the entire 3-hour `decision_interval_minutes` bucket (the idempotency key floors to it regardless of trigger source), so every further deliberate click that session returned `duplicate_tick` — confirmed live before touching anything (a failed 06:56 run had claimed the `06:00–09:00` bucket; the next window didn't open until 09:00).
  - **Root cause, precisely:** one key was doing two unrelated jobs — tick dedup (needs a deterministic key derived from a scheduled tick's identity) and concurrency exclusion (needs a real mutex). For a manual click there is no tick, so tick dedup is meaningless, yet the code applied it anyway. The deeper issue found while diagnosing: concurrency exclusion was never actually provided either — the bucketed key only excluded invocations landing in the *same* bucket, never stopped two invocations straddling a boundary from overlapping — even though `open_position_atomic`'s own migration comment asserts outright that "agent_runs idempotency prevents overlapping decision cycles." That assumption was only ever approximately true; simply making manual keys unique without adding a real mutex would have made it actively false, turning a genuine concurrent double-open into a raw, confusing `23505` from `positions_one_open_per_asset_idx` instead of a clean no-op.
  - **New pure module** `supabase/functions/agent-cycle/cycle/idempotency.ts` — `floorToIntervalIso` moved here from `index.ts` (previously private, untested, now covered), plus new `parseTrigger` (fail-safe defaults to `'scheduled'`, the *more* restrictive semantics, on any absent/malformed/unrecognized body — never the reverse), `buildDecisionIdempotencyKey` (scheduled: unchanged bucketed format, guarded by a literal-string regression test; manual: millisecond-precision per-click key), `staleRunCutoffIso` (10-minute threshold), and `classifyRunInsertConflict` (distinguishes a genuine mutex collision from an idempotency-key collision on the same `23505` code, by constraint name with a trigger-based fallback). 17 new tests.
  - **New mutex, not just a wider key** — `agent_runs_one_running_decision_idx`, a partial unique index on `(kind) where status='running' and kind='decision'` (`20260922075501_agent_runs_running_mutex.sql`). Deliberately scoped to `kind='decision'` only, never `monitor` — a single wedged monitor row would silently block all future SL/TP monitoring, precisely the class of invisible failure the `service_role_key` incident (Current Phase) just caused; `position-monitor` needs no mutex regardless, since `close_position_atomic` is already fully race-safe via its own `won_race` return. The same migration corrects `open_position_atomic`'s comment to name this index as the actual mechanism, in place of the no-longer-quite-true claim it replaces.
  - **Stale-run reaper**, immediately before the mutex-guarded insert: any `kind='decision', status='running'` row older than 10 minutes (an Edge Function crash/timeout before its own `catch` block could run) is marked `failed` first, so a single abandoned row can never permanently wedge every future manual click behind a run that will never complete. Idempotent under a race — two requests reaping the same stale row both just no-op past the already-updated row.
  - **New `already_running` status**, distinct from `duplicate_tick` from here on: `duplicate_tick` means "this scheduled bucket was already handled" (now realistically only reachable by a future scheduled caller, since manual clicks get their own key); `already_running` means "a different cycle is in flight right now." `CycleSummary`, `AgentCycleRunResult` (`src/features/home/run-agent.ts`), and `describeRunResult`'s switch (`home-screen.tsx`, TypeScript-exhaustive) all thread the new value through.
  - **Also fixed in the same pass, flagged before implementing rather than silently bundled**: `agent_runs.completed_at` was being set from the same `nowIso` captured at request start, so every run recorded an artifactual exactly-0.00s duration — confirmed across all 16 live rows before fixing.   - **The veto is now a calibrated probability (Jev's "noul", 0-1), not a boolean.** No threshold existed anywhere pre-migration (Gemini's own schema was a bare `veto: BOOLEAN`), so `JEV_VETO_THRESHOLD = 0.70` (`model/jev/question.ts`) is a genuinely new decision surface, shipped explicitly **provisional** — never described as validated, in code, in this file, or in the commit message. **The raw `noul` is persisted on every model-assisted decision** (`agent_decisions.output_payload.noul`, alongside the derived boolean) specifically so the threshold can be revisited later as a SQL query over real accumulated observations, not by replaying decisions through the API. Two properties shaped the design directly: questions are "evaluated in parallel and in isolation" (→ one shared `state`, keyed by asset, plus N independent per-asset `noul` questions in ONE request, rather than either N separate requests or one prose question blending assets); `noul=0.5` means genuine ambiguity, not a neutral midpoint (→ the threshold's only justification is directional, never described as tuned).

- **Phase 2 — "Jev as a portfolio-management decision layer" (2026-09-22/23): code-complete, tested (415/415), NOT YET DEPLOYED.** Expands Jev from a pure entry-veto into managing existing BTC/ETH positions — `HOLD`/`ADD`/`REDUCE`/`CLOSE`/`MODIFY_PROTECTION` — while manual-only execution, the 180-minute cadence, one-net-position-per-asset, and the deterministic V1 regime strategy's authority over entry eligibility all stay exactly as they were. Planned via the same rigorous cycle as the Jev migration itself: a repository audit *before* any design (three parallel Explore agents mapping the position model, risk gate, and Jev/cycle wiring first-hand), a plan draft, then four independent user corrections during `ExitPlanMode` review — two of them genuine correctness defects, not preferences (see below) — applied before implementation began.
  - **The audit surfaced the design-shaping facts, not assumptions:** `agent_decisions_run_asset_unique` + `trades_decision_unique` already guarantee "one action per asset per run" for free — the plan's own worry about an `ADD → REDUCE → ADD` sequence within one cycle was structurally impossible before a line of Phase 2 code existed. Four separate DB objects blocked partial exits, not just "unimplemented": `trades_one_close_per_position_idx` (a genuine UNIQUE index — at most one `CLOSE_*` trade per position, ever, which is why `REDUCE` needed its own `ADD_LONG`/`ADD_SHORT`/`REDUCE_LONG`/`REDUCE_SHORT` intents rather than reusing `CLOSE_*`), `positions_closed_fields` (forbids `realized_pnl` on an open position — nowhere for a partial realization to live), `positions_qty_positive`, and `positions_sl_tp_ordering_valid` (defined relative to `entry_price`, so an ADD moving the weighted entry can silently invalidate existing protection unless re-checked). Most load-bearing: `gate.ts:111` used to derive direction via `proposal.action === 'OPEN_LONG' ? 'long' : 'short'` immediately after the HOLD/CLOSE branches — any new action reaching that line would have been silently mis-signed as a short open. This was fixed *before* any new action literal existed, and the gate's dispatch is now an exhaustive per-action switch ending in a `const exhaustive: never = proposal` compile-time check — the identical fix was independently needed and applied in `cycle/plan-decision.ts`, which had the same trap at its own `OPEN_LONG ? 'long' : 'short'` line.
  - **Four user corrections during plan review, applied before implementation — two were real defects, not style preferences:**
    1. **Realized-P&L contradiction (correctness bug in the draft plan):** the plan introduced `trades.realized_pnl` as the source of lifetime P&L, sum'd across a position's whole lifecycle, but said `close_position_atomic` stays "reused unchanged" — which would silently omit every full close from that sum, under-reporting lifetime P&L. Fixed by modifying `close_position_atomic` minimally (`p_realized_pnl` was already a parameter — it now also lands in the trade insert's column list) via `create or replace function`, with **no signature change**, so both existing callers (`agent-cycle`, `position-monitor`) needed zero changes.
    2. **Blanket confidence-gating was unsafe, not just unvalidated:** an earlier draft normalized any Choice/Score answer below 0.50 confidence to `HOLD`. The counterexample that killed it: `Jev → CLOSE, confidence 0.47` normalized to `HOLD` keeps risk *on* precisely when the model leans toward removing it — "HOLD is safe" is not universally true across five actions with different risk polarities. Resolved to **no confidence gating anywhere in v1** — persist `actionConfidence` and the full probability distribution on every decision (exactly like `noul`), gate on nothing, and let a future task-specific gate (if any) be chosen from real observed data.
    3. **SL/TP intent formulas were underspecified.** `TIGHTEN_TO_STRUCTURE` was cut entirely from v1 rather than invented on the spot — "structure" has no defined meaning in this codebase (swing low? EMA? ATR offset?) and no such signal is computed anywhere; the SL action space is exactly `KEEP | TIGHTEN_TO_BREAKEVEN`. `MOVE_CLOSER`/`MOVE_OUT` got an exact, pre-specified formula instead of being left to implementation: one ATR-in-price-terms step (`TP_STEP_ATR_MULTIPLE = 1.0`, provisional, versioned into `MANAGEMENT_QUESTION_VERSION`), reusing the same `atrPct ÷ 100` unit-conversion discipline (percentage-as-number vs. fraction) `strategy/rules.ts`'s `stopLossPctFor` already established.
    4. **The sizing-boundary claim was stated too absolutely.** "Jev must not decide sizing" is false of Phase 2 — an ADD magnitude genuinely is a sizing signal. Corrected to the precise, defensible version now in `CLAUDE.md`/`architecture.md`: *"Jev may choose a bounded adjustment magnitude; deterministic code remains the sole authority over final size."* Jev picks a point on a code-defined scale (0.25/0.50/1.00 for ADD, 0.25/0.50/0.75 for REDUCE — deliberately never reaching 1.00, since a full reduce is a `CLOSE`, normalized upstream, never executed as a 100% `REDUCE`); code multiplies it against a risk-derived maximum computed from the position's *existing* stop, then applies every cap on top. Jev never names a dollar figure and can only ever request less than the deterministic ceiling.
  - **`src/shared/risk/gate.ts` — the direction-trap fix plus four new evaluation branches, all independently tested (47/47 in `gate.test.ts`, including 20 new Phase 2 cases with hand-verified numbers).** New `OpenPositionSnapshot` context field (the position's own entry/stop/target/quantity, distinct from `entryPrice`, which has meant "current market price" since before Phase 2 existed). `evaluateAdd`: risk-derived headroom computed against the *existing* stop via the same `deriveRiskBasedNotional` the fresh-OPEN path already calls; the existing SL/TP are re-validated against the new *weighted* entry via a new `validateAbsoluteProtection` (`sl-tp.ts`) — a live-verified rejection case exists where a price pump (100→300) alone, with no change to the absolute stop, pushes the *percentage* stop distance from 10% to exactly 17.5% (independently computed: `210/1200 = 0.175`), above `maxStopLossPct` — correctly rejected, SL/TP never silently moved. `evaluateReduce`: floored (below `min_trade_notional_pct × NAV` → `not_applicable`, never `rejected` — see below) and ceilinged (`Math.min(magnitude × quantity, quantity)`, a defensive backstop for the upstream 100%→CLOSE normalization), never capped upward. `evaluateModifyProtection`: a stop may only tighten (long: only up; short: only down) — any widen is rejected outright, existing stop preserved; a stop/target landing at-or-through the current price is rejected as a disguised CLOSE. **A genuinely new, deliberately reused vocabulary decision**: an ADD/REDUCE below the minimum notional returns `riskStatus: 'not_applicable'`, *not* `'rejected'` — this is what lets `index.ts` normalize the persisted action back to the original HOLD candidate using the *same* gate result, with no second gate call, since `not_applicable` already means exactly what a HOLD's own risk status means.
  - **Pre-existing hazard found and fixed in the same pass, not introduced by Phase 2:** the cash sizing cap always passed raw `cash` as the affordable notional, but `netCashDelta = -(grossValue + fee)` is strictly larger than the requested notional once slippage and fees are added — a cash-bound trade could in principle drive `cash_after` negative and throw on `trades_cash_after_nonneg`. Fixed via a new `affordableNotionalUsd(cash, feeBps, slippageBps)` helper reserving that headroom, applied to *both* the pre-existing OPEN path and the new ADD path. One existing test's expected value updated accordingly (`0.05` → `0.0499250874`, independently reverified: `500 / 1.0015005`).
  - **`broker/accounting.ts` — `addToPosition`/`reducePosition`, proven against 7 hand-verified scenarios (19 new tests in `accounting.test.ts`).** `addToPosition`: true weighted-average entry (`newCostBasis / newQuantity`), holding by construction because `costBasis ≡ quantity × fillPrice` at every prior open/add. `reducePosition`: cost basis released *proportionally* (`costBasis × reduceQuantity/quantity`), entry price *never* touched — proven to keep `costBasis/quantity` internally consistent with the unchanged entry after a partial exit. **The load-bearing consistency proof**: a 100%-quantity `reducePosition` call produces *arithmetically identical* trade/cash/realized-P&L numbers to `closePosition` on the same inputs (tested directly, field-by-field) — this is what makes normalizing a full-magnitude REDUCE to a CLOSE upstream safe, while also proving why that normalization is *required*, not optional: `reducePosition` alone never sets `status`/`closedAt`/`closeReason`, so an un-normalized 100% reduce would leave a `quantity = 0` *open* position, violating `positions_qty_positive`. A dedicated test shows fees alone can turn a nominally profitable reduce (`realizedPnl = +50`) net-negative (`-150.5`) once round-trip costs (`open fee + reduce fee = 200.5`) are counted — the exact reasoning the state payload's `estimatedRoundTripCostPct` field exists to give Jev visibility into.
  - **`model/jev/schema.ts` generalized to a real discriminated union** (`NoulAnswer | ChoiceAnswer | ScoreAnswer`, matching the confirmed API contract that any request can freely mix all three types) — a genuine, deliberate behavior change from the pre-Phase-2 shape, which assumed every answer was `noul` and exposed `.noul` unconditionally. Caught and fixed a **real latent bug** while doing this: the old code's unconditional `.noul` read would have silently evaluated to `undefined` for a wrong-typed answer, and `undefined >= threshold` is `false` — a silent, incorrect ALLOW, never surfaced. Now `expectNoul`/`expectChoice`/`expectScore` throw `JevOutputShapeError` on a type mismatch instead. Existing veto call sites/tests updated mechanically (5 call sites); zero behavior change for the live veto path beyond this fix.
  - **New module `model/jev/management-question.ts`** — state (`JevPositionSnapshot`: direction/entry/current price/quantity/notional/unrealizedPnl%+$/estimatedRoundTripCostPct/heldHours/SL/TP/distance-to-SL/TP%; portfolio-level `navUsd`/`availableCashUsd`/`totalExposurePct`, present only when ≥1 position is being managed) and five questions per OPEN candidate (`{asset}_action` Choice; `{asset}_add_conviction`/`{asset}_reduce_magnitude` Scores, asked speculatively regardless of the actual action per the documented fan-out pattern; `{asset}_stop_intent`/`{asset}_target_intent` Choices). Never sends: fee/slippage bps themselves (only the *derived* cost percentage), portfolio/position/user ids, or any other-asset's own state — a management question is scoped to exactly one position; cross-asset risk stays exclusively the deterministic gate's business. 15 tests in `management-question.test.ts`.
  - **`model/jev/provider.ts` — new `requestPortfolioDecisions(vetoCandidates, managementCandidates, navUsd, availableCashUsd, totalExposurePct, apiKey, fetchImpl)`, the new seam `index.ts` actually calls.** Builds ONE shared state + one combined `questions` map (veto's noul questions ∪ management's choice/score questions) and calls `callJev` exactly once — **live-tested that a genuinely mixed cycle (one FLAT veto asset + one OPEN management asset) is exactly one HTTP request**, not two. `requestVetoDecisions` (the Task 6 migration's original function) is kept, unchanged, still separately tested — a pure veto-only cycle degrades to it structurally, but the cycle wiring now always goes through the unified function. 18 tests in `provider.test.ts` (5 new).
  - **`cycle/apply-management.ts` — the containment seam, mirroring `cycle/apply-veto.ts`'s role exactly (12 tests, `apply-management.test.ts`).** `HOLD` returns the candidate **by reference** (`assertStrictEquals`); `ADD`/`REDUCE` proposals carry *only* `addMagnitude`/`reduceMagnitude` — asserted directly by enumerating the proposal's own keys, proving there is no field anywhere Jev could smuggle a price or dollar figure through. `MODIFY_PROTECTION` is where "deterministic code computes the price" actually happens: `TIGHTEN_TO_BREAKEVEN` computes `entry × (1 ∓ minStopLossPct)` — the tightest *legal* stop, since a literal `SL === entry` fails `positions_sl_tp_ordering_valid`'s strict inequality; `MOVE_CLOSER`/`MOVE_OUT` apply the one-ATR step in the direction-correct sense for both long and short (tested both ways). Both legs resolving to "no change" normalizes to the original HOLD, never a no-op `MODIFY_PROTECTION`.
  - **`cycle/plan-decision.ts` — three new `DecisionPlan` kinds** (`add`/`reduce`/`modifyProtection`), dispatched through the identical exhaustive-switch-plus-`never`-check restructuring `gate.ts` needed, for the exact same reason (this file had its own, independent `OPEN_LONG ? 'long' : 'short'` trap). 19 tests in `plan-decision.test.ts` (8 new), including the negative/throwing cases (approved-but-no-open-position, matching the existing CLOSE convention).
  - **Migration `20260923060000_phase2_portfolio_management.sql`** (27 statements, `pglast`-parsed clean) — widens `agent_decisions_action_valid` and `trades_intent_valid`/`trades_provenance_valid` (drop-then-re-add, the established pattern — Postgres has no `ALTER CONSTRAINT` for a CHECK body); adds `trades.realized_pnl` (nullable, NULL on OPEN/ADD, populated on REDUCE/CLOSE) with a **backfill** for every historical close trade from `positions.realized_pnl` (unambiguous pre-Phase-2, since exactly one close ever existed per position); modifies `close_position_atomic` via `create or replace` (no signature change) to populate that new column; adds two new RPCs, `adjust_position_atomic` (ADD and REDUCE share one RPC — both only ever mutate `quantity`/`entry_price`/`cost_basis` on the one open row, conditional on `status = 'open'`, returning `won_race` exactly like `close_position_atomic` already does) and `modify_protection_atomic` (no trade, no cash effect — just the two position fields the gate already validated); adds `agent_decisions`'s nine new provenance columns; adds `agent_settings.min_trade_notional_pct` (0.0100) / `min_trade_notional_usd` ($25.00), both explicitly commented **provisional**, matching this project's established pattern for un-tuned values.
  - **`index.ts` wiring** — Pass 1 unchanged; management candidates are collected only for an OPEN asset whose Pass-1 candidate is `HOLD` (a regime-flip `CLOSE` is authoritative and is *never* routed through Jev — management is only ever asked about a position the deterministic system already decided to keep open this cycle). One combined `requestPortfolioDecisions` call replaces the veto-only call, gated the same way (`news_veto_enabled`, now understood as "is the model layer on at all"). Pass 2 gained a parallel branch for `openPosition && candidate.action === 'HOLD'`, populating the nine new provenance columns (`proposed_adjust_notional` computed via the *same* exported `deriveRiskBasedNotional` the gate itself calls — reuse, not a second implementation of its math) and applying the not-applicable→HOLD normalization *after* the gate result is known, reusing that single gate call rather than gating twice. - **Phase 2.1 — "decouple entry eligibility from position management" (2026-09-23): a user-reported control-flow bug that, on investigation, turned out not to exist in the deployed code — but surfaced one real defect and one real test-coverage gap, both fixed.**
  - **The reported symptom ("a deterministic HOLD with an intact 50DMA thesis prevents Jev management from running") does not describe the deployed Phase 2 code.** Verified two ways before writing any plan: (1) code — management candidates are collected under `openPosition && candidate.action === 'HOLD'`, and for an open LONG that condition IS the intact-thesis case (a regime-flip produces `CLOSE`, not `HOLD`); (2) live evidence — the Phase 2 smoke test an hour earlier had both BTC and ETH open LONG with intact regime and `HOLD` candidates, and both genuinely reached Jev (`model_version: 'jev-1.13.0'`, `prompt_version: 'jev-management-v1/atr1.0'`). The reported observation almost certainly described **pre-Phase-2** behavior (collection used to read `if (candidate.action !== 'OPEN_LONG') continue` — an open position never reached Jev at all), true for most of the ~2-day window referenced, and stale by about an hour at the time of the report. This was reported back to the user directly, with both pieces of evidence, before any plan was written — not silently "fixed" as if the premise had been correct.
  - **The real defect: `news_veto_enabled` silently also gated the management layer.** `index.ts`'s single combined guard (`if (settings.newsVetoEnabled && (vetoCandidates.length > 0 || managementCandidates.length > 0) ...)`) meant turning off the entry veto would have silently disabled ADD/REDUCE/CLOSE/MODIFY_PROTECTION too — two unrelated product decisions sharing one switch, inert only because the flag happened to be `true`. Fixed with a new, independent `agent_settings.management_enabled` column (migration `20260923103000_management_enabled_flag.sql`, default `true`) — each flag is now read by exactly one branch of candidate collection, with no code path by which one can affect the other.
  - **The real gap: neither candidate collection nor the "should we call the model" decision was a testable function.** There was no `index.test.ts`, and both lived as inline logic in `index.ts` — exactly what let the pre-Phase-2 behavior go unnoticed for two days. Extracted into `cycle/collect-candidates.ts`: `collectModelCandidates(sources, flags, nowIso)` (pure, mirrors the extraction logic `apply-veto.ts`/`apply-management.ts` already established) and, as a **separate function** after user review caught that collection tests alone don't prove the call actually happens or is correctly suppressed on failure, `shouldCallModel(vetoCandidates, managementCandidates, modelCallFailedReason)` — the complete 5-row truth table (0/0/null→No, veto-only→Yes, management-only→Yes, both→Yes, any+failure→No), each row its own test. Together these prove the full chain — candidate generation → call eligibility → model invocation — without needing to test `index.ts` itself.
  - **News-provider-failure coupling was reviewed and deliberately kept, but promoted from an inline comment to a named, documented invariant** (both `CLAUDE.md` and `architecture.md`): *"When news retrieval fails, discretionary model management is fail-closed for the cycle; the independent position monitor remains the only automatic protection mechanism."* Stated alongside its product consequence, per explicit user request: a news outage can prevent profit-taking, ADD, REDUCE, CLOSE, and protection changes, not just a new entry — so a quiet cycle with no management action can mean *the model was never called*, not *it chose HOLD*. `agent_decisions.model_version` (`'jev-*'` vs `'call-failed'` vs `'not-called'`) is what disambiguates the two after the fact; any later analysis of management behavior must filter on that column, never count actions directly.
  - **17 new tests in `collect-candidates.test.ts`** — both open-position-with-intact-thesis cases (BTC and ETH individually, then both in one batch), the FLAT-no-candidate zero-call case, the FLAT→veto path unchanged (deliberately **behavioral only, no literal-string guard** — a second round of user review caught that a string-matching test would fail on a legitimate rename while proving nothing about actual behavior), a regime-flip CLOSE never reaching management, the full `shouldCallModel` truth table, and — the direct regression tests for the actual defect — `management_enabled=false` leaving veto collection unaffected, `news_veto_enabled=false` leaving management collection unaffected (this is the literal coupling being fixed), and both flags off together.
  - **4 new tests in `apply-management.test.ts`** (test 4, per a third round of user review requesting a stronger, more explicit fixture): one shared, deliberately-generous gate context — `regime UP, candidate HOLD (thesis genuinely intact), position OPEN and in profit` — with REDUCE, CLOSE, ADD, and MODIFY_PROTECTION each run through the complete chain (`applyManagementOutcome` → `evaluateRiskGate`) from that identical starting state, each landing on a real, hand-computed `approved` verdict (not just "not rejected"). Proves an intact bullish thesis never short-circuits management for any of the four actions, not only for the one the earlier tests happened to cover.
  - **2 new tests in `position-monitor/independence.test.ts`**, with an honest stated limit: a unit test cannot prove runtime independence (that's what live monitor ticks are for — see the Phase 2 entry's own "five clean ticks" observations). What it *can* prove, and does: no static import edge from any of `position-monitor`'s three source files into `model/jev/**` or `cycle/apply-*`/`cycle/collect-candidates.ts`, and that the monitor's own close path is the shared `broker/accounting.ts`'s `closePosition`, not a second implementation. **This is the first test in the repo needing a real OS permission** (`Deno.readTextFile`) — every prior test used mocked `fetchImpl` injection specifically to avoid this. Scoped as narrowly as possible (`--allow-read=supabase/functions/position-monitor`, not blanket `--allow-read`) rather than avoided by weakening the test to something that doesn't actually read the source. **The full-suite verification command changes from here on**: `deno test --allow-read=supabase/functions/position-monitor supabase/`.
  - **`deno test --allow-read=supabase/functions/position-monitor supabase/` — 438/438** (23 new: 17 collect-candidates, 4 apply-management, 2 independence). `npm run typecheck` / `oxlint` / `npm run build` all clean.

One controlled manual cycle, full chain inspected:
