This was scoped narrowly to what Phase 2 actually needed rather than retroactively fixing OPEN's own original gap — that original observation (a token-sized OPEN is still structurally approvable) technically still stands for OPEN specifically, just no longer for ADD/REDUCE.

Do not resolve these by silently inventing behavior. Resolve them when the relevant implementation unit requires them and record the decision here.

## Architecture Decisions

- The Chrome extension is a UI/control surface, not the autonomous engine.
- The autonomous loop runs in Supabase Edge Functions.
- ~~Supabase Cron/pg_cron triggers the loop every 3 hours.~~ **Superseded 2026-09-19 for V0 — see the manual-only execution-mode entry below.** Remains the target design for a future autonomous mode; not current V0 behavior.
- V0 uses one LLM call per cycle covering BTC and ETH.
- Technical indicators are deterministic code.
- Risk enforcement is deterministic code.
- Paper execution is deterministic code.
- ~~V0 is long-or-flat and paper-only.~~ **Superseded 2026-09-18** — position model is now FLAT/LONG/SHORT (`context/specs/trading-domain-contract.md`); still strictly paper-only and unleveraged, that part didn't change.
- All decisions, including HOLDs and skipped cycles, are persisted.
- Exact model input payloads are persisted for later replay/evaluation.
- News is treated as untrusted input.
- The decision interval is configuration, even though V0 starts at 3 hours.
- Event-driven triggers are deferred to V1.
- **Gemini access for V0 runs a single paid-tier API key behind the `callModel(payload)` seam** (superseded from an initial 3-key free-tier rotation — see below). The rotation loop itself (`model/call-model.ts`) is unchanged and still correct for N keys: a key-specific failure (HTTP error or network throw) moves to the next key in the array; a content-level failure (bad finish reason, invalid output shape) fails the call immediately without rotating, since a different key would hit the identical problem. Only `agent-cycle/index.ts`'s `Deno.serve` handler (which key(s) it reads from the environment) needed to change — exactly the "config change behind the same seam, not a code change" this file already anticipated for the eventual move to a paid key, just landing during Step 7 rather than at a later separate "launch" milestone.
- **~~Gemini model: `gemini-2.5-flash`~~ → `gemini-3.6-flash` (user, 2026-09-19, superseding the 2026-09-18 choice).** Found live during Step 7: `gemini-2.5-flash`, working correctly via direct API calls just hours earlier (Step 6), started actively failing — 2 of 3 free-tier keys returned HTTP 503, the third an explicit HTTP 404 stating it's "no longer available to new users" and naming `gemini-3.6-flash` as the replacement, even though `models.list` still listed it as available on all three keys at that exact moment (confirmed by re-checking immediately — a listing-vs-invocation gap, not a fluke). Surfaced to the user rather than silently switched; the user's own resolution was both the model change and the single-paid-key change above, together. `gemini-3.6-flash` was live-verified before switching (same `responseSchema`/`thinkingConfig` mechanism, correct structured output) and spends *more* thinking tokens by default than 2.5-flash did (275-298 vs. 100-162 on the same trivial prompt) — reinforcing, not undermining, the `maxOutputTokens: 8192` headroom decision below. Still a code-level constant (`model/call-model.ts`), not an env var or `agent_settings` column, for the same reason as before: prompt/model versions are execution-time facts recorded on `agent_runs`/`agent_decisions`, not user-tunable configuration. No vendor SDK — a raw `fetch` client against the classic `generateContent` REST endpoint, matching the CoinGecko/RSS provider pattern exactly.
- **Gemini generation config (Step 6, unaffected by the Step 7 model swap)**: `temperature: 0.2`, `responseMimeType: 'application/json'` + `responseSchema` (schema-constrained generation, not prompt-only JSON instructions), `maxOutputTokens: 8192` — raised from the superseded spec's original 4096 recommendation after finding live that "thinking" tokens share the same budget as visible output. Thinking itself is left at Gemini's default (not forced to a budget of 0) — deliberately flagged as a reasoning-quality lever adjacent to the deferred NEWS/TECHNICAL methodology review, not decided unilaterally here.
- **`open_position_atomic` (Step 7, `supabase/migrations/20260919055617_agent_cycle_open_rpc.sql`) is the atomic-open counterpart to `close_position_atomic` (Step 5)** — deliberately simpler (no conditional race-guard: trading-domain-contract.md §6 already establishes opens have no equivalent race). Both RPCs persist exactly what the pure broker functions (`openPosition`/`closePosition`, Step 4) already computed and apply `net_cash_delta` via a fresh read-modify-write; neither does its own accounting math.
- ~~The decision-cycle's own `pg_cron` schedule was deliberately not created in Step 7 — the function is deployed and works when explicitly invoked, but nothing currently triggers it automatically, and `agent_settings.is_paused` remains `true`. Both are separate, explicit actions for the user to take when ready to let the agent run unattended.~~ **Resolved 2026-09-19 — V0 execution mode: manual-only (explicit user product decision).** The decision cycle (`agent-cycle`) runs exclusively from the Chrome extension's own "Run agent" button (Home screen) — invoked directly with the anon key as bearer token, no separate `control` wrapper Edge Function (the `control/` System Boundary entry above remains a future possibility, not built). `agent_settings.is_paused` is now permanently `false` (migration `20260919131706_manual_execution_mode.sql`) so a manual click actually executes rather than being skipped — but **no `pg_cron` schedule was created, and none should be**, per the user's explicit instruction. `decision_interval_minutes` (180) stays configured and unused, reserved for a future autonomous mode the user will deliberately opt into later. CORS support (an OPTIONS handler + `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`/`-Headers`) was added to `agent-cycle/index.ts`'s `Deno.serve` wrapper only — this function had only ever been invoked server-to-server before (cron, or this session's own live-check scripts), and Supabase does not add CORS headers to Edge Function responses on its own. **Accepted, bounded risk, stated explicitly rather than glossed over**: the anon key — already public-by-design (see the auth-model entry below) — can now directly trigger a live, Gemini-costing, portfolio-mutating `agent-cycle` invocation from anyone who has it, not just the extension's own user. The existing per-3-hour-bucket idempotency key (`agent-cycle/index.ts`'s `floorToIntervalIso` — unchanged by this task) bounds the realistic worst case to "one extra decision per window," not unlimited spam. No new protection was added for this, since doing so would mean editing the pipeline's own idempotency/auth logic — bigger scope than "invoke the existing function," which is exactly what the user asked for.
- **Secrets mechanism for the Gemini key(s)**, set up ahead of the Gemini seam unit so there's a correct place for them the moment they exist: one gitignored root `.env` (template committed as `.env.example`), read two ways by design — locally via Deno's native `--env-file` flag for smoke-test scripts (same pattern as `coingecko.live-check.ts`/`rss-news.live-check.ts`), and pushed to the deployed Edge Function's actual secret store via `supabase secrets set --env-file=.env` (confirmed live via `--help`, not assumed) — never a plain checked-in file the function ships with. Variable name: `GEMINI_API_KEY_1` (originally `GEMINI_API_KEY_1/2/3` for the 3-key rotation; see the Step 7 entry above for why that was retired). Explicitly never `VITE_`-prefixed anywhere — Vite inlines any `VITE_*` var straight into the client bundle, which would ship a Gemini key inside the publicly-readable Chrome extension (invariant 2).
- **`context/specs/gemini-decision-agent.md` is superseded, not deleted.** It's the pre-pivot decision-agent spec (long-only BUY/SELL/HOLD, raw-percentage sizing, no stops) — kept in the repo history and on disk as a record of the earlier design, but `context/specs/trading-domain-contract.md` is the live reference from 2026-09-18 forward. Step 6 (Gemini seam) rewrites the superseded doc's output-schema and prompt sections rather than working from them as-is.
- **Position/action/short model (2026-09-18, user-directed pivot).** Long+short, one net position per asset (FLAT/LONG/SHORT), explicit actions `OPEN_LONG`/`OPEN_SHORT`/`HOLD`/`CLOSE` (not `BUY`/`SELL` — ambiguous once both directions exist), mandatory SL/TP on every open, position sizing derived from risk-at-stop rather than a raw NAV percentage, and SL/TP execution on an independent 10-minute monitor cycle rather than gated by the 3-hour decision cycle. Full detail, including every accounting formula, is `context/specs/trading-domain-contract.md` — not restated here to avoid two sources of truth.
- **Shorts are 1x unleveraged synthetic paper only** — collateral-equal-to-notional reservation, no margin, no funding, no exchange liquidation. Collateral exhaustion (loss reaching the reserved collateral, at `2× entry`) is a **deterministic close**, not a P&L clamp on a position left open — this was a specific, load-bearing correction the user required (§0 item 5 of the original request) over an earlier draft that would have clamped P&L while leaving the position running.
- **The stop-out re-entry block is an explicitly-labeled proxy for "avoid re-entering the same failed thesis,"** not real thesis matching (asset + direction + elapsed time only) — stated as such in the contract doc so it's never mistaken for something more semantic than it is. `CLOSE` is never blocked by it or by anything else; only re-entry is throttled.
- **The position monitor polls at 10 minutes but replays the 5-minute price series since its last run**, not a single spot snapshot — verified live (2026-09-18) that CoinGecko's `/market_chart` gives 5-minute points (no high/low) while `/ohlc` gives 30-minute candles (with high/low). Detection is data-limited at 5 minutes, not poll-limited at 10 — but this is explicitly documented as an approximation of a real stop order, not a claim of equivalence: sub-5-minute wicks that breach and recover are invisible to it.
- **`CLAUDE.md`'s real-money/leverage/shorts prohibition, approved as reworded** (2026-09-18): now bans real-money trading, exchange execution, real leverage, and derivatives, while explicitly permitting 1x unleveraged synthetic paper shorts. `ai-workflow-rules.md`'s separate, absolute real-money prohibition was untouched — it didn't name shorts specifically, so it never needed rewording. All five context files updated to match (Completed has the full file-by-file list).
- **SL/TP distance bounds are genuinely provisional**, not just conservatively chosen: `min/max_stop_loss_pct` (0.5%/15%) and `min/max_take_profit_pct` (0.5%/50%), seeded directly in Step 1's migration and flagged there as placeholders. The real values are explicitly deferred to the NEWS/TECHNICAL methodology review (position-model plan) — whoever does that review should treat these as "wide enough not to bind yet," not as considered defaults. The one bound that ISN'T provisional: a short's stop distance must stay below 100% regardless of what `max_stop_loss_pct` is eventually set to — that's enforced unconditionally by `positions_sl_tp_ordering_valid`, independent of the settings table.
- **Dropped/superseded `agent_settings` columns are gone, not deprecated-in-place**: `max_position_pct` and `cooldown_minutes` no longer exist as of Step 1's migration (replaced by `max_single_trade_pct`/`max_asset_exposure_pct` and `stop_out_reentry_block_minutes` respectively). Any code or doc still referencing the old names by that point predates the pivot and needs updating, not backward-compatibility handling — code-standards.md's "change the code, not backwards-compatibility shims" applies here.
- `extension/` owns only Chrome-specific surface (manifest, MV3 lifecycle, `chrome.*` adapters); `src/components/` and `src/features/` (referenced in `code-standards.md` § File Organization) own the React app `extension/` renders. Resolves the apparent overlap between `architecture.md` § System Boundaries and `code-standards.md`'s `src/` entries.
- `src/shared/` must stay Deno-compatible (explicit `.ts` extensions on relative imports, no Node built-ins) so Vite (extension) and Supabase Edge Functions (Deno) import the same files with no build step — this is how `code-standards.md`'s "do not duplicate domain types across extension and server" is actually satisfied given the two runtimes. Binding on every future file added under `src/shared/`. Refined in Unit 3: "dependency-free" turned out to be the wrong bar — `zod` is now a real, sanctioned dependency (schema-validating every provider boundary, per code-standards.md § TypeScript "validate all unknown external input"), resolved two different ways per runtime from the same bare `import { z } from 'zod'`: Vite/tsc via the normal `package.json` dependency + `node_modules`, Deno via the root `deno.json`'s `{"imports": {"zod": "npm:zod@^3.24.1"}}` import map. The actual bar is "resolves identically for both runtimes without a per-runtime source fork," not "zero dependencies" — a dependency is fine as long as both import maps point at the same package and version. This was also proven, not just designed: `deno check`/`deno test` and `tsc -b` both pass clean on the same `src/shared/` files (Unit 3).
- Root `lib/` (per `code-standards.md` § File Organization) and shadcn/ui's conventional `@/lib/utils` both claim the same territory. Resolved with two path aliases rather than one: `@/lib/*` → root `lib/*`, `@/*` → `src/*` (more specific alias listed first in both `tsconfig.app.json` and `vite.config.ts`). shadcn components added later resolve `@/lib/utils` unmodified.
- MV3 manifest ships popup-only for Unit 1 (no `background` key) — a background service worker is valid to omit for a popup-only extension and nothing in Unit 1 needs one. Add it when control actions (pause/resume/run-now) require messaging — that extension-side work isn't itself one of the position-model plan's numbered steps (0–7 end at backend agent-cycle wiring); it follows once that plan completes.
- Font tokens (`--font-sans` / `--font-mono`) are wired to Inter / JetBrains Mono with full system fallback stacks, but the actual font files are not yet vendored — V0 renders on fallback fonts (`ui-sans-serif` / `ui-monospace`) for now. Deliberate: avoids a network font-CDN dependency in the popup; vendor the files locally under `extension/` in the UI-focused unit (extension shell — after the position-model plan's Step 7, not itself numbered by it) rather than link a remote stylesheet.
- Risk appetite, not a raw threshold, is the user-facing risk control. `agent_settings.risk_appetite` stores `conservative | balanced | aggressive`; the deterministic risk gate derives its minimum-confidence threshold from that via a pure mapping function in `src/shared/` (tunable without a migration). The threshold actually in force for a given decision is denormalised onto `agent_decisions.effective_min_confidence`, so re-tuning the mapping later never makes historical decisions unreadable. Seeded at `balanced`. Supersedes the earlier "exact minimum confidence threshold" open question.
- V0 config values (seeded in the `seed_v0_config` migration): starting capital $10,000; fee 10 bps/side; slippage 5 bps/side — still current. ~~per-asset cooldown 360 min; max position 25%~~ **superseded 2026-09-18** — `cooldown_minutes`/`max_position_pct` no longer exist (Step 1 migration); replaced by `stop_out_reentry_block_minutes` (same 360 min value carried over, now asymmetric — never blocks CLOSE) and the risk-derived sizing + `max_single_trade_pct`/`max_asset_exposure_pct` caps (20%/35%, not a single flat 25%) — see the Architecture Decisions entries above for the position-model pivot.
- Two seeded values were implementation choices rather than user decisions, flagged in the seed migration's header so they are visible: `news_lookback_overlap_minutes` = 15 (buffer so headlines near a cycle boundary are not dropped when the interval changes) and `max_data_staleness_minutes` = 30 (above this the cycle fails closed per invariant 6). Revisit either if the first live runs suggest different values.
- ~~`agent_settings` starts with `is_paused = true`. The agent must not begin trading the instant the schema lands; unpause explicitly after the loop is smoke-tested.~~ **Superseded 2026-09-19** — permanently `false` now (see the manual-only execution-mode entry above). The original concern (autonomous trading starting unexpectedly) no longer applies the same way: with no `pg_cron` schedule ever wired to `agent-cycle`, `is_paused`'s only remaining effect is gating whether an explicit manual click does anything at all.
- Prompt and model versions are execution-time facts recorded on `agent_runs` / `agent_decisions`, not columns on `agent_settings` — they describe the code that ran, not user-tunable configuration. This also kept the "exact Gemini model identifier" question (resolved above: `gemini-3.6-flash`) from blocking the schema back when it was still open.
- **`close_position_atomic` (Step 5, `supabase/migrations/20260918124421_position_monitor_rpc.sql`) is the single, shared atomic-persistence primitive for closing any position** — used by the position monitor now and intended for the agent cycle's own `CLOSE` handling in Step 7, rather than a second implementation of the same conditional-update race guard. It does no accounting math itself (that stays exclusively in Step 4's pure `closePosition()`); its only two responsibilities are the conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status = 'open'` and applying `net_cash_delta` to `portfolios.cash` via a fresh read-modify-write inside the same transaction.
- **Any Edge Function that will actually be deployed needs its own `deno.json`** — `supabase functions deploy` does not resolve the project-root `deno.json` import map the way local `deno check`/`deno test` do (confirmed against Supabase's own docs, then empirically against a real deploy failure and fix in Step 5). Auto-discovered from the function's own directory; no `--import-map` flag needed. `agent-cycle/` will need this the first time Step 6/7 deploys it.
- **Any code reading a `positions`/`agent_decisions`/`trades`/etc. row back from Postgres must normalize `timestamptz` columns before parsing them with a `src/shared/` Zod schema** — PostgREST serializes them as `"...+00:00"`, which `z.string().datetime()`'s default (`Z`-only) rejects. Normalize via `new Date(x).toISOString()` at the DB-row boundary (see `position-monitor/index.ts`'s `toIsoZ`), not by loosening the schemas — CoinGecko's own timestamps are already `Z`-format, so the schemas' strictness is correct and worth keeping uniform.
- Supabase auth for V0: anon key only, no login screen, no Supabase Auth user. The extension ships the public anon key; every exposed table has RLS, with policies scoped to the anon role (read-only where the table permits it — no table should grant anon broad write access; mutations go through the authenticated server-side Edge Functions, which use the service-role key and are never reachable from the extension). Accepted risk: this is genuinely single-user and paper-only, so the exposure is someone finding the extension ID and reading paper P&L — low stakes, and avoids building a login flow and session-persistence layer for a security boundary that isn't protecting anything real. Revisit if V0 ever moves toward real funds (invariant 11 already forbids that without an explicit architecture change).
- **Trading Strategy V1 (2026-09-21, implemented — see Completed for the full entry): the model boundary flipped.** Deterministic code (`agent-cycle/strategy/`) now originates every decision; Gemini is a binary veto called at most once per cycle, only for OPEN_LONG candidates, only asking whether a named exogenous confound should block the trade. `CLAUDE.md`, `architecture.md`, and `project-overview.md` were all updated in the same pass — see their own text for the corrected flow. This supersedes the "one Gemini decision call" framing throughout this file's earlier entries (Steps 6-7, UI steps) — those entries are left as-written since they accurately describe what was true when built; this note is the pointer forward.
- **`atrPct` is a percentage-as-number (e.g. `1.10` meaning 1.10%), while every `*Pct` field derived from it (`stopLossPct`, `takeProfitPct`, the `effective_*` columns) is a fraction (`0.025` meaning 2.5%).** `strategy/rules.ts`'s `stopLossPctFor` does the one real `/100` conversion; a dedicated test (`'unit conversion'`) guards against ever reintroducing a 100x sizing error here. Worth remembering before touching this file again — `calculateATRPercent` (Unit 5) has used this convention since before V1 existed, V1 is just the first place a percentage and a fraction meet in the same formula.
- **`effective_portfolio_risk_ceiling_pct` is `portfolio_risk_ceiling_multiplier × effectiveRiskBudgetPct`, a fraction of NAV (e.g. `0.01125`) — not a USD figure, and not itself bounded to `≤1` the way `effective_max_total_notional_pct` is** (the multiplier can in principle push it past 1, though no realistic config does). The operational USD figure (`portfolioRiskCeilingUsd = that fraction × current nav`) is recomputed fresh per gate call in `index.ts` and never persisted directly — only the fraction is, so re-tuning `nav` doesn't retroactively change what a historical decision's ceiling "was."
- **`agent_decisions.model_vetoed` is `null` whenever no *completed* model verdict exists for that decision** — not just "no model call was attempted." This covers three distinct cases identically: a non-OPEN_LONG candidate (never reaches the veto step), `news_veto_enabled = false` (veto deliberately off), and a veto call or its dependent news fetch that failed (fails closed to HOLD, but is recorded as no-verdict rather than a fabricated `true`) — see Completed's implementation entry for the reasoning on the third case specifically. Never read an absent/null `model_vetoed` as "not vetoed" — check `strategy_version`/`action`/`reasons` for why no verdict exists before drawing any conclusion from its absence.
- **`cron.job_run_details.status = 'succeeded'` does NOT mean the target Edge Function ever ran, or authorized the request — it only means the SQL body executed without a database-level error.** Both this project's scheduled jobs call `net.http_post` (`pg_net`), which is fire-and-forget: the cron job "succeeds" the instant the HTTP request is *queued*, regardless of what status code the target eventually returns. Found the hard way (2026-09-22, market-quotes plan): `position-monitor-10min` showed clean successes for hours while its actual requests were all being rejected 401 by a broken Vault secret (see Current Phase's CRITICAL entry). **To actually verify a cron-triggered Edge Function ran, check its own effects** — a fresh row in whatever table it writes (`agent_runs` for position-monitor, `market_quotes.fetched_at` for market-refresh) — never `cron.job_run_details` alone. `net._http_response` (also queried async, by the request's own `id`) carries the real status code and body if a direct check is needed.
- **Independent market-quote refresh (2026-09-22, implemented — see Completed): a third, deliberately trade-incapable Edge Function.** `market-refresh` imports only the CoinGecko provider and row mappers — never the strategy, risk gate, broker, or either atomic RPC — and touches only `market_quotes`, a new 2-row table separate from `market_snapshots` (the strategy's own immutable per-decision audit trail, unchanged). Runs on its own 5-minute `pg_cron` schedule (`market-refresh-5min`), independent of `agent-cycle`'s manual-only decision cadence and `position-monitor`'s 10-minute exit cadence; `agent-cycle` additionally upserts the same table on every manual run at zero extra CoinGecko cost. No `agent_runs` row and no idempotency key for this function — unlike a decision or a monitor tick, a duplicate refresh is harmless (invariant 10 protects against duplicate *trades*, and there is nothing here to duplicate).
- **The public anon key is a legitimate way to test-invoke any Edge Function directly over HTTPS** (`curl` with `Authorization: Bearer <anon key>`) — Supabase's gateway accepts any valid JWT, privileged or not, to authorize *invoking* a function; what that function's own internal code can then read/write is a completely separate question governed by RLS and whatever `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` it holds internally. Used this (2026-09-22) to verify `market-refresh` end-to-end while the shared `service_role_key` Vault secret was broken, without ever needing to fix that secret first or handle its value.
- **`agent_runs.idempotency_key` and concurrency exclusion are two different concerns, not one (2026-09-22, "manual idempotency" plan — see Completed).** A key's *uniqueness* only ever proves "this exact tick/click doesn't already have a row" — it does NOT prove "no other cycle is running right now" unless every possible concurrent invocation is guaranteed to compute the identical key, which a wall-clock-floored bucket only does for invocations landing in the same bucket. Real mutual exclusion for decision cycles comes from `agent_runs_one_running_decision_idx`, a partial unique index on `(kind) where status='running' and kind='decision'` — deliberately scoped to `decision` only, never `monitor` (a wedged monitor row would silently block all SL/TP monitoring, matching the `service_role_key` incident's own lesson about invisible safety failures). `position-monitor` needs no equivalent: `close_position_atomic` is already fully race-safe via its own `won_race` conditional-update return. Any future third kind of decision-cycle-adjacent mutation should ask the same two questions separately — "how do I dedupe a retried tick?" and "how do I stop two of these running at once?" — rather than assuming one key answers both.
- **A `'running'` row that never transitions out is a real, structural risk whenever a mutex depends on `status`** — an Edge Function can crash or hit its wall-clock limit between acquiring the mutex (the `agent_runs` insert) and its own `catch` block running. `agent-cycle` now reaps any `kind='decision', status='running'` row older than 10 minutes immediately before every insert attempt (`cycle/idempotency.ts`'s `staleRunCutoffIso`) — cheap, idempotent under a race, and the only thing standing between a single bad deploy/timeout and every future manual click being permanently blocked.
- **Gemini → TypeSafe Jev (2026-09-22, implemented — see Completed): the model provider changed, the model boundary did not.** Jev occupies exactly the same narrow veto-only slot Gemini did (trading-strategy-v1.md §11-12) — this migration is a provider swap, not a re-litigation of "the LLM proposes vs. vetoes" (that question was already settled by V1). The veto changed shape though: Gemini returned a boolean directly; Jev returns a calibrated probability (`noul`, 0-1) that code thresholds into a boolean via a versioned, explicitly-provisional constant (`JEV_VETO_THRESHOLD` in `model/jev/question.ts`) — see the Completed entry for why the threshold ships unvalidated on purpose and what makes that safe (raw-`noul` persistence, not pre-validation).
- **No provider fallback exists by design, for Jev exactly as it never existed for Gemini** — a total Jev outage means no new entries open that cycle; it does not mean an implicit approval, and it does not mean trying a second model. Exits, SL/TP, `position-monitor`, and every risk control remain entirely independent of any model provider and are unaffected by an outage.
- **CoinGecko has two entirely different free tiers, and this project used the wrong one for its whole lifetime until 2026-09-22.** The fully anonymous public endpoint (no key, what every request used until now) is rate-limited to 5-15 calls/min, shared across ALL of CoinGecko's worldwide anonymous traffic. The free **Demo** API key (register at coingecko.com/en/api/pricing, no card) raises that to a stable 100 calls/min + 10,000/month — the 10,000/month figure CoinGecko's own docs advertise belongs to THIS tier, not the keyless one, a mismatch that looked like an inexplicable rate-limit bug until traced. `providers/coingecko.ts` now sends `Deno.env.get('COINGECKO_API_KEY')` (when set) as the `x-cg-demo-api-key` header on every request, across all three Edge Functions; unset, it degrades gracefully to the old keyless behavior rather than failing. Worth remembering before adding any NEW CoinGecko-calling code: always thread the optional `apiKey` param through, the same way `fetchImpl`/`baseUrl` already are.
- **A blanket "derive direction/branch from the action string right after the early-return guards" pattern is a live trap the moment a new action literal can be added later, not just a style nit (found 2026-09-22/23, Phase 2).** Two independent files had it — `gate.ts` (`proposal.action === 'OPEN_LONG' ? 'long' : 'short'`) and `plan-decision.ts` (the identical shape) — both fixed the same way: an exhaustive per-action `if` chain ending in `const exhaustive: never = proposal; throw ...`, so a future action added without its own branch is a **compile error**, not a silent mis-signing. Apply this shape on sight anywhere a discriminated union is dispatched by a short-circuiting ternary after only 1-2 branches have been peeled off — the bug is invisible until the union actually grows, which is exactly when it's most dangerous.
- **`riskStatus: 'not_applicable'` is reusable vocabulary for "this decision is now a HOLD," not exclusively "the proposal already was HOLD" (Phase 2).** An ADD/REDUCE the gate determines is below the minimum trade notional returns `not_applicable` rather than `rejected` — deliberately, because `not_applicable` already means "nothing to gate," which is exactly the HOLD it's about to become once `index.ts` normalizes `finalProposal` back to the original candidate. This lets one gate call serve both "compute the real numbers" and "signal the normalization," rather than gating twice. Contrast with a genuine `MODIFY_PROTECTION` rejection (e.g. an attempted widen), which stays `rejected` and visibly shows up as a rejected `MODIFY_PROTECTION` in the decision feed — that one is a real denial, not a too-small normalization, and conflating the two would hide real Jev requests that got refused.
- **Confidence is not a uniform safety signal across actions with different risk polarities — a lesson from a rejected draft, not just a design choice (Phase 2).** A single "confidence below X → HOLD" rule looks like a sensible fail-safe until applied to `CLOSE`: normalizing a low-confidence CLOSE to HOLD *keeps risk on*, the opposite of safe. Any future confidence-based gate must be evaluated per-action against what "the safe direction" actually means for that specific action, never applied as one blanket rule — and should be chosen from real observed confidence-vs-outcome data (Phase 2 persists `actionConfidence` and the full probability distribution on every decision specifically so that data can eventually exist), not assumed.
- **An "X may not influence size/price" rule needs to name the actual boundary, or it becomes false the moment the system does something an absolutist reading forbids (Phase 2).** Jev's ADD magnitude genuinely is a sizing input — the accurate invariant, stated in `CLAUDE.md`/`architecture.md`, is "Jev may choose a bounded adjustment magnitude; deterministic code remains the sole authority over final size," not "Jev may not influence size" (which an earlier plan draft said, and which Phase 2 as approved does not honor literally). When a "may not X" rule is about to be written, check it against every code path that's about to exist, not just the ones already built.

## Session Notes

- The first implementation priority is reliability of the end-to-end loop, not trading performance.
- Do not add the V0.1 news/no-news control, buy-and-hold benchmark, or SOL until the V0 loop has run cleanly for several days.
- If a new requirement conflicts with this context, stop and surface the conflict before changing architecture.
- **Unit 1 complete.** Scaffold only — no domain types, schemas, providers, `callModel`, risk gate, or broker exist yet; none of invariants 1–12 apply yet since nothing in this unit touches the extension/server boundary, the model, or trading logic.

- **Incident (2026-09-19, Step 6) — two of the three real Gemini API key values were briefly exposed in the assistant's own tool output**, caused by a shell-quoting interaction with the `.env` file's original `KEY = value` spacing (`bash source` interprets a space after `=` as "assign empty, then run the rest as a command," which prints the "command" — i.e. the key value — as part of the resulting error). Not a Supabase/service-role-key-shaped incident (see Unit 2 / Step 5 notes on that separate, recurring issue) — a different, self-inflicted mechanism, root-caused and fixed (`.env` whitespace stripped) before it could recur. The user was told directly and advised to treat those three keys as compromised and rotate them; this file does not repeat the values. Lesson for future sessions: when sourcing a `.env` file into a shell for a one-off check, verify the file's exact formatting first (or use a language runtime's own env-file loader, which is more tolerant and — as here — never needs the value to pass through a shell command line at all) rather than assuming `KEY=value` spacing is safe.

Short version: Jev can now manage an EXISTING open position (not just veto a new entry) — `HOLD`/`ADD`/`REDUCE`/`CLOSE`/`MODIFY_PROTECTION` — but deterministic code still originates every entry and remains the sole authority over final size/price for everything. `requestPortfolioDecisions` (`model/jev/provider.ts`) is the one seam covering BOTH veto and management questions in a single shared request; `cycle/apply-management.ts` is the containment seam for management, mirroring `cycle/apply-veto.ts` exactly. **Code-complete, fully tested (415/415), full gate green — but NOT deployed.** Deployment needs its own explicit go-ahead (Next Up); do not deploy without asking first, matching this session's own established pattern for every prior deploy.

- **STRAT-1 — cost correctness, evidence instruments, and a historical research engine (2026-10-08, in progress).** Triggered by a live investigation (`context/diagnostics/long-only-losses-investigation-2026-10-08.md`) into sustained losses across every account: 21 champion trades, 0 shorts ever, 15 losses. Initial hypothesis (bias/direction) was real but mis-prioritized — the decisive finding, from real `initial_risk_usd`-normalized R values (n=21, mean 1R=$40.08): **pre-cost price movement is -0.141R, t=-0.91, 95% CI [-0.445,+0.163]R — not statistically different from zero.** The *certain* loss is cost drag, **-0.215R/trade** (fees $122.10 + slippage $61.05 over 21 trades), confirmed in code (`accounting.ts:56`, funding charges shorts only — the wrong side to penalize if shorts are ever enabled) and in the sizing math (`size_cap_applied='single_trade'` pinned on 20 of 23 sized decisions — every risk multiplier was inert because the 0.5% risk budget asked for far more notional than the 15% cap permits).
  - **P1 — sizing fix, DONE, DEPLOYED, LIVE-VERIFIED.** `STRATEGY_PROFILES.intraday_ls.risk.riskBudgetPct`: `0.005` → `0.0015` (`src/shared/strategy/profiles.ts`), so `deriveRiskBasedNotional`'s result lands under the 15% single-trade cap at the common 1.2% stop floor (12.5% vs 41.7% before) — multipliers can now actually change size instead of clamping to the identical cap every time. Mirrored into `config-presets.ts`'s `V4_COMPAT_CONFIG.riskBudgetPct` for hash-consistency (confirmed this field is NOT yet live-read for sizing — `index.ts:518` reads `strategy.risk.riskBudgetPct` from `profiles.ts` directly, never `activeIntradayLsConfig.config.riskBudgetPct` — so the DB's already-seeded `strategy_configs` row stays stale-but-harmless until that future wiring happens). One pinning test updated (`domain/strategy-profiles.test.ts`) since this is a deliberate value change, not a refactor. Full suite 844/844, typecheck/lint/build clean. Deployed; live-verified on a fresh champion decision row (`effective_risk_budget_pct: 0.0015` on all 4 assets). E4's own 06:30 cron tick raced the deploy and used the stale value once (benign, self-resolved at its next 15-min tick, same as any other code deploy mid-cycle).
  - **P2 — daily-close look-ahead fix + quarantine, DONE, DEPLOYED, LIVE-VERIFIED.** New `gridAlignedTail(points, intervalMs)` (`strategy/closed-bars.ts`) trims any trailing point not landing exactly on its own interval's grid — a deterministic, grid-based backstop `closedPoints`'s gap-heuristic alone can't provide (a response that omits today's 00:00 point makes the trailing live point's gap look >= 24h, so `closedPoints` leaves it in place). Composed in `providers/coingecko.ts` as `gridAlignedTail(closedPoints(raw, DAY_MS), DAY_MS)` for `dailyCloseSeries` only. New migration `20261008120000_market_bars_quarantine_off_grid.sql` adds `market_bars.is_off_grid` (default `false`, never deleted-and-reinserted data) and backfilled it `true` for every pre-fix off-grid `1d` row — **28-30 rows per asset found**, more than the ~24 directly observed for the one visible day, confirming the defect was intermittent across multiple historical days, not a single-day fluke. `replay/market-bars-reader.ts`'s `assembleAsOfCycle` now excludes `is_off_grid` rows from `dailyCloseSeries` before the existing visibility logic runs.
    - **A second-order bug found and fixed during live verification, not assumed away**: `upsertMarketBars`'s own "what's the stored max `open_time`" query (`db/market-bars.ts`) didn't exclude `is_off_grid` rows — so a pre-fix off-grid row's bogus future-dated timestamp (e.g. `...T06:43:20` written for "today") permanently poisoned the dedup comparison `filterNewBars` relies on: every GENUINE future daily close is midnight-aligned (`...T00:00:00`), which is always numerically *earlier in the day* than an intraday-stamped bogus entry for the same calendar day, so `filterNewBars`'s `openTime > storedMaxOpenTime` check would have silently rejected every real `1d` write forever. Fixed by adding `.eq('is_off_grid', false)` to that query. Caught by directly checking a live post-deploy cycle's own write (correctly wrote **zero** `1d` rows for a day that hasn't truly closed yet — exactly right — rather than re-writing an off-grid row), not by inspection alone.
    - Live-verified end to end: a fresh decision row's `regime.dailyClose` reads `85540.09` (2026-10-07's real closing value) instead of the live spot price (~$82,6xx at call time); a post-fix cycle wrote zero new `1d` rows (correctly recognizing Oct-7's clean value as not yet superseded); the quarantine query (`is_off_grid=false`) correctly resolves the stored max to the clean `2026-10-07T00:00:00+00` row instead of a bogus intraday one. 854/854 tests (7 new: `gridAlignedTail` unit tests + 3 `assembleAsOfCycle` quarantine tests), typecheck/lint/build clean throughout, including a corrected fixture (`coingecko.test.ts`'s `DAILY_CHART_FIXTURE` previously used non-midnight-aligned timestamps — `08:00 UTC`, not `00:00 UTC` — which was never actually representative of the real API and `gridAlignedTail` correctly flagged once added).
  - **P3 — event-keyed shadow candidates + random-entry baseline, DONE, DEPLOYED, deferred piece flagged.** New table `shadow_candidates` (migration `20261008140000_shadow_candidates.sql`), keyed on the market event itself — `unique(asset, arm_id, trigger_bar_ts, detector_version)` — deliberately NOT `(run_id, asset, arm_id)`, which would re-log one edge up to 8x (the 4-bar window scanned every 15 min) and once per portfolio on top (5 live portfolios now: champion + 4 E4 accounts). `strategy/intraday-ls/detect-all-opportunities.ts`'s `detectAllOpportunities` runs all six arms UNGATED by bias/config/shortEnabled — reusing the identical pure `detectBreakout`/`detectPullback`/`detectFadeOpportunity` functions `detect-candidate.ts` itself calls — then classifies each hit's `shadow_cause` (`taken`/`bias_blocked`/`arm_disabled`/`direction_disabled`, plus a 5th value the plan's own prose didn't enumerate: `lower_priority`, for a bias-eligible/arm-enabled hit that simply lost to a higher-priority arm the SAME cycle — e.g. breakout_long taken, pullback_long also fired). **Deliberately a separate module, never a rewrite of `detect-candidate.ts`** — the plan's own literal wording was "replace the call, apply bias as a post-filter," but keeping the real candidate's code path with zero new callers is a strictly stronger behavior-neutrality guarantee: a bug in the new shadow-detection code cannot regress what actually trades, because it is never on that call path. `strategy/intraday-ls/shadow-baseline.ts` adds one long + one short random-entry baseline row per asset per cycle (plan correction E6 — "breakout_short won 38%" means nothing without a control), keyed on the most-recent 30m bar's own timestamp so repeated cycles observing the same bar dedup via the table's own unique index. `bias.ts` gained one ADDITIVE export, `evaluateH4Trend` (the raw 4h EMA20-vs-EMA50 leg in isolation, `'Up'|'Down'|null`) — `evaluateBias` itself is byte-unchanged — so `regime_daily`/`regime_4h` persist as separate sliceable columns, never only the fused bias. Forward outcomes (`return`/`mfe_r`/`mae_r` at 1h/4h/8h) are nullable and **deliberately deferred** — a separate labeler job (not yet built) must read the 30m true-OHLC series once 8h has elapsed; **also deliberately NOT stored as `[pessimistic, optimistic]` intervals** — invariant I11 is about stop-vs-target ORDER ambiguity within one realized exit, which doesn't apply to a pure, order-independent forward price-path statistic, so a single point value is both correct and simpler (a documented reading of I11's scope, not an oversight). Non-fatal writes (`db/shadow-candidates.ts`'s `insertShadowCandidates`, upsert with `ignoreDuplicates: true`), wired into `index.ts`'s existing intraday_ls Pass-1 block, entirely downstream of `detectCandidate`'s own result and feeding nothing back into it. 891/891 tests (22 new), typecheck/lint/build clean, migration validated offline (`pglast`), pushed, `agent-cycle` deployed. **Live-verified** on the 07:15 UTC tick (2026-10-08) across all 5 live portfolios (champion + 4 E4 accounts): `shadow_candidates` filled 11 rows across BTC/ETH/SUI/AVAX (2 baseline each + a `bias_blocked` pullback_short/breakout hit where one fired) — critically, event-keyed dedup held: 5 portfolios observing the SAME tick produced ONE row per market event, not 5x inflation. Geometry is sane (e.g. AVAX stop 2.156%/target 4.312%, ATR-derived above the 1.2% floor; BTC/ETH at the 1.2%/2.4% floor). The real `agent_decisions` rows from the identical tick are byte-identical in shape to pre-P3 (`no_candidate_reason: 'no_arm_triggered'`, `regime_state`/`eligible_arms` populated, `arm_id`/`bias` null) — direct proof the shadow-write block never touches the real candidate path.

Full design: plan section `RESEARCH-1`, `/Users/nishchal/.claude/plans/pricing-and-model-selection-ethereal-fox.md`.
    - **R0 — scope amendment, DONE.** New `context/project-overview.md` subsection "Historical Market-Data Scope (2026-10-08)" — public, read-only, unauthenticated exchange kline/funding endpoints only (Binance), a SEPARATE table from `market_bars`, no credentials/trading endpoints ever. `CLAUDE.md`/`ai-workflow-rules.md:17` point back to it, same one-canonical-copy discipline the 2026-10-03 backtest scope change established.
    - **R1 — Binance provider + historical ingestion, DONE, LIVE-VERIFIED.** `providers/binance.ts` (spot klines + USDS-M futures funding-rate history, both genuinely public/unauthenticated — verified directly against Binance's own API docs, not assumed). Two new tables (`20261008150000_historical_bars.sql`): `historical_bars` (30m/1h/4h/1d, TRUE per-interval volume — fixing CoinGecko's rolling-24h defect) and `historical_funding_rates`. Ingestion logic (`research/ingest-core.ts`) is resumable and request-budget-bounded, exposed two ways: a local script (`research/ingest-historical-bars.ts`) and a deployed Edge Function (`functions/ingest-historical-bars/index.ts`) — the function exists specifically because this session had no local `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` and, per this project's own standing secret-handling discipline, does not fetch one via `supabase projects api-keys`; invoked instead with the already-public anon key, the established safe pattern from this project's own `market-refresh` verification.
      - **Found and fixed live, not assumed**: (a) the Bash tool's own 2-minute default timeout was silently killing the ingestion mid-run — resolved by using the deployed function's own request-budget parameter to keep each invocation short, not by fighting the timeout; (b) real Binance funding-rate history includes `markPrice: 0` placeholder rows (pre-tracking era) that the original `mark_price > 0` CHECK constraint rejected outright — fixed with a follow-up migration (`20261008160000`, never editing the applied one) relaxing it to `>= 0`, the data recorded as-is rather than discarded.
      - **Live row counts, verified sane rather than assumed**: BTC/ETH ~3,339 daily bars (~9.1 years, since 2017) / ~160,013 30m bars; SUI 1,254 daily (~3.4 years, matching its real, later Binance listing); AVAX 2,207 daily (~6 years) — exactly the "never fabricated, SUI/AVAX visibly shorter" sanity check the plan's own gate required.
    - **R2 — backtest engine core, IN PROGRESS (detection layer done and live-smoke-tested; portfolio/broker simulation loop not yet built).** `research/historical-detect.ts` is the historical equivalent of `strategy/intraday-ls/detect-candidate.ts`'s `detectCandidate` — reuses `evaluateBias`, `detectIntradayLsOpportunity`, `computeIntradayLsProtection`, `passesIntradayLsCostGate`, `shouldEmitOpportunity`, `isOpportunityStillValid`, `computeStopLossTakeProfitPrices` **unmodified**, exactly the same sequence/branch order as the live composition. Deliberately a SEPARATE composition, not a call into `detectCandidate` itself: that function's own `intradayFeaturesFor(intraday)` call requires a true 5-minute spot+volume series this engine never fetches (a deliberate R1 scope decision — none of V4's deterministic layers read anything finer than 30m/1h/4h/1d, so 1-minute ingestion would have bought zero fidelity for real cost). The two breakout-confirmation features that path actually needs (`ret60mPct`, `volumeTrendRatio`) are recomputed directly from the 30m series using the identical time windows `aggressive/features.ts` defines (60 min back; a 30-min-over-2h volume ratio) — fed Binance's TRUE volume rather than CoinGecko's meaningless rolling-24h figure. `research/historical-bars-reader.ts` assembles a point-in-time snapshot from raw rows (deliberately simpler than the live replay harness's own `assembleAsOfCycle` — no `ingested_at` confirming-write logic needed, since a historical bar is unambiguously known by its own `close_time` alone, with no live-cycle timing uncertainty to reconstruct). `research/db/fetch-historical-bars.ts` is the one paginated impure read. 22 new tests across both modules (one real bug caught by its own test: `assembleSnapshotAsOf` wasn't filtering by `asset` at all before the fix). 906/906 suite green, typecheck/lint/build clean.
      - **Live-smoke-tested against real BTC data** (late Jan 2024, a historically real choppy/declining stretch after the spot-ETF launch): stepped through 145 real 30m-bar closes and got a sane, varied result — `SHORT` and `NEUTRAL` regimes both appeared (the live system has NEVER once produced a `SHORT` bias in its entire history — a first, and a believable one given the real period chosen), `breakout_short`/`pullback_short`/`fade_short`/`breakout_long` all fired at least once, the opportunity-consumption lifecycle correctly deduped 4 repeat observations, and the narrow smoke window's own `data_insufficient` count (14, before enough 30m bars accumulated within that deliberately short local test slice) was exactly the expected, non-bug outcome.
    - **R2 — backtest engine loop, DONE, LIVE-SMOKE-TESTED.** `research/backtest-engine.ts`'s `runBacktest` is the portfolio/broker simulation — reuses `evaluateRiskGate`, `openPosition`/`closePosition`/`computeNav` (`broker/accounting.ts`), and the position-monitor's own `findFirstTrigger`/`resolveFillPrice`/`findHardMaxHoldExit`/`findSoftTimeStopExit` **unmodified**. Cross-asset state (shared cash/NAV, portfolio-risk ceiling, same-direction notional, stop-out re-entry block) is tracked across a single merged tick clock — the asset with the most 30m bars is the master clock (Binance's grid is globally aligned, verified during R1, so every other asset's bars are a true subset). Per-asset series are fed to detection as a BOUNDED trailing window (120 daily / 180 4h / 60 hourly / 60 30m) — both for O(n) performance over a multi-year run and because this is what live code itself actually fetches each cycle, never the full all-time history. **Five stated, documented scope simplifications** (all in the module's own header comment): intrabar stop/target ambiguity always resolves PESSIMISTICALLY (stop-first), never a `[pessimistic,optimistic]` interval, so a backtest can never overstate an edge; the giveback ratchet is not simulated; funding is charged only at open/close (not continuously); cross-asset tie-breaking uses a fixed caller order, not the live rotation; detection reuses the exact same bounded-window convention live code uses. 7 new tests (multi-asset master-clock selection, stop/take-profit/time-stop closes, a fully-flat zero-trade run, `shortEnabled=false` suppression). 913/913 suite green, typecheck/lint/build clean.
      - **Live-smoke-tested against a real month of BTC data (January 2024)**: 1,489 30m ticks, **52 trades** — and for the first time in this project's entire history, **short trades actually EXECUTED** (`breakout_short`/`pullback_short`/`fade_short` all fired and closed, not just shadow-logged) — because `V4_COMPAT_CONFIG.shortEnabled = true` and this engine, unlike the live system, has actually lived through a real SHORT-biased regime (BTC's well-known mid-January 2024 post-ETF-launch decline from ~$46-48k to ~$39-40k). Result for the month: -$143.40 gross realized P&L across 52 trades, final NAV $9,736.07 (-$263.93 net of fees/slippage) — a single month, not evidence of anything either way, but a concrete, internally-consistent proof that detection, sizing, and both exit paths all work correctly end to end against real history.
    - **R2 — daily-trend + inverse-vol-targeting baseline, DONE, LIVE-SMOKE-TESTED.** `research/baseline-daily-trend.ts`'s `runDailyTrendBacktest` is the literature-motivated comparison point the plan's own first-questions list calls for ("the daily leg should be the alpha, not a filter"). Deliberately **zero new strategy code**: reuses `evaluateTrendRegime` and `buildCandidateProposal` — the exact, currently-live 'balanced' profile's own state machine (OPEN_LONG while FLAT+UP, CLOSE while LONG+DOWN) — completely unmodified, and "size ∝ 1/ATR%" needs no new formula either, since `deriveRiskBasedNotional` (already inside `evaluateRiskGate`) already sizes inversely to stop distance, which `stopLossPctFor` already sets proportional to ATR%. A separate, simpler loop from the V4 engine (daily cadence, state-based, no six arms/opportunity-lifecycle/time-stops) that emits the SAME `ClosedBacktestTrade`/`OpenBacktestPosition` shapes (widened with one new literal, `armId: 'daily_trend'`) so both strategies' results are directly comparable downstream. 5 new tests, including a real, verified SL/TP-vs-regime-flip precedence proof (a single day sharp enough to flip the regime is, by construction, also below a tight stop — confirmed stop_loss correctly wins) and a clean isolation of the pure regime-flip path using a wider, realistically-derived stop. 918/918 suite green, typecheck/lint/build clean.
      - **Live-smoke-tested against real BTC data (Sept 2023 – Jan 2024, ~4.5 months)**: 7 trades, **+$576.83 net** (+5.8% on $10k) — two large winners (+5.84R, +5.90R, both take-profit exits) during BTC's real Q4 2023 rally (~$27k → ~$40k+) outweighed several small losses. Directly contrasts with the V4 engine's own January-2024-only smoke test (-$264 net over a narrower, choppier sub-window) — a first, concrete (not yet statistically meaningful) data point in favor of the plan's own literature-driven hypothesis that the daily trend carries more real signal than V4's 30-minute intraday arms. Neither result is remotely close to resolving anything on its own — both are single-window smoke tests, not the R4 run.
    - **R3 — statistics module, DONE, LIVE-DEMONSTRATED.** `research/stats.ts` is pure, takes no engine-specific types (a structural `StatsTrade`/`BacktestStatsInput` shape both engines' outputs satisfy), and covers everything `trading-strategy-v1.md` §22/§23 require: the full panel (trade count, win rate, avg win/loss R, expectancy, profit factor, fees/slippage/funding, exits-by-type, CAGR, max drawdown + its longest duration, Calmar, Sharpe, Sortino, downside deviation, time-in-market, take-profit touch rate, splits by direction and by asset); **Deflated Sharpe Ratio** (Bailey & López de Prado 2014, implemented from the paper's own formula — a hand-written inverse-normal-CDF via Acklam's algorithm and an erf-based normal CDF, no external stats library); and a general-purpose **purged, embargoed CPCV** splitter (`buildCpcvSplits`, de Prado's Combinatorial Purged Cross-Validation) operating on any `[start,end]` interval set. The **"risk rejections by reason" row is real, not a gap**: both backtest engines now call a new, shared `classifyRejectionReason` (also in `stats.ts`) on every genuine gate rejection and return a `rejectionsByReason` histogram alongside their trades/NAV series — a small, honest addition made specifically so this panel row isn't fabricated or silently dropped. Every statistic that needs it carries its own n/MDE via `withPowerCheck`, printing `NOT ACTIONABLE` mechanically (`formatPowerChecked`) rather than relying on a future report author to remember invariant I5 by hand (the same check this session's own P4 report applied manually). 23 new tests (including comparative DSR properties — more trials raises the null bar and lowers DSR for an identical observed Sharpe; a higher Sharpe never lowers it — and real purge/embargo proofs on hand-built overlapping/adjacent intervals). 941/941 suite green, typecheck/lint/build clean.
      - **Live-demonstrated against both real backtests already run this session** (V4 on BTC Jan 2024; the daily-trend baseline on BTC Sep 2023–Jan 2024): the module correctly flagged **both** strategies' expectancy as **NOT ACTIONABLE** at their current n (V4: -0.292R at n=52, MDE=0.381; baseline: +1.250R at n=7, MDE=2.974) — exactly the discipline this plan exists to enforce mechanically, even when a number looks dramatic (V4's -27% CAGR / -7.29 Sharpe over one losing month) or encouraging (the baseline's 4.85 profit factor over 7 trades). The illustrative DSR calls are explicitly logged as such (`numTrials=1`, a placeholder) — not a claim about either strategy's real validated performance, which only R4's actual pre-registered run can produce.
    - **R4 — pre-registration + full historical run, DONE.** Pre-registration (`context/diagnostics/p5-pre-registration-2026-10-08.md`, committed before any result was viewed): 13 variants (control, 6 isolated arms, 2 random-entry baselines, daily-only bias, 2 geometry alternatives, the daily-trend baseline), a sealed holdout (train < 2026-07-01, evaluated exactly once), CPCV params (10 groups/2 test groups, 2d embargo for V4-style / 14d for the baseline), and the binding decision rule (actionable AND DSR>0.95 AND CPCV-median-sign-match, else "not yet resolved"). Executed by `research/run-backtest.ts` against the full real BTC+ETH `historical_bars` dataset (9.1 years, 2017-08-17 → 2026-10-08, ~160k 30m bars/asset) — results in `context/diagnostics/p5-historical-backtest-results-2026-10-08.md`.
      - **Two real O(n²)-class performance bugs were found and fixed in `stats.ts` during the first full-grid execution attempt** (a background run stalled at 99.7% CPU for 5+ minutes against a single variant, far past the ~3s/variant baseline measured moments earlier): `computePerformancePanel`'s `timeInMarketPct` was a naive O(navTicks × trades) nested check, rewritten as a linear merge-sweep over sorted non-overlapping trade windows; `buildCpcvSplits`' purge/embargo check was O(testSize) per train interval plus a `Math.min(...array)`/`Math.max(...array)` spread risking a stack overflow on a high-turnover variant's large interval array, rewritten to compare against each test group's own `[start,end]` boundary (O(testGroupsPerSplit) per interval, computed via plain loops) — both faster AND the methodologically correct, conservative choice per de Prado's own CPCV method. 3 new regression tests (a 60,000-interval stress test completing in 58ms; a conservative-purge proof; a multi-window time-in-market sweep with gaps). 954/954 suite green, typecheck/lint/build clean.
      - **The stale pre-fix background run (task `bfwdkasrq`) was found to have silently crashed, not hung** — it completed all 12 high-frequency BTC variants (~8.5 min, consistent with the un-fixed O(n²) cost) but never reached the 13th variant or ETH at all; its wrapper reported `exited with code 0` only because the real exit code was masked by a `| tail -150` pipe. The fixed code, re-run fresh, completed both assets' full 13-variant grids cleanly in well under a minute, confirming the fix.
      - **Result: every one of the 26 (variant × asset) cells is "not yet resolved"** by the formal decision rule — no cell clears DSR > 0.95 (all arm/geometry/random/daily-only variants have strongly negative train Sharpe, deflating to 0.000 under N=13 honest trial-counting; the sole variant with a positive point estimate, the daily-trend baseline, fails on actionability instead — MDE 0.53-0.58R dwarfs its 0.01-0.31R point estimate). But the data underneath is far from silent: **(1)** all 11 arm/geometry/random/daily-only-bias variants show a robust, CPCV-confirmed NEGATIVE expectancy (-0.13R to -0.29R/trade, n=1,352-3,136, MDE 0.04-0.09R — an order of magnitude below the effect, `actionable=true`), directly extending STRAT-1's own F6 finding from 21 live trades to ~100x the sample size; **(2)** the six-arm detector logic is statistically indistinguishable from random entry (BTC v4-compat -0.256R vs. random-long -0.266R/random-short -0.246R; ETH -0.225R vs. -0.314R/-0.211R) — the negative result is attributable to cost drag and geometry, not bad signal selection; **(3)** geometry-alt-1 (3.0x ATR stop, 2.0% floor) roughly halves every other V4-style variant's loss rate (BTC -0.128R, ETH -0.126R vs. ~-0.22 to -0.26R control) — still negative, but the least-bad variant by a wide margin; **(4)** **a critical, verified-not-assumed caveat**: 10 of 13 variants show zero holdout trades on both assets — not an engine bug, but the live risk gate's own unmodified 50%-NAV drawdown breaker correctly halting all further opens. Directly verified: BTC v4-compat's last trade closed 2020-02-26 at NAV $5,078.81 (50.8% of starting capital), after which 10,856 further opens were rejected (`drawdown_breaker`) for the remaining ~6.6 years — meaning **a live account following the control variant's real historical edge would have tripped its own safety breaker within ~2.5 years**, and the sealed holdout is only actually evaluated for the three low-frequency variants (fade_long, fade_short, daily-trend baseline); **(5)** the daily-trend baseline remains the most promising, still-unresolved direction (BTC +0.314R point estimate, CPCV OOS median +0.114 but IQR straddling zero; ETH ~neutral) — underpowered at only 159-182 trades over 9+ years, needing a cross-sectional (more-assets) rather than a longer-history design to resolve.
      - No code change was made as a result of this run — per the pre-registration's own binding rule, a backtest finding is advisory input to a separately human-reviewed version bump, never a silent config change.

Replayed all 20 champion `intraday_ls` positions (19 closed + 1 open) against the real subsequent 30m true-OHLC price path, for the 14 closed via Jev's discretionary `agent_close` specifically (the other 5 closed ones — 4 `time_stop`, 1 `take_profit` — ARE already their own deterministic-exit outcome, no counterfactual needed). Every position resolved definitively — zero ambiguous same-bar stop+target intervals, zero undetermined/data-cutoff cases. **Result: F3's provisional "Jev's CLOSE helps" read does NOT survive the full-path replay and is NOT ACTIONABLE either way at this n** — paired diff (actual − counterfactual) = -0.162R/trade, n=14, 90% CI [-0.548,+0.225]R (spans zero), MDE at this n is 0.542R (3x the point estimate, per invariant I5). The point estimate itself is not robust: one single AVAX trade (closed 10-06 at -1.059R; the real subsequent path shows it would have hit take-profit at +1.763R, a 2.82R swing) drives the entire aggregate signal — excluding it, the remaining 13 trades are a wash (+0.043R mean), matching the full-sample median (-0.031R) far better than the outlier-skewed mean does. Jev's CLOSE was not changed or removed by this — a diagnostic read only, zero code touched.

    - **Order-of-Work Step 4 continued — running the universe builder against real data — DONE, 2026-10-09, after four failed designs and one real data-quality bug found by its own verification step.** The naive "fetch every bar into the Edge Function, rank in TypeScript" approach failed three times in a row, each failure diagnosing the next bottleneck: (1) sequential OFFSET pagination (804 pages) hit the platform's 150s idle-request timeout; (2) concurrent OFFSET pagination hit Postgres error 57014 — OFFSET is O(n), a half-million-row offset times out regardless of concurrency; (3) per-asset pagination fixed the query cost but then hit `WORKER_RESOURCE_LIMIT` holding ~803K raw bars in memory while recomputing eligibility/ranking from scratch for every one of 106 formation months. **Fixed by moving the computation into Postgres itself** (migrations `20261009110000`/`120000`/`130000`): a SQL function (`dt1_build_and_write_universe_membership`) reproduces the exact, already-tested `eligibility.ts`/`build-universe.ts` contract (confirmed by reading those modules' actual behavior — `evaluateEligibility` only ever uses the first/last element of the sorted bar list, nothing about bars in between) set-based, with a per-function `statement_timeout` override (the PostgREST-facing role has a much shorter default than the `db query --linked` path, confirmed via the identical 57014 error recurring there too), writing directly to `research_universe_membership` and returning only a row count — avoiding both the earlier memory problem AND a second PostgREST `max_rows`-truncation bug found along the way (an RPC call returning rows is capped at 1000 exactly like a plain `select`). Final run: 1,902 membership rows across 103 formation months (2018-03 through 2026-09) in ~37-40s, correctly showing the expected thin-early-years ramp (N=2 at the first eligible month, full N=20 cap from mid-2019 — BTC/ETH's own 180-day eligibility window lands almost exactly on the live first-eligible-month found).
    - **Real data-quality finding, caught by a mechanical post-build verification sweep, not a spot-check**: querying every asset that ever entered the built universe for price coefficient-of-variation (genuine crypto assets never sit near a stablecoin's ~0.05% figure) found **four symbols the name-based classifier had missed** — `USD1` (World Liberty Financial USD1), `RLUSD` (Ripple USD), and `U` (all three stablecoins with no textual "USD"-like hint in their tickers — exactly the limitation the classifier's own comment warns about), plus `EUR` (a genuine Binance-listed fiat currency pair, not a cryptocurrency at all — added as a new, explicit `fiat_currency` asset class, migration `20261009140000`). The same migration also made the ranking function itself robust against this CLASS of problem recurring: it now explicitly joins `research_contracts` and filters to `excluded=false` under the given `mapping_version`, rather than trusting that `historical_bars` only ever contains non-excluded assets (the assumption this incident falsified) — a future classifier correction can now be applied by re-running the build under a new `mapping_version`, with no need to touch `historical_bars` at all. Re-verified via the same coefficient-of-variation sweep widened to 50%: a clean, enormous gap remains between true stablecoins (~0.05%) and the lowest-volatility genuine crypto asset in the universe (`PAXG`, gold-backed, ~30-39%, real gold-price movement — correctly NOT excluded, since it is a legitimately volatile commodity-tracking asset, not a $1 peg). **Canonical, verified-clean version: `mapping_version='dt1-v3'` / `universe_version='dt1-v3'`** (`dt1-v1`/`dt1-v2` preserved in the DB as superseded, documented artifacts of the correction process, never deleted — same "legacy preserved for audit, corrected canonical" discipline as the DSR correction). 32 contracts-module tests, full suite 1005/1005, typecheck/lint clean.

Data-only (read-only SQL against `historical_bars`/`research_universe_membership`, zero strategy code touched). **T**: 103 months / 3,136 calendar days (2018-03 through 2026-09), both figures agreeing exactly since BTC's own daily bar count over the identical window is also 3,136. **N**: ramps 2→20 over the first 17 months (2018-03 to 2019-07), then holds at EXACTLY 20 for all remaining 86 months with zero dips — across the 2020 crash, the 2021 bull run, and the full 2022 bear market; 159 distinct underlying assets have held an external-sleeve slot at some point even though N itself stays flat. **ρ̄** (daily log-return pairwise Pearson correlation, external sleeves only — BTC/ETH excluded per A6): full 9-year population (159 assets, 12,165 pairs) = **0.459**; current-snapshot-only (18 assets) = **0.498**; stable at 0.476–0.493 across overlap-floor sensitivity checks (365/730/1,095 days) — clusters tightly in **[0.46, 0.50]** regardless of methodology. **Flagged finding, not resolved unilaterally**: every real-data ρ̄ estimate sits AT OR BELOW the Stage-A-frozen power-simulation grid's lowest point (`{0.5, 0.65, 0.8}`) — the full-population figure (0.459) is actually below 0.5 entirely. Reopening the frozen grid now, having seen this number, would be exactly the kind of post-hoc threshold change the plan's own discipline forbids, so this is reported as a decision point for the user (leave the grid as-is, which makes the simulation's power estimates conservative rather than optimistic since higher ρ̄ lowers effective n; or widen it with a dated amendment) rather than something I resolved myself.
    - **ρ̄ grid amendment — DONE, 2026-10-09 (explicit user decision).** `0.45` added as a fourth, dated transparency point alongside the Stage-A-frozen `{0.5, 0.65, 0.8}` (never a substitution — the original three and A10's 80%/Sharpe=0.54 threshold are untouched). Recorded identically in the plan document (§6.7b, S11, the A10 ambiguity table), the Stage A pre-registration doc, and the measurements diagnostic's own resolution note, so all three stay in lockstep. Going forward the `ρ̄` grid is `{0.45, 0.5, 0.65, 0.8}`.
    - **E2's three dependence estimators — DONE, 2026-10-09.** New `research/dependence/` module (`student-t.ts`, `icc-cluster.ts`, `newey-west.ts`, `block-bootstrap.ts`, `e2-estimators.ts`), 66 new tests, all numerically validated against known-answer cases rather than merely type-checked: **Student-t** critical values match textbook two-sided-90% tables to within 0.01 across df 1–120 (own regularized-incomplete-beta + bisection implementation, no external library). **Overlap-clustered ICC** (observation = one closed trade's net R; cluster = connected components of holding-period overlap via a merge-intervals sweep, NOT entry-month — verified via a transitive-overlap case where A-C never directly overlap but both chain through B into one cluster) matches a hand-computed two-cluster ANOVA example exactly (n_eff=2.25 on the nose); `max(ρ_intra,0)` floor confirmed to hold n_eff≤N even on a deliberately-constructed negative-raw-ICC input. **Newey-West** on the corrected monthly series (each trade assigned to its CLOSING month only, zero-trade months as the well-defined pair (0,0), the automatic lag rule, delta-method SE on the HAC-estimated covariance of the two monthly series, Student-t(T-1) reference) — caught and fixed one real division-by-zero bug during testing (an all-zero-trade window produced NaN, not a clean 0, before an explicit guard was added). **Stationary block bootstrap** with Politis-White (2004) automatic mean-block-length selection (flat-top lag window, the algorithm's documented structure, not a byte-for-byte port of any specific reference implementation — stated plainly) validated qualitatively (white noise → small block length; a persistent AR(1) series → materially larger; monotonically increasing in the AR coefficient) since the algorithm has no simple hand-computable closed form. **The E2 combinator** implements the exact three-way tie-break (all three exclude zero, same direction → significant; any interval including zero → not significant, even alongside a disagreement; two non-null but opposite directions → `estimator_conflict`, never resolved by majority) and reports all three estimators unconditionally regardless of verdict, per Stage A's own requirement. Full suite 1071/1071, typecheck/lint clean.
    - **Power simulation engine — DONE, 2026-10-09; full grid run in progress.** New `research/power-sim/` module. `measure-inputs.ts` (local script) re-ran R4's own unmodified config (`R4_DAILY_TREND_CONFIG`) against the now-available real `historical_bars` for BTC/ETH to recover the actual 352-trade population (166 BTC + 186 ETH — close to, not identical to, the earlier-recalled "341," consistent with a fresh full-history reproduction) R4's own aggregate stats never persisted per-trade: measured mean R=0.170, sd=2.921, skewness=1.152 (real right-skew, consistent with the 6:1 take-profit), excess kurtosis=0.043, mean holding ≈9.4 days, plus per-sleeve median daily log-return volatility=6.41% measured across 90 external-sleeve assets. `dgp.ts` is the data-generating process: E1's daily portfolio-return series via an equicorrelation factor model (verified empirically — realized pairwise correlation lands within 0.03 of target `ρ̄` at every tested value including the 0/1 boundaries; realized annualized Sharpe lands within 0.15 of target over a long run); E2's trade population via a resampled-shape (R4's own standardized R values, preserving its exact skew/kurtosis) calibration with a shared MONTHLY common-factor shock inducing genuine cross-trade dependence at strength `ρ̄` (not an artificially independent population) — **caught and fixed one real test-design flaw during development, not a DGP bug**: an early verification test assumed realized Sharpe would converge tightly at `ρ̄=0.3` over 200 months, but the shared monthly factor's own sampling noise only averages out over ~200 independent monthly draws, not the much larger total trade count — correctly isolated by re-testing the core calibration at `ρ̄=0` (converges tightly, as it should) and separately confirming same-month trades are genuinely more correlated than cross-month ones at `ρ̄>0` (a qualitative check, not a tight-convergence one). `replicate.ts` runs one Monte Carlo replicate through the complete pipeline: E1's stationary-bootstrap CI (reusing `block-bootstrap.ts` unmodified), E2's three estimators (reusing `e2-estimators.ts` unmodified), the robustness condition (best-contiguous-year removed, still positive), and the full secondary three-clause diagnostic at N=1 and N=14 (reusing `stats.ts`'s own already-tested `deflatedSharpeRatio`/`buildCpcvSplits` unmodified; the N=14 trial-variance input uses the real 13 R4 variants' own per-period Sharpes, pooled BTC/ETH-mean per variant, in a new `registry-sharpes.ts`, dated and documented). **Explicitly, honestly out of scope for this round**: the MaxDD veto (condition 2 of §6.8's three-condition "Supported" verdict) — stated plainly in `replicate.ts`'s own header comment rather than rushed or silently omitted; the simulation's power estimate covers condition 1 (E1 clears the required Sharpe) alone, reported as such. 19 new tests (16 DGP, 3 replicate — including a real statistical differentiation check: a true Sharpe of 2.0 clears the power bar far more often than a true Sharpe of 0, which hits it essentially never). Benchmarked at realistic scale (T=3,136 days, 500 within-simulation bootstrap resamples — explicitly reduced from the real analysis's 10,000, a stated computational simplification) at ~76ms/replicate; the full 24-cell grid at 400 replicates/cell (Monte Carlo SE on the power estimate ≈±2.5% at p=0.5) is running as a background task, ~12 minutes. Full suite 1090/1090, typecheck/lint clean.
    - **Power simulation grid run — DONE, 2026-10-09. Stage B frozen. S11 gate: FAIL, decisively, across the entire grid.** Full writeup: `context/diagnostics/dt1-power-simulation-stage-b-2026-10-09.md`; raw results: `research/power-sim/power-simulation-results.json`. 24 cells (`ρ̄∈{0.45,0.5,0.65,0.8}` × true Sharpe `∈{0,0.3,0.5,0.54,0.8,1.2}`), 400 replicates/cell. **Power at the frozen Sharpe=0.54 hurdle: 3.0–4.0% at every single ρ̄ point** (0.45→3.5%, 0.5→3.5%, 0.65→3.0%, 0.8→4.0%) — nowhere close to A10's 80% bar, and the verdict agrees at every grid point (not fragile). Power stays far below 80% even at true Sharpe=1.2 (more than double the hurdle): 35.2%–59.0% across the same ρ̄ grid. **Independently verified as a genuine statistical fact, not a simulation bug**, before being trusted: the observed ~1.10–1.12-wide 90% CI on annualized Sharpe matches the Lo (2002) asymptotic standard-error formula almost exactly (predicted 1.123) — annualizing a daily-data Sharpe multiplies its own sampling noise by √365≈19.1, and 3,136 days is not enough to overcome that at this effect size. **Secondary findings, reported for completeness, never decision-bearing**: DSR at N=14 is exactly 0.0% in every one of the 24 cells at every true Sharpe including 1.2 — independently reproducing R4's own DSR-correction finding (the N=14 benchmark is structurally unpassable) by simulation rather than by the single R4 observation alone; DSR at N=1 scales sensibly with true Sharpe (4–5%→88–98%); the robustness condition (best-year-removed, still positive) climbs from ~30% to ~99–100% with true Sharpe, confirming the DGP generates real signal even where E1's own CI stays too wide to prove it; CPCV median-sign-agreement sits flat at 91–95% regardless of scenario, not strongly discriminating; E2's conservative three-way tie-break produces `not_significant` as the overwhelming majority verdict in every cell, even at Sharpe=1.2, exactly as its design intends. **No threshold, grid, or strategy parameter was touched in response to this result** — A10's 80%/Sharpe=0.54 threshold, the ρ̄ grid (including the 0.45 transparency amendment), and R4's own config are all exactly as frozen before the simulation ran.

Then, per the user's decision: either re-scope and re-run the simulation, or proceed to P2 pre-registration as a hard gate before any full DT-1 run with the underpowered caveat stated up front. Full staged plan with every stop gate (S1–S12 plus A10, now closed): `DT-1` section of the plan file.

- **Found-and-fixed pre-existing type error (2026-10-09) — not part of any currently active plan unit.** Running `deno check` across every non-test file in `supabase/functions/` in one invocation (`find supabase/functions -name "*.ts" ! -name "*.test.ts" | xargs deno check`) — something this project had apparently never done in one shot before — surfaced `TS2322` at `agent-cycle/index.ts:969` (`biasResolved: regimeStateForRow` not assignable to `ShadowCandidateRow.biasResolved: Bias`). Root cause: `rowFor` (the shadow-candidate row builder defined inside the `if (regimeStateForRow && ...)` block, STRAT-1 P3) is a closure over the outer `let regimeStateForRow` — TypeScript does not carry control-flow narrowing into a nested function body for a mutable outer binding, so inside `rowFor` the type widens back to `PassOneResult['regimeState']`'s full `Bias | null | undefined`, even though the enclosing `if` makes null/undefined genuinely unreachable at every call site (both calls happen synchronously, inside the same `if` block, before `regimeStateForRow` could be reassigned). Fixed by snapshotting the narrowed value into a `const resolvedBias: Bias = regimeStateForRow` immediately before `rowFor`'s definition, and having `rowFor` read `resolvedBias` instead — no logic change, purely lets the compiler see what was already true at runtime. Confirmed via `git diff --stat` that neither `index.ts` nor `db/shadow-candidates.ts` had uncommitted changes beforehand — this was committed, latent since STRAT-1 P3 (2026-10-08), caught only because `npm run typecheck` (`tsc -b`) never covered the Deno Edge Function tree. Verified clean: `deno check` across the full directory, `deno test --allow-read=supabase/functions/position-monitor supabase/ src/` (1003/1003), `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`.
